**Likes and ratings are removed** (decided 2026-09-29: "dont keep local likes or a rating"; "you can show downloads"). No page draws a Like control or a rating; the local likes store, its sends and its retries, the `[Thunderstore] SessionToken` setting and the `like` / `rating` readback keys are gone; Core deletes `BepInEx/progenitor-cache/likes.json` once at load if it exists (that one file only, one log line). Thunderstore's published **download counts stay** (the Install tab's Downloads column, which sorts by them). `rating_score` is still parsed into `CatalogEntry.RatingScore` and used nowhere. A future opt-in could come back only with a qualified Thunderstore account connection. Source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified. [Requirement](mod-package-capabilities.md#rating-control-acceptance). Earlier Like and rating details below describe the removed control, not current behavior.

# Mods workspace

**Planned extension:** [Mods, Install mods and Configuration tabs](mod-package-capabilities.md) retain this workspace's directory/settings and add available-mod browsing with declared hot-swap support and validated restart decisions. The player MCP shares that catalog and installation plan. The owned tabs (Mods, Install Mods, Configuration and, since KEYS1, [Keybinds](#keybinds-tab)) are in this workspace; installation from this menu downloads, checks, plans and writes to pending for the restart route (promote happens at the next start), and — for an admitted hot-load plan only — writes this process's `progenitor-reload/` directly (job HOT1).

The client Mods workspace is the player-facing directory for discovered mods, personal settings, opted-in server settings and custom pages. Authors register through `ctx.Menu`. The one buildable example is [`samples/Progenitor.Sample/SampleMenu.cs`](../samples/Progenitor.Sample/SampleMenu.cs).

There is no Escape-menu or System-page Mods button. `EscapeModsEntry` is not installed.

**Settings organization, three homes ([settings-organization.md](settings-organization.md#three-homes-2026-10-06)):** the tab bar is **Mods · Install Mods · Keybinds · Preferences · Server**. **Preferences** is how this Mods window works on this PC, a mod's own settings are on its page in **Mods**, and how the joined server is set up is in **Server**. Groups of more than four settings are drawers, each tab lists its groups on the left, and each setting keeps one stored value wherever it is shown. What is built is in [Where Progenitor's settings are drawn](#where-progenitors-settings-are-drawn).

## Workspace presentation

The in-game workspace uses five tabs grouped on the left: **Mods**, **Install Mods**, **Keybinds**, **Preferences** and **Server** (three homes, 2026-10-06; Server last, as requested; Preferences was Configuration). It borrows the native inner-tab button visuals and red selected highlight. Captions are centered; a native silver divider meets the artwork below the tabs. Pane and passive header/footer backgrounds are transparent, while interactive controls retain their own backgrounds and hover states.

### Tab look per host

Every host draws the same four tabs; the look depends on which native donor the strip can reach when it is built. The strip clones the game's own inner-tab button (the Spellbook tab: framed, dark unselected, red selected) through a ladder, and the first rung that clones wins:

| Rung | Donor | Reached from |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `FullscreenMenu.InnerTabGroup.EntryPrefab.Button` | In game, with the FullscreenMenu up (Esc → MODS, and the shortcut in a world, below). |
| 2 | A hidden copy of that clone, kept for the Core generation (`progenitor-ui-core-tab-donor-template`, inactive, `DontDestroyOnLoad`) | Any strip built without the menu, after the in-game menu has been seen once in this Core generation. It is taken the first time the FullscreenMenu is found (and on every rung-1 strip) and dropped when any of its sprites is unloaded. |
| 3 | A `FullscreenMenu_InnerTabEntry` already loaded in memory, found by `Resources.FindObjectsOfTypeAll(Il2CppType)` | The title screen on a cold client. Three scans per Core generation at most; only ever cloned. |
| 4 | The composed flat button | Nothing above reached. This is the look every title strip had before. |

| Host | Expected look |
|---|---|
| Esc → MODS (`top-tab`) | Native (rung 1). |
| Shortcut in a world | Native (rung 1): the shortcut opens through the FullscreenMenu. Flat only when it falls back to the standalone window and rungs 2–3 miss. |
| Title screen (`main-menu`, or the shortcut at the title) | Native from rung 2 after a game session this generation, else rung 3 when the prefab is resident, else flat. |

Each clone has the native click callbacks removed and the school icon and new-tag graphics switched off; the selected tab sets `ForceHighlight`. The template is destroyed and the counters reset on every Core unload. Log: `Mods workspace: tab source=<fullscreen-inner|cached-template|resident-inner-tab|flat>`, once per change. Readback: `mods_ui tabs` `detail.stripTabSource` (same names, `unresolved` before the first strip). **Verified live** 2026-09-29 on the Steam client, Core 2026.9.73.1: a cold title drew framed native tabs with `stripTabSource=resident-inner-tab`; the title after leaving a world, `cached-template`; F10 in a world, `fullscreen-inner`. The template's fonts and materials check, and the one-scan-per-strip budget, were added after that run and are game-free tested (`WorkspaceTabLadderTests`) only. The candidate's owned framed reproduction (commit `1f63928c`) was not ported, so a total miss is still flat.

**Tab captions are checked on every show (TABCAPS1, source-checked and game-free tested; not live-verified).**

**Live (2026-10-04, Steam client in game on the isolated test server, branch fix/blank-tab-labels f745621a, client generations 33 and 34):** twice, the game menu was closed (`mods_ui menu {op:"close"}`), Core was hot-swapped (log `strip prebuilt … source=resident-inner-tab`, the path that produced the blank strip), and F10 pressed through `keybind_press`; `mods_ui tabs` read `stripCaptions.residentDonor: menu-prefab`, all four tabs `ok: true` with intended equal to rendered text, font alive, alpha 1, `repairs: 0`; the `strip tab targets` line read `highlighted=Tab_Active` (not `OrnamentedTab_Passives_Active`); the screenshot showed all four captions. **Not live-verified:** the repair path itself (no fault occurred), Esc → MODS and title-screen opens with the new readback.

Once (2026-10-03, Core generation 32) the strip's four tabs were seen with no text in them. The incident was not reproduced. The client log narrows it down:
- After a Core swap in a world with the game menu down, the strip was prebuilt from rung 3 (`tab source=resident-inner-tab`), and that is the strip the next open reused.
- That donor's highlighted sprite was `OrnamentedTab_Passives_Active`, another menu's tab. Every rung-1 strip clones the Spellbook tab, whose highlighted sprite is `Tab_Active` (log `strip tab targets …`). The same mismatch shows at an earlier resident build.
- A later rebuild from rung 1 drew normally.

How that donor blanked the captions is not pinned. The candidates are:
1. The caption was written into the first TMP in the clone, including inactive ones, while that tab shows a different label.
2. A color-tint target on that tab fades its text.
3. That prefab's font or material.

Changes:
- **Label choice:** a cloned button's caption now goes into the first enabled TMP whose objects are active (`PrimaryLabel`).
- **Rung 3 donor:** rung 3 first looks for the FullscreenMenu's own inner-tab prefab, the twin of the rung-1 donor, before taking any inner-tab entry. `mods_ui tabs.stripCaptions.residentDonor` reads `menu-prefab` or `any-entry`.
- **Self-heal:** each time the strip is shown, and again on the next frame, every tab's caption is checked against its intended words. A fault is any of these: no label, inactive, disabled, other text, missing font or glyphs, or alpha under 0.05.
- **Repair:** a fault is repaired (activate, enable, rewrite the words, the kit font, full alpha) and logged as a warning: `Ui: tab caption repaired <tab> (was '<old>') faults=<…> font=<…> alpha=<…> source=<rung>`.
- **Target report:** the `strip tab targets` log line now also gives each tint target's normal alpha and how many text graphics it drives.

Readback: `mods_ui tabs` → `stripCaptions {strip, visible, tabs[{tab, intended, text, labelFound, labels, active, enabled, font, fontAlive, glyphs, alpha, faults, ok}], repairs, lastRepair, residentDonor}`. This is read without repairing, from the shown strip or else the last one.

The custom artwork uses an owned copy of a native flowmap smoke material (shader `Stunlock/UI/UIFlowmap`) and a `Divider_Big_04` trim, from the first rung that answers:

| Rung | Smoke material | Trim |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The live FullscreenMenu's `JournalBackgroundSmoke` | The live menu's `Divider_Big_04` Image (disabled while open, restored on close) |
| 2 | A copy taken the first time the Esc menu appears this Core generation (owned `new Material`, destroyed on Core unload; dropped when a texture it samples is unloaded) | The sprite remembered from that menu |
| 3 | A `JournalBackgroundSmoke` already loaded in memory (non-generic `Resources.FindObjectsOfTypeAll(Il2CppType)`; one scan per pane build, shared with the trim; three per generation) | A resident `Divider_Big_04` sprite (same scan) |
| 4 | Title: the Play backdrop clone's own material (`MenuSmoke_Material`), with its overlay tiling fitted to the pane so the smoke keeps its authored size | — |
| 5 | Plain image | No trim |

The tab strip stands on the artwork pane's top edge in every host (`MenuPanelLayout.ArtworkPaneTop`, the rect `PlaceHolder` draws from), with the trim `MenuPanelLayout.TrimLift` (4) above it over the tab bottoms, as in game; the title strip used to float 16 units above the frame line. Only rung 1 of the trim disables a native graphic. Log: `Mods workspace: artwork source=<fullscreen-smoke|cached-copy|resident-smoke|play-backdrop|plain> trim=<fullscreen-donor|cached-sprite|resident-sprite|none>`, once per change. Readback: `mods_ui tabs` `detail.artworkSource` / `detail.trimSource`, and `detail.artworkMaterials {source, pane, liveDonor, playBackdrop}` (each: material name, shader, render queue and every shader property's value; textures with scale/offset), read on demand to compare the pane's copy with its donor. **Source-checked and game-free tested (`WorkspaceArtworkLadderTests`), not live-verified.** Before this, a title pane only ever took rung 1, so it drew the plain image.

Native callbacks are removed from cloned buttons; the original divider is restored and owned graphics/materials are released on close or Core unload. If the native donor is unavailable, controls use the composed fallback rather than borrowing an unrelated button.

The bottom of the workspace shows the framework's component versions in small muted text, centered on the workspace frame: `Progenitor Core <core> · Bootstrap <bootstrap>` (for example `Progenitor Core 2026.9.58 · Bootstrap 2026.9.57`). The dev bridge is not listed: players never have it, and its version normally repeats Core's; the bridge's own status reports it. Versions follow [versioning.md](versioning.md) with build metadata removed; Bootstrap's is read from the loaded `Progenitor.Bootstrap` assembly, and a missing one reads `Bootstrap unknown`. The line is the main pane's [version footer](ui.md#version-footer) (the Mods detail, Install and Configuration panes all carry it; the sidebar and tab strip do not), drawn below the pane's last row in a band its viewport is inset above; it adds no background, tab-strip or palette change. Readback: `mods_ui status.footer` (empty while closed). **Source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified.**

Implementation and scoped live verification are recorded in the internal research notes. In-game tab handlers, native-menu return, and final 16:9/ultrawide spacing were checked on the test client. Physical hover/input, title-screen donor parity and gamepad behavior are not established by that review.

### One layout for both hosts

HOSTS1 (decided 2026-09-30: keep both hosts, make Progenitor look the same in both; the in-game look is the target). The workspace has two hosts: in a world it lives inside the game's FullscreenMenu (`top-tab`, native top bar, Back handling); at the title screen, and in a world when the shortcut falls back to the standalone window, it lives in a Progenitor-owned window. Both size and handle input through one shared layer, so a fix applies to both and a test covers both (`WorkspaceHostsParityTests`).

- **Tabs and notch after Spellbook (round 13, user review 2026-09-30).** Measured at 3840×1080 against Spellbook's own tab row (`reviews/2026-09-30-window-fit/v-Spellbook.png`): Spellbook's tabs are drawn y 88..128 (40 tall, the frame of a 176×50 rect), about 168 wide, 10 apart; ours were drawn y 96..128 (32 tall), about 212 wide, 24 apart. In the band the strip is now 48 tall (the donor frame is drawn 8 shorter, so y 88..128), and each tab is sized to its caption plus the donor's padding, at least 176 (drawn 168), 2 apart (drawn 10), left-aligned from 24 in (`WorkspaceHostLayout.TabHeight`, `TabMinWidth`, `TabGap`, `TabWidths`; `MenuPanelLayout.StripButton`); the caption stays centered on the tab rect, as the donor's is. *Inferred:* the caption padding (24 each side) and the average glyph width used to size a tab (11 units); the readback reports every tab rect. The silver notch is back where round 2 asked for it: on the window's top edge, centered in the free stretch right of the last tab, clear of every tab (round 8 had centered it on the window, under the Keybinds tab, a regression). Both hosts. Readback: `mods_ui rects.hosts.windowRects.tabRects` (each tab, with its caption) and `notchRect` (`split`, `centerX`), in screen pixels. *Game-free tested; not live-verified.*
- **The window is the vanilla rectangle (round 8, decided 2026-09-30).** Both hosts draw the Mods window exactly where every vanilla menu page draws its window: 1664×916 with its top 128 units below the canvas top, the window of Map, Social, Vampire Powers and Spellbook (screen y 127..1043 at 1080p). That rectangle is the native `ViewParent` (1664×870) plus the `ConsoleInputsGroup` strip under it, below the 56-unit band where Spellbook's own tabs sit. Our tab strip (Mods, Install Mods, Configuration, Keybinds) stands in that band, its bottom on the window's top edge, starting 24 units in from the window's left edge like Spellbook's first tab (`MenuPanelLayout.StripInBand`, `WorkspaceHostLayout.StripLeftInset`); the panes, the backdrop and the version footer take the whole 916. The silver `Divider_Big_04` trim and its notch were centered on the window's top edge, as on Map (superseded in round 13: the notch sits right of the tabs again). When the game's menu is in its collapsed layout (the band not laid out, ViewParent top 72, which is what the Mods page's own route produced), the in-game frame is moved down 56 to the vanilla place (`WorkspaceHostLayout.PinCanonical`, log `… at=(0,-46) … pinned=down56`); the native black strip then lies inside the window under the backdrop in both layouts, so nothing is switched off below the window any more (`HidesVacatedStrip` is false). `[Workspace] CanonicalInGameFrame=false` draws the native frame as read (the strip goes inside a collapsed frame, which has no band). The title uses the same model (`NativeFrameModel.Top` 128) and the same records (made canonical before they are kept; records at the old top 72 no longer pass). Readback: `mods_ui status.hosts.windowRects` and `mods_ui rects.hosts.windowRects`: `windowRect`, `tabBandRect` and, while the game's menu is up, `vanillaViewParentRect` and `vanillaWindowRect` (ViewParent grown by the footer strip) in top-origin screen pixels (`ModsWorkspaceChrome.DescribeWindowRects`), so a Mods window can be compared with a vanilla page (open the Esc menu on Map with the Mods backdrop kept from an earlier open). *Game-free tested; not live-verified.* Rounds 3–7 (below) took the collapsed layout (top 72) as canonical and put the strip inside the window; that is superseded.
- **Frame** (`WorkspaceHostLayout.TitleFrame`, path `title-host`). The owned window draws the in-game frame, from the first source that answers: the settled in-game frame recorded for this canvas size (`recorded`); one recorded on a canvas of the same height, with its top, height and width carried (`carried`); else `NativeFrameModel` (`model`): 1664×916 with its top 72 units below the canvas top (`at=(0,10)` on a 1080-unit canvas), at every width. That is the **canonical** in-game frame. Live, the native ViewParent at one canvas sits either at top 128 (`at=(0,-46)`) or at top 72 (`at=(0,10)`), and it is 128 exactly when the inactive `InnerTabGroup` entries still hold a laid-out row (50 tall at y 79): p1 3840×1080 and 1920×1080 had that row, p1 1600×1024 and r2 3840×1080 had 0×0 entries (`reviews/2026-09-30-hosts-probes/p1-game-*-menu.json`, `reviews/2026-09-30-hosts-after/r2-game-3840-menu.json`). Both variants carry the footer (`ConsoleInputsGroup` 48 under ViewParent), so the footer is not the cause, and neither is the resolution. The 56 units are the inner-tab row (50 + 6) a page with inner tabs (Spellbook) leaves reserved; the Mods page has none, so the canonical frame is the one without it (`NativeFrameModel.Canonical` removes it). Since round 4 (coordinator decision 2026-09-30) the in-game host draws the canonical frame too, so both hosts match every session: when the native frame is 1664 wide with its top 128 (±8), it is moved up 56 (`WorkspaceHostLayout.PinCanonical`, log `… at=(0,10) … pinned=+56`), and the window (panes, artwork and scrim, and the version footer at the bottom of the detail pane) ends at the canonical bottom, exactly as on the title screen. Round 4 had the artwork reach on into the vacated band (988–1044 at 1080 units), which left the version footer floating about 70 px above the art's bottom (user, 2026-09-30: "version at the bottom is out of place"). Since round 5 the only native graphic in that band, `ViewParent/ConsoleInputsGroup` (an opaque black strip, Image rgba 0,0,0,1, no sprite; probe menus p1 and r2), has its graphics switched off while a pinned workspace is shown and restored when it closes (round 6 fix: the menu's `ViewParent` field is the inner `ViewParent (1)`, so round 5 never found the strip and it still drew black at 995–1045; it is now found under the field's parent or by its path, and one line per open says where and how many graphics were held: `Mods workspace: vacated strip … graphics held=N`), so the band shows what is outside the window everywhere else, as in the canonical layout (`WorkspaceHostLayout.HidesVacatedStrip`, `ModsWorkspaceChrome.SyncVacatedStrip`). `[Workspace] CanonicalInGameFrame` (default on) turns pinning off; off draws the native frame where the game put it. Live 2026-09-30 (round 4): six System→Mods opens at 3840×1080 all drew `native-pending` then `native-container … at=(0,10) pinned=+56`. **Not live-verified:** the round 5 strip hold. A record is used only when its (canonical) top is within 24 units of 72 and its height is sane, and it is always bounded to the canvas through the model's `Fit` (8 units kept at each side). *Inferred, not probed:* 16:10 sizes other than 1600×1024, 4:3, and anything narrower than the 1664 frame. The strip lift (`WorkspaceHostLayout.TabLift`), pane split, strip, trim line and artwork pane follow from the same arithmetic as in game (`MenuPanelLayout.StripConsumesFrameTop` is true for both paths). Readback: `mods_ui status` `framePath`, `host`, `frameSource`, `tabLift`, `titleBand`, `backgroundIntensity`; log `Mods workspace: frame path=title-host … reason=title-host-<recorded|carried|model>`.
- **Record.** What is recorded is the applied in-game frame (ViewParent grown by the footer chrome) with the workspace open, made canonical (the stale inner-tab row removed): only while the workspace is shown in the FullscreenMenu, on a canvas that has its real size, and after the same frame (within one unit, the one tolerance every comparison uses) has held for 15 frames; each settled frame is committed once (`NativeFrameSettle`, `WorkspaceHostLayout.ObserveInGame`, called from the backdrop's per-frame sync, not from `Resolve`). The first read of an open, taken before the overlay canvas has its size, falls back to a 1920×1080 canvas and read `at=(0,-20)`; it is never recorded. Records are kept per canvas size (at most 8, newest first) in `BepInEx/config/progenitor-workspace-frame.txt` (`NativeFrameMemory`): a header, a `build<TAB><game build identity>` stamp (a file from another game build, or with no stamp, is ignored), then `canvasW canvasH width height x y` lines. Writes are debounced by one second (and forced on close), merge in the canvases another process wrote, and replace the file through `….tmp`.
- **Top band** (title host only). Where the game's outer tab bar sits (48 units, the full canvas width, the bar's own color: `OuterTabGroup` Image rgba 0,0,0,1, no sprite), the title draws a band with a centered **Mods** caption in the outer tabs' font (`BORUTTA GROUP - Nocturne Serif Regular DropShadow`, else its family, else the kit font; size 24, small caps). The game's entries (Spellbook, Map, …) cannot exist there; that is the one accepted difference. The in-game host has the game's bar; the standalone window in a world draws no band (it would cover the HUD).
- **Trim ornament.** The `Divider_Big_04` trim's center ornament used to sit under the Keybinds tab (the trim is centered on the frame, the tabs are left-aligned; user report 2026-09-30). It is now centered in the free stretch from the last tab's right edge to the frame's right edge, in both hosts (in game the game's divider is disabled and this copy drawn). The sprite keeps its size: the line is two masked copies, the right one centered on the ornament's new place, the left one where it always was, joined at a seam placed under a tab's center (`WorkspaceHostLayout.Trim`). Readback: `mods_ui tabs.trimNotch {split, notchX, seamX, lastTabRight, frameRight}`. *Inferred:* the ornament is under 128 units wide; the sprite's ends are not shown at the frame's right edge (the right copy is clipped there).
- **Tab hover.** The cloned native tab marks the selected tab with `ForceHighlight`, the same Highlighted state a pointer gives any tab, so a hovered tab looked exactly selected. On each unselected tab's own copy, color-tint targets' highlighted color is moved 45% of the way from normal (`WorkspaceTabHover`), so hover is lighter than selected; the selected tab keeps the donor's color. Sprite-swap targets are left as the donor has them and logged once (`Mods workspace: strip tab targets …`).
- **Artwork look** (`WorkspaceArtworkIntensity`). Two graphics make it, in both hosts, applied live each layout: the artwork image's color multiplies the art by the intensity times a warm balance (r 1, g 0.5, b 0.3; the game's menus are dark red with almost no green or blue), and the scrim over it is a flat dark-red floor (sRGB 0.066/0.0065/0.005) at alpha `1 − contrast`, so each channel reads `lerp(floor, art·k·tint, contrast)`. Settings: `[Workspace] BackgroundIntensity` (0.2–1.0, default 0.9) and `[Workspace] BackgroundContrast` (0.1–1.0, default 0.18). The PNG, the smoke material's animation, the panes and the text are not changed. **How the numbers were measured** (coordinator, 2026-09-30, Steam client, 3840×1080): mean RGB and standard deviation over a 590×500 area of the right-side background in a capture. The game's Vampire Powers menu read 23/2.2/1.5, sd 13; ours at the first default 0.55 (cool tint, scrim 0.52) 12.6/1.0/6.5, sd 5.6 ("seems darker than the other menus"); ours at 1.0 25/5.6/11.3, sd 27. Round 4 estimated the art from the 1.0 reading (43.7/6.4/15.2, sd 56) and predicted 23.1/2.0/2.1, sd 14.1 at 0.9/0.28; live at 0.9/0.28 it read 19.9/2.9/2.8, sd 23.1 (right color, too much contrast). Round 5 fits the art's effective mean and spread to that live reading instead (30.9/13.6/24.9, sd 91.7), lowers the default contrast to 0.18 and re-solves the floor (0.086/0.0043/0.0038): predicted 22.9/2.0/2.0, sd 14.9 (`WorkspaceArtworkIntensity.Predict`), against the target 23/2/2, sd 13–15. *Inferred:* the model treats capture values as linear; re-measure and tune the two settings live. Live round 5 at 0.9/0.18 read 24.8/2.8/2.8 (the mean the new floor predicts) but sd 23.1, the same as at 0.28: the spread did not follow the art's share, so it is probably the pane's own text and rows in the measured region, not the art (*inferred*); measure an area without text to check. One line per backdrop build says what was applied: `Mods workspace: background applied intensity=… contrast=… image=(…) scrim=(… a …) material=…`. Readback: `mods_ui tabs.backgroundIntensity {configured, contrast, applied, color, floor, predictedMeanSd, pinInGameFrame}`, `status.backgroundIntensity`.
- **Replacing the artwork.** `src/Progenitor.Core/Ui/Assets/mods-background.png` (embedded as `progenitor.ui.mods-background.png`) can be swapped for another image of the same or a similar aspect with no code change: the texture's own size is read at load and the image is cover-cropped into the frame, right-aligned (`CoverAlignX` 1) and vertically centered. The `Figure*` constants only describe the current art's focal region (the tree) for its tests.
- **No resize on open.** Before the native frame is read (the first frame of an in-world open, `reason=no-overlay`) a top-tab workspace lays out with the canonical frame under the path `native-pending`: in-game geometry, never presented. Before round 4 it laid out as the 1276×800 floating card, which is what a pane showed for a moment before growing (user report 2026-09-30).
- **Open timing.** Every open from closed is timed until it is first shown, with one line per open: `Mods workspace: open timing total=…ms frames=… spans=[prepare=… backdrop-build=… backdrop-texture-decode=… artwork-donors=… index=… list-refresh=… strip=… sidebar=… show-layout=…] unaccounted=…ms` (`WorkspaceOpenTiming`; readback `status.openTiming`). `unaccounted` is wall time no span covers; since round 6 the line also carries `phases=[begin=ms/frames to-first-tick=… to-show=…]`, which cover the whole open: the open call up to the end of `Begin`, then everything until the backdrop's first tick after it (the rest of the open call, and Unity's own end of frame, canvas rebuild and render), then the frames until the workspace is shown. The backdrop PNG (1672×941, 1.6 MB) was decoded on every open, copied into the interop array one byte per interop call (1.6 million calls); it is now decoded once per Core generation with one block copy, pre-decoded when the MODS top tab first attaches, and destroyed at Core unload (`ModsWorkspaceChrome.ReleaseBackdropTextureCache`). *Inferred by static analysis, not measured:* that decode was most of the 364–508 ms open hitch.
- **The Mods list kept between opens.** The Mods list pane (about 220 ms to build per open, live round 4) is kept hidden instead of disposed when the workspace closes or another tab is selected, and shown again when an open or the Mods tab would build the same list. The key (`ModsMenu.SidebarSignature`, `ModsListKey`) is change counters plus view state, never a recomputed list (round 5 hashed the decided list, which took as long as building it: 209–227 ms live, and reuse never hit): `ModMenuInventory.Generation` (every owed retake, the server context set or cleared), the menu's own counter (`BumpListGeneration`: a plugin loaded, failed, skipped or unloaded through `ModRegistry.Changed`, a mod page registered or removed, the install index served or fetched), `ModProblems.Version` (a problem reported again word for word no longer moves it), the install ledger stamp, the search, the filter, the group toggles and the row width. *Inferred:* a live problem source (one computed when read) can change without moving a counter; the kept list then shows it at the next real change. Round 7 (live round 6: every reopen still logged `list=built`): `ModMenuInventory.RestoreServerContext` bumped the generation on every call, and every inventory snapshot re-adopts the handshake through it, so the key moved on every build in a world. It now moves only when the server rows (guid, version, level, rule, declared), the catalog or the epoch changed (`ModMenuInventory.SameRows`). Every list build logs which parts of the key moved against the kept list: `Mods workspace: list built (inv 12→13, …)` (`ModsListKey.Diff`; readback `status.hosts.lastKeyChange`). A build also takes one inventory snapshot and one ledger scan (a build scope; it used to take three snapshots and a disk scan per caller) and resolves install links from one pass over the index per index generation (`InstallLinkMap`; it used to walk the whole index, with each listing's resting identity, twice per row). The open timing line breaks the list build into `sidebar-search`, `sidebar-model`, `sidebar-entries` (with `entry-listing`, `entry-facts`, `entry-links` summed over rows), `sidebar-top`, `sidebar-rows`, `sidebar-groups`, `sidebar-bottom` and `sidebar-install-links`. Round 9 (live round 8: reopens reused the kept list at 23–31 ms total; a genuine build, the first open after joining, took 271 ms, `entry-facts` 227 of it): each row's facts asked for every problem, which evaluates every live problem source (the keybind and command conflict checks among them), read the preloader's state file and stat-ed the disabled DLLs, asked the pause backend twice, and copied the handshake's provided rows. Inside a list build each of those is now read once (`ModProblems.EnterScope`, `ModSwitch.EnterScope`, `ModsMenu.ProvidedRecords`), and `entry-facts` is broken into `facts-version`, `facts-sides`, `facts-owner`, `facts-switch` and `facts-problems`. A kept list is also rebuilt, hidden, 2 s after the inventory's generation moves while the workspace is closed (`ModMenuInventory.GenerationChanged`, `RefreshParkedList`, log `Mods workspace: list refreshed (inv 0→2) in …ms`), so the first open after a join shows a kept list. *Game-free tested; not live-measured.* Round 10 (live round 9: no refresh after a join; the first open after it 1116 ms, `entry-facts` 554 against a 204 ms list build): the refresh subscribed only when the MODS tab attached through the native route, which the join never reached, so it now subscribes at Core install (`ModsMenu.InstallListKeeper`, from `NativeModsEntry.Install`) and also builds a list when none is kept yet; both ends log (`list refresh scheduled in 2 s (inventory generation N)`, `list prebuilt (none kept) in …ms`, `list refreshed (…)`, `list refresh skipped (workspace open)`). `entry-facts` ran outside the build too: every `mods_ui status` (the bridge reads one after each open) decided every row again with no shared reads. The status is now one `readback-status` span (`WorkspaceOpenTiming.Readback`; the spans inside it are not added to the open's) and uses the same once-per-build reads. The cost itself was the uninstall-pending check: for a row no install ledger names, the seam resolved the plugin against the whole index twice (every listing's resting identity) and read each root's ledger; such a row cannot have an uninstall pending, so it is no longer asked (`SidebarStateOf`, span `entry-state`). *Game-free tested; not live-measured.* Round 11 (live round 10: the idle prebuild and refresh ran, 35 and 18.5 ms, but every open after a join logged `list built (list 1→2)`, then `(list 2→3)`): serving the install index from its cache, or fetching it again, moved the list counter on every open even with the same listings. It now moves only when the index content the list reads changes (`ModsMenu.IndexContentStamp`: the listings' keys, manifest ids, versions and package names). Every bump names its cause (`index-cache-served`, `index-fetched`, `registered:<id>`, `removed:<id>`, `registry:<id>`), and a `list built` line that moved on the list counter says which (`…; list moved by …`). The timing line adds `detail=[show-apply-layout=… show-activate=… show-strip=… show-list=… show-content=…]` (the layout's text measurement apart from the rows' activation, and each pane's show; nested in `show-layout`, so outside the span sums) and a `to-native-frame` phase (the frames spent waiting for the game's frame read, apart from the shows after it). *Game-free tested; not live-measured.* Round 12 (live round 11: after a join `list prebuilt` 262 ms and `list refreshed` 29 ms, then the first open still rebuilt, `list moved by index-cache-served`, and its tab strip cost 138 ms, 2 ms later): the idle prebuild now serves the cached install index first (`ServeCachedIndexNow`, span `prebuild-index`, log `Install: cached index served ahead of the open`), so the open's serve finds it served and moves nothing. The tab strip is kept the same way as the list: soft-hidden, parked on close, reused while it draws the same tabs (`StripKey`: the selected tab and the tones) and was drawn from the native tab donor (a flat strip is rebuilt, since the donor may be reachable later; `MayReuseStrip`), disposed on world leave and unload with the list. It is prebuilt on the frame after the list (`PrebuildStrip`, log `Mods workspace: strip prebuilt in …ms (kept=…, source=…)`), so the two prebuild costs land on different frames. *Game-free tested; not live-measured.* `mods_ui status` carries `hosts` (`ModsMenu.DescribeHostsStatus`, copied whole by the Dev status: `sidebarSource`, `sidebarParked`, `sidebarKey`, `parkedKey`, `lastKeyChange`, `openTiming`, `host`, `backgroundIntensity`, `backgroundContrast`) and `sidebarSource` / `sidebarParked` at the top level; round 6's fields were in `DescribeWorkspace`, which the Dev status merges field by field, so they never reached it. *Not live-measured.* The kept pane is hidden softly (its canvas and raycaster off, objects active: `Ui.SetSoftHide`), so showing it again rebuilds no text meshes. The selection highlight and the search filter are re-applied on reuse. It is disposed on world leave (`CloseForLifecycle`, open or not), Core unload (`ModsMenu.Uninstall`) and whenever a different list is built. It is also built once, hidden, when the MODS top tab first attaches in a Core generation (`PrewarmModsList`, log `Mods workspace: list prewarmed in …ms`), so the first click can reuse it. Readback: `mods_ui status.sidebarSource` (`built`, `reused-hidden`, `reused-parked`) and `status.sidebarParked` (round 6: round 5 put them in the geometry readback only), `list=` at the end of the open timing line, and its `sidebar-signature` / `sidebar` spans. The backdrop (the canvas, the art, the trim copy, the band) is kept the same way since round 6: a close or an Escape parks it (canvas off, the game's divider and black strip given back, the per-frame sync stopped) and the next open in the same host shows it again (`ModsWorkspaceChrome.Park` / `Unpark`, span `backdrop-unpark`); world leave, Core unload, and an open in the other host release it. *Not live-measured.* The strip and the detail pane are still rebuilt per open.
- **Scaler.** Every workspace canvas (panes, backdrop/artwork, curtain) copies one donor through `UnityUiRuntime.AdoptGameScaler` (the curtain's parent menu scaler first).
- **List viewport.** The list viewport is fitted below the pinned top rows again after the pass has placed them (`WorkspaceHostLayout.ListViewportTop`, `RefitAfterPlacement`), so a toolbar that grew between passes cannot cover a list row after a jump (title report: `keys op=jump target=mods` left "Deselect Current Placement" partly behind the toolbar).
- **Input.** `WorkspaceInputPolicy` orders Escape for every host: key capture, then dialog, then search clear, then leaving the empty search box ([SEARCHCLEAR1](#clearing-a-search-searchclear1)), then close. `Ui.OnBootstrapKey`, `ModsMenu.TryConsumeEscapeHide` / `TryClearSearchOnEscape` and `MenuHotkeyGuard.NextCloseHold` all consult it. A press a capture or a dialog took is marked taken until Escape is released (or 400 ms), so the pump's search clear cannot act on the same press after the dialog closed. Only the in-game host holds the game's `Back`/`Menu_Close` (`HoldsGameClose`, the 498 guard) and dismisses the FullscreenMenu on close (`DismissesGameMenuOnClose`); the title has no game menu or input context to hold.

What stays host-specific: the title's full-bleed Play backdrop (no world behind it), the band (title only), the in-game hosted-view and outer-tab holds, the footer-chrome extent, the curtain's −52 top inset (it exposes the game's bar), and the donor ladders' first rung.

**Live, Steam client, 2026-09-30** (commit `2b3a61ed`): a cold title at 3840×1080 drew `title-host applied=1664x916 at=(0,-46) split=360/1288 reason=title-host-model`, matching in game, with the band and **MODS** caption; a cold title at 1600×1024 windowed drew `at=(0,10) reason=title-host-model`, matching in game. Both warm titles drew `at=(0,-20) reason=title-host-carried` from a record the unsized first read had written under the 1920×1080 default canvas (the "warm 1600" capture ran at 3840×1080 full-screen window, so it looked up a 3840 canvas and carried that record). The settle, canvas-size and plausibility gates above are the fix; they, the ornament placement, the hover dimming and the intensity are game-free tested only.

## Entry sites

| Site | What it does |
|---|---|
| Main menu | Owned button `progenitor-ui-core-main-mods`. Hand-built (not cloned: `MenuButton.Update` re-applies the donor `LocalizationKey`) into `MainMenuNewView.MenuCollection.ButtonsParent` at sibling index 0, above Continue. Continue's subtitle (`MenuButton.AdditionalText`) is a child of that button, not a list sibling. Typography (font asset, resolved size, character/word spacing, auto-size, transform scale) is copied from a live single-line neighbor (Options), not hard-coded. Hover swaps that neighbor's `_FontHover` / `_FontStandard` on owned pointer enter/exit (the native title glow; a color tint is not the mechanism) and restores on pointer exit and when the view is destroyed. A post-attach self-check requires both sibling index 0 and at least 40 Unity screen pixels of EXIT-glyph clearance above the social-icon graphics (local/mesh corners through `TransformPoint` then `RectTransformUtility.WorldToScreenPoint` on the canvas event camera; a 3840×1080 PNG row is `1080 - y`). A seventh row translates `ButtonsParent` `offsetMin.y` / `offsetMax.y` up by the measured owned-row cost (50px at 3840×1080, scaled with client height — not the 74px secondary-entry pitch) into the 160px RISING–CONTINUE band so CONTINUE–EXIT stay put (EXIT glyphs ending near y=947, 53px above the icons at y=1000) and MODS stays at least 48px below RISING (predicted 54px at that lift); a lone `anchoredPosition` write does not move a stretched list. Those two gaps are a two-sided bound: a lift that restores EXIT by tucking MODS under the wordmark is rejected, and a lift that frees MODS by pushing EXIT past y=960 is rejected. Spacing shrink is only when that headroom is missing. “No room” is not a corner reason. Fallback to the top-right of `MainMenuNewView` is only for a visibly wrong inline row (wrong label, not interactable, wrong size, or an actual overlap). Click opens the workspace; a second click while it is open is a no-op. One info line names the placement (`inline` or `corner fallback` plus the reason). |
| Top navigation | Owned button `progenitor-ui-core-mods-tab` beside the native `OuterTabGroup` (Emotes, Vampire Powers, Spellbook) while the fullscreen HUD menu is up. Click opens the workspace; a second click while it is open is a no-op (`OnOwnedButtonClicked` / Bootstrap `ClickSlot`). Native tab entries are not replaced. Attach is idempotent per host (reuse a live owned tab; destroy an untracked leftover by name first) and unload destroys the GameObject so a Core swap leaves one MODS entry (`ownedTopTabCount`). A join after Core is already loaded attaches on first host discovery — the rescan counter is not zeroed while that host is held without a button. The button exists only while that HUD menu is current. |
| Shortcut | Default **F10**, rebindable (`OpenSettingsPanel` in the Core cfg). Calls `ModsMenu.Toggle`, including at the main menu (`allowWhenChatUnknown`) and while the workspace is capturing input (`allowWhileCaptured`). Description: “Open or close the Progenitor Mods menu.” At the title it opens the workspace directly. In a world it opens through the game's FullscreenMenu on the MODS tab — see [Shortcut in a world](#shortcut-in-a-world). |

### Shortcut in a world

With a client world and HUD, a shortcut press while the workspace is closed takes the same path as clicking MODS in the top bar (`ModsShortcutRoute`), so the top menu bar and the native tabs are up before the strip is built:

1. If the FullscreenMenu is not up, request it with `HUDMenuManager.GoToMenu(world, FullscreenMenu)` (only while `ViewManager.GetCurrentView()` is `None`, as for the existing in-world request).
2. Each frame, once the menu is up and the owned MODS tab is attached, run that tab's own click: the native route's typed `SetState(7)` + open (`TryOpenViaNativeRoute`), else the imitation button's stored open with the shortcut reason. A workspace opened meanwhile by a MODS click is adopted.
   The open waits until the menu's outer tab bar is active in the hierarchy, not just until the HUD reports the menu.
3. A second press while waiting cancels the open and dismisses a menu this press requested. The open is also canceled when the menu it waited on goes down (Escape) or another HUD menu becomes current (at once if the FullscreenMenu was already seen, else after 10 frames). Log: `Mods workspace: shortcut open cancelled why=<pressed-again|menu-closed|other-menu|…>`.
4. If the menu or the tab has not arrived within 120 frames, if the request is refused, or if the tab's open refuses, the standalone window opens instead, but never over a FullscreenMenu: a menu this press requested is dismissed first and the window opens once it is down (the open is canceled with `menu-stayed-up` if it is still up after 30 frames); a menu the player was already in is kept and the open is canceled (`players-menu-kept:…`). The fallback logs `Mods workspace: shortcut fell back to standalone window why=<no-fullscreen-menu|tab-not-attached|menu-request-refused|native-open-refused:…|imitation-open-refused:…> reason=<shortcut:KEY>` once per Core generation. While the standalone window is open, the in-world "raise FullscreenMenu under an open workspace" request is suppressed.

The owned MODS outer tab's caption follows its native siblings' case (`Mods` beside mixed-case localized names, which the donor's TMP draws in small caps) and re-applies the donor TMP's font style, sizes, spacing and alignment; its font asset and material stay the clone's own, because the button's `FontState` swaps them per selection state. **Source-checked and game-free tested (`NativeTabCaption`), not live-verified.**

`ModsMenu.Toggle()` therefore does not open synchronously in a world: `IsOpen` is still false straight after the call and `ModsMenu.ShortcutOpenPending` is true until the routed open lands. `ModsMenu.Open` opens at once, and `ModsMenu.OpenStandalone` opens the standalone window without the route (also `mods_ui open site=standalone`).

Closing a workspace the shortcut opened over a menu it raised returns to gameplay: after the shortcut again or **Close Mods**, the route waits for the native return transition (at most 30 frames) and then calls `GoToMenu(None)`, logging `Mods workspace: shortcut closed to gameplay …`. Escape already dismisses the menu on this site. A close caused by another native tab (the player chose Spellbook, say) or by a lifecycle edge leaves the menu alone. If the player was already in the menu when they pressed the shortcut, closing returns to the menu, as a MODS click does. A press inside the return window of a raised open keeps that menu the route's, so the next close still returns to gameplay. A Core unload dismisses a menu the route raised or requested (after the native detach), and leaves a menu the player opened. From another HUD menu (inventory, map), the close goes to gameplay, not back to that menu. Readback: `mods_ui tabs` `detail.shortcutRoute {pending, framesLeft, raisedMenu, standalone, lastRoute, lastFallback, lastCancel}`.

**The raised menu's own tab selection (F10SETTLE, source-checked and game-free tested; not live-verified).**

**Live (2026-10-04, Steam client in game, branch fix/f10-opens-spellbook b050da3e):** F10 was pressed by hand from gameplay with no menu up several times; each press logged `shortcut opened through menu route=native raisedMenu=true reason=shortcut:F10` then `shortcut menu settle re-selected MODS over native state=2 frame=0 count=1` (the game selected Spellbook on the first settle frame) and no `close … reason=native-tab`; it was confirmed by hand that it opens on Mods first time. Bridge presses with the menu already up opened and toggle-closed normally (`raisedMenu=false`, `close … reason=shortcut:F10`). **Not live-verified:** a vanilla tab click right after the press, Escape inside the window, gamepad tab switches.

Asked: "how come F10 brings up the spell book and then the second press brings up MODs?" The client log (2026-10-03) shows why:
- The first press raised the FullscreenMenu, and the workspace opened on MODS (`shortcut opened through menu route=native raisedMenu=true`).
- A few frames later the menu finished opening and selected its remembered tab, Spellbook.
- `NativeRouteTick` read that as the player leaving MODS and closed the workspace (`close … reason=native-tab caller=NativeRouteTick`).
- The second press found the menu already up (`raisedMenu=false`), so nothing changed the tab afterwards and the open stuck.

Now, for `ModsShortcutRoute.MenuSettleFrames` (45 frames, about 0.75 s) after a routed open over a menu the shortcut raised, a move of the mapper off MODS is treated as the menu's own. The native route selects MODS again (`NativeRouteTickAction.ReselectOwned`) and the workspace stays open. The menu's choice becomes the return state. The rule has these limits:
- One window re-selects at most twice (`MaxSettleReselects`).
- The window ends when the workspace closes.
- A press of the primary mouse button inside the window ends the re-selection, so a click on a vanilla tab closes Mods as before. Any tab change after the window closes Mods as before.
- A press while the menu is already up, the title-screen window and the standalone fallback have no window.
- F10, Close Mods and Escape inside the window close normally, and the raised menu is dismissed as before.

Log line: `Mods workspace: shortcut menu settle re-selected MODS over native state=<n> frame=<f> count=<c>`. Readback: `mods_ui tabs` → `shortcutRoute.settle {frame, reselects, pointerSeen, reselectsTotal, allowed}`.

**Bridge reproduction (GAMEMENU1):** `mods_ui menu {op: close}` returns the client to plain gameplay through the game's own dismissal. `keybind_press {chord: F10}` then runs the raised-menu path, and `mods_ui menu {op: status}` verifies `tab=mods` with the workspace open. Recipe: [bridge-ops-ui.md](bridge-ops-ui.md#the-games-full-screen-menu-menu-op).

**Known gap:** a gamepad bumper or keyboard tab change inside the first 0.75 s is not told apart from the menu's own selection, so MODS is put back once.

**Verified live** 2026-09-29 on the Steam client, Core 2026.9.73.1, through `mods_ui open site=shortcut` (the same `ModsMenu.Toggle`): from gameplay the open reported `routedThroughMenu=true`, site `top-tab`, `fullscreen-inner`, with the native top bar and tabs; close returned to gameplay; with the menu already up on Map, open then close left the menu on Map. **Not probed live:** Escape during a routed open, other HUD menus (inventory, map as the current HUD menu), rapid presses, death or disconnect mid-route, the fallback window, and a Core swap mid-route. The cancel rules, the re-press rule, the no-fallback-over-a-menu rule, the foreign-selection clear and the unload dismissal were added after that run and are game-free tested (`ModsShortcutRouteTests`) only. Observed on that run: SPELLBOOK stayed drawn selected beside MODS (probe F, Core 2026.9.73.3: after switching to Map it drew Normal, so it is ours, not the game's unspent-points highlight). `FullscreenMenuMapper.OnUpdate` repaints each outer tab through `FullscreenMenu_OuterTabEntry.Refresh`, which calls `SimpleStunSelectable.DoStateTransition` only when `ForceHighlight` changes, and `DoStateTransition` returns early on an object that is not `activeInHierarchy` (native, research `reviews/2026-09-12-native-tab-release/native`). Selecting MODS in the first frames of a menu the route raised, before the tab bar is active, can therefore consume SPELLBOOK's one repaint. The route now opens only once the FullscreenMenu's `OuterTabGroup` is active in the hierarchy (a player's click can only happen then), and 2 and 10 frames after the open it re-runs the same transition on every unselected, unheld native outer tab (`ForceHighlight` true then false, the setter `Refresh` uses). **Source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified.**

Top-tab requires the native FullscreenMenu. From gameplay (HUD `None`) the player path is `HUDMenuManager.GoToMenu(world, FullscreenMenu)` — the same call NativeModsEntry already uses when Mods is open without a top bar, and the same call `mods_ui open site=top-tab` uses before it polls up to 120 Drains for the owned button and invokes it. If the button never attaches, that op fails with `detail.error = "top-tab button not attached"`, reports the HUD menu type in `detail.menu`, and restores the menu (`detail.menuRestored`) when this op requested FullscreenMenu. If Mods is already open in-world while the HUD is `None`, NativeModsEntry requests FullscreenMenu and waits up to 120 frames; if that never arrives, it closes Mods.

The top-tab site has two routes. The **native route** (guarded prototype, job NATIVE1) registers an owned eighth `FullscreenMenu_OuterTabEntry` cloned from a pristine native donor, an owned page under `ViewParent` bearing the game's own concrete `FullscreenMenuView`, and an owned `StateToView` appended to `Views`, with the game's `FullscreenMenuMapper` painting and activating it like any native tab (state = `Views` index = `_OuterTabs` index = 7, requirement `None`); the click is a Bootstrap `UiActionSlot` trampoline (a process-lifetime `Il2CppSystem.Action`, never a Core delegate), which calls typed `SetState(7)`, reads back both `CurrentView` pointers and opens the workspace; a native or gamepad transition away closes the workspace through `ModsMenu.Close`. It is selected only when every invariant holds — Bootstrap offers the adapter, the mapper's menu is this HUD, `ViewParent`/`OuterTabGroup` are alive, both lists hold exactly the seven native states `0..6` in order with no competing slot 7, a requirement-free donor exists, the page's `TabDatas`/`KeyBindingContexts` come back non-null and empty, and the click lease succeeds — and the attach is a transaction (validate → clone → page → append → wire → publish → expose) rolled back in reverse on any failure. On any failure reason the **imitation route** (the hand-built button, curtain, hosted-view hold and outer-tab painter hold) runs unchanged; `mods_ui status.route` / `rects.detail.route` report `native` or `imitation` and `nativeRoute {state, viewsIndex, listIndex, reason, callback, selected, mounted, adapter}` carries the reason (`attempts` counts attach attempts on the current host). A `validate:mapper-menu-mismatch` refusal is reported as `mapper-menu-mismatch:mapper=<0x…|null>,found=<0x…>,mapperPath=<path|n/a|dead>,foundPath=<path>,mapperRunning=<bool>` (job NATIVE2): when the mapper holds no live menu yet the imitation route stays attached and the native validation is rehearsed read-only every 30 frames while the menu is up, up to 20 attempts, and on success the imitation entry is dropped before the native route registers; when the mapper holds a different live menu, that menu is the registration target and the native readback carries `menu-authority:mapper,mapperPath=…,foundPath=…`. On the native route there is no painter hold and no curtain; native hover feedback on other tabs is native behavior. Detach (Core unload, HUD rebuild, reconnect) disables the tab, closes the workspace, moves the mapper to the previous native page (or System) through typed `SetState` and verifies it while the owned record and instance are alive, resets a remembered index 7, removes only the owned list entry and record (restoring the original `Views` array only if it is still exactly the one installed), releases the click lease and destroys the owned objects; the mapper's view-state entity is never destroyed by Progenitor. Live acceptance of this route is pending; see the roadmap.

Job NATIVE3 closed the three defects seen after the first live registration. **Frame:** the workspace chrome root stays a top-level `ScreenSpaceOverlay` canvas on the native route (`NativeModsEntry.NativeRouteMountsChrome = false`); parenting it under the owned page had left the nested canvas without a screen-driven size (`no-overlay`, fallback frame) while the panes are separate top-level `overlay-N` canvases placed from the same screen-space frame, so the frame is read exactly as on the imitation route — `ViewParent` plus the footer union — and the workspace follows `CurrentView` through the native-route tick. **Ops:** `mods_ui open site=top-tab` on the native route runs the click's own path (`NativeRouteOps.Open`: typed `SetState(7)` through the registration with readback, then the stored open; `detail.handler = NativeOuterTabRegistration.TrySelectOwned+ModsMenu.Open`, `detail.route = native`, `detail.steps`), `close` runs `ModsMenu.Close` then the typed return transition to the saved native page while the mapper still selects the owned one (`detail.handler = ModsMenu.Close+NativeOuterTabRegistration.ReturnSelection`, `detail.nativeReturn` names a failed return the tick retries), and `native_tab` without the FullscreenMenu up is a no-op with `detail.reason = no-menu`. **Menu closing:** the tick resolves the menu through the HUD's current menu type, which changes before the mapper's `OnStopRunning`, so a registration with no found menu is detached at once: while the mapper still holds the owned record as its `_CurrentView` the typed `SetState(previous)` transition runs (the mapper deactivates the owned page like any native transition); once that field is null the transition is skipped and `_SelectedOuterTab` is reset through the typed field, a `menu.CurrentView` still naming the owned record is nulled, the owned entry and record are removed and the objects destroyed — a failed transition holds the registration only while the FullscreenMenu is still the HUD's current menu, and a held registration is released the same way when the menu goes. After any detach `_SelectedOuterTab` is a native index and `menu.Views` the native array, so the next P raises the menu on the saved page. **Doubled list:** a menu reopen can re-append the game's seven entries (`OnStartRunning` appends on its missing/dead-menu branch without clearing); a list repeating native states is a foreign change — attach refuses it (`validate:outer-list-count:14`), reconcile detaches with `outer-list-doubled:N` (never `Reappend`, which is reserved for an exact seven-entry rebuild) and only the owned entry is removed, so the imitation route runs and the menu stays openable.

Job NATIVE4 keeps the route across a Core hot swap: the unload path detaches the registration on its own reason (`Mods top tab detached (native route, core-unload)`) whatever the menu state — the return transition still runs while the menu is up, but a failure no longer holds it and the owned `StateToView`, list entry and objects go even when only the owned page died with a HUD rebuild — and an attach that finds state 7 already taken prunes it when it is this integration's leftover (page object named `progenitor-ui-core-mods-page`, or destroyed; clone named `progenitor-ui-core-mods-tab`), logging `native route: pruned stale owned slot (views@7, page=…, gen=unknown, why=…)` and reporting `nativeRoute.pruned`/`nativeRoute.staleReason`, while a foreign state-7 entry keeps the `competing-slot-7` refusal unchanged.

Job NATIVE5 closes the two gaps that still cost the route after a swap: every owned leftover is pruned before the shape check — an `_OuterTabs` entry or `OuterTabGroup` child named `progenitor-ui-core-mods-tab`, or a `Views` record whose page is named `progenitor-ui-core-mods-page`, goes whatever its `State` (the live gen-5 refusal was `validate:outer-list-count:8` on such a leftover carrying a foreign state), the unload/Drop path removes the owned entry from the mapper's `_OuterTabs` as well as destroying the object, and a shape/count refusal is now retried while a menu is up (up to `NativeRouteRetry.MaxShapeAttempts` = 5 attempts, after the imitation attach's own sweep) with `nativeRoute.retryable` / `nextAttemptOn` (`menu-open` | `none`) and `pruned`/`staleReason` kept on the entry's status so they survive a refused registration — only `adapter-unavailable` and a live foreign state-7 owner stay terminal.

## Two-pane workspace

The in-game tab site sizes the workspace frame, backdrop and two panes to the union of `FullscreenMenu.ViewParent` and native menu-root chrome whose rect overlaps ViewParent horizontally, whose bottom is ≤ 120 overlay units below it (the footer strip: System-page `EscapeMenuView.ConnectionInfoRect` / `ConnectionInfoText*` / `ReportABugButton` / `FAQButton`, or unnamed FullscreenMenu children — `FullscreenMenu` declares no footer field), excluding the outer-tab group and anything wider than 1.2× ViewParent (HUD `BottomBarParent`). The hosted page's `BackgroundImage` / `Background` (429454 / 428944) stretch-fills ViewParent and is not the extent. That walk is once per open (`TransformPoint` of two corners, cached as an overhang until close); a miss is ViewParent. `mods_ui rects` `workspace-frame` matches the grown extent; `detail.frameExtentWhy` names the objects that set the bottom. If ViewParent cannot be read, the previous floating-card size is kept and one info line names `path=fallback` plus why. In game the native frame is read on open before the first layout and panes stay invisible until that read succeeds (`detail.firstShowFrame` / `layoutBeforeShow`; log `Ui: workspace shown frame=<n> framePath=<native-container|fallback>`); the title site has its own frame (below). Ultrawide content areas wider than 2:1 are centered and width-capped so the sidebar stays a sidebar (30% of inner width after the gap, max 360). The title-screen site has no ViewParent; since HOSTS1 it draws the in-game frame itself (path `title-host`, see [One layout for both hosts](#one-layout-for-both-hosts)), not a ratio fit or the floating cards. The owned main-menu MODS button is unchanged. The hold deactivates only the hosted page instance and the header strip; footer chrome of the menu root is left alone (the blocker at 4997 still covers it for input).

The embedded image `progenitor.ui.mods-background.png` (1672×941) is the backdrop behind both panes, cover-cropped into the whole workspace frame (not the detail pane alone) the way Spellbook’s `BackgroundImage` / Vampire Powers’ `Background` fill ViewParent — content area only, not under the outer tab bar. Overflow is clipped to the frame (`RectMask2D` on the holder). The crop is right-weighted so the tree on the right survives at 3840×1080 and 1600×1024. A dark scrim (alpha 0.52) sits on the art; sidebar and detail pane fills share alpha 0.70 so the image ties the two boxes together without dropping text contrast. The Unity texture is destroyed on Close / Uninstall.

Owned overlay canvases sit in one band, lowest first: pointer blocker 4997, backdrop 4998, panels 4999, `Ui.Panel` overlays 5000 (`ModsCanvasBand.Consent`, named for the consent prompt removed 2026-10-05). Those numbers are derived from `UiOwnership.OverlaySorting`; two root canvases at the same order have no defined winner. At the title site the backdrop canvas's **first** child is a full-bleed clone of Play's `MenuBackgroundImage` (metadata-backed; see Close and back); workspace art and the two panes sit above it.

Both panes use `UiCaptureMode.WhileShown`. Search filters as you type: the sidebar field's Bootstrap text slot is wired to TMP `onValueChanged` when the field is created (settings editors stay on `onEndEdit`). Case-insensitive match on display name or id; clearing the field restores every row. The box has a clear button while it has text, and closing the workspace empties it ([SEARCHCLEAR1](#clearing-a-search-searchclear1)). `status.search` is the live field text. Those keystrokes do not reach gameplay: `WhileShown` registers Core’s gameplay block (order 599) for as long as a pane is shown, and a focused TMP field also makes `TextFieldFocused` true so `Keybinds.Pump` returns before any bind fires. They do not reach the fullscreen menu's own tab shortcuts either: while either search field (sidebar or the Install toolbar's `CellKind.Input` cell) has owned focus, `MenuHotkeyGuard` adds the eight menu-open actions and the four tab-cycling actions to that same per-frame mask, so typing `p` edits the text instead of switching to Social, and `status.focus` reports `{field, hotkeysBlocked, blockedActions[]}`. The mask that takes those actions is applied by a second input context at order 498 — registered and removed with the 599 one, masking the hotkey set alone and only while a field has owned focus — because `FullscreenMenuMapper`, which reads the in-menu letters, is itself a context at order 502 and a mask at 599 is walked after it; `status.focus.guard` reports `{registered, order, masked}`, where `masked` is applications in the last second.

The tab strip is five buttons in one row (Mods / Install Mods / [Keybinds](#keybinds-tab) / Preferences / Server, three homes 2026-10-06; one `ModsTabStrip.Order` list feeds both the title-screen and the in-game strip) across the workspace frame (no panel title): a `fallback` frame keeps it in the unused `TopInset` band `Gap` above the panes; a `native-container` or `title-host` frame puts it inside the frame top (panes start below the strip by strip height + `Gap`) so the native tab bar stays free — `ForStrip` / `ForFrame` follow the frame path, not the site string (3840×1080 and 1600×1024). No panel title (`Title.SetActive(false)`); chrome is the catalogue/footer button fill only. Selected gold is `MenuRowTone.Selected`. The strip is a workspace pane (panels band 4999, `WhileShown`), shown/hidden/disposed with the workspace, never parented under `UICanvasBase`. `mods_ui rects` emits four `kind=tab-strip` rows (`name`, `selected`, `x y width height visible`). Each strip button's uGUI `onClick` selects `Order[i]` for button i (not row index among all rows — the hidden title must not shift Mods onto Install); a strip click is never Close and never a sidebar row. Captions are stretched to the button with TMP `raycastTarget` off so EventSystem hits that button's Image, not a full-strip label overlapping the neighbor. A pointer on a strip button selects the slot under that point (the strip frame split into four), and a click on the Install search rect focuses that field so typed keys update `status.search`. Switching tabs never calls `ModsMenu.Close()`; `IsOpen` and capture stay. Each tab remembers its own search, selection and scroll. The Install search field uses the same shown-input / `UiTextEdit` path as Mods and refilters listing rows in place (placeholder, caret, Backspace repeat, two-stage Escape). Real OS clicks take the uGUI `Button.onClick` path (`Ui: tabdiag click-strip` may be absent); `status.tab` after those clicks is `mods` / `install` / `configuration`.

- **Mods (left, MODS1 — source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified).** *MODLIST1 redraws every entry, the marker and the group captions described here; see [Mods list (MODLIST1)](#mods-list-modlist1). Provenance, grouping, order, search and the readback below are unchanged.* The player's installed mods, searchable by name or id (case-insensitive). Every loaded mod is listed, whatever installed it, and each row carries its provenance, decided in `ModMenuInventory.BuildSidebar` from the same evidence the Install tab reads: **`progenitor` first** for a plugin a root's `BepInEx/progenitor-install.json` install ledger lists as one of the player's mods (a `mod` row, or the `pending` download that becomes one — `ModMenuInventory.IsLedgeredMod`), then `required` for the plugin loader and this framework's own plugins (`ModMenuInventory.FrameworkIds`, an explicit allowlist since [FWALLOW1](#framework-plugins-and-reload-folder-mods-fwallow1): `progenitor` — Core, whose GUID Bootstrap shares — `progenitor.dev` and `progenitor.preloader`, matched exactly; panes ask `ModMenuInventory.IsFrameworkId` instead of naming ids of their own), then `progenitor` for any other id the ledger names (INSTORD2 — by the package id in the row's source or by the plugin file name it wrote), then `reload` for a plugin whose DLL sits in `BepInEx/progenitor-reload` with no ledger row (FWALLOW1: samples, examples, apivalidation and test plugins, and hand-copied builds), then `server` for an entry only the server reported, else `other`. The ledger is asked before the id prefix because a mod the player installs may publish a GUID under this framework's namespace: MODSPOLISH1 found `progenitor.hotdemo`, installed from the Install tab and ledgered `mod`, drawn in `Required (8)` with no Remove (live, 2026-09-13). A ledgered mod is never required, whatever its id spells; `Required` stays BepInEx plus the framework's own images, which are ledgered `framework` or not ledgered at all. The default list is the `progenitor`, `reload` and `other` rows loaded on this client. **Required (N)** (MODS2, decided 2026-09-13 — “show the progenitor mods and BepInEx as required without the option to uninstall them”) is a collapsed group at the **bottom** of the sidebar, after the player's mods and after `On this server (N)`, because a player is not looking for those entries; it holds `BepInEx <version>` first (the loader, whose version is the chainloader assembly's — `typeof(IL2CPPChainloader).Assembly.GetName().Version`, since `BepInEx.Paths` carries folders and not a version — and whose sidebar id is `bepinex`, an id nothing loads under), then `Progenitor <version>`, then the rest of `ModMenuInventory.FrameworkIds` (the dev bridge and the preloader; FWALLOW1 took the samples out) that the install ledger does not list as a player's mod — the HotDemo dummy, installed from the Install tab, is one of the player's mods and sits in the default list. Its rows are read-only: no Remove, no Update; their settings groups and custom pages still open as before, and the loader's page is one line, `The plugin loader. Required by every mod; not managed here.` The group's expanded state is Mods-tab memory (`ModsTabPageState.RequiredGroupExpanded`). MODS1's **Developer mods** checkbox, `ModsTabPageState.ShowDeveloper` and the `hiddenDeveloper` / `showDeveloper` readback fields are gone with it. Server-only entries keep their own collapsed group, `On this server (N)`, drawn between the mods and the required group and expanding in place (`ModsTabPageState.ServerGroupExpanded`); those rows stay read-only and their page keeps the existing “loading is unconfirmed” reason. Search applies to whatever is shown. **Overview / refresh** and **Close Mods** are unchanged: Overview / refresh clears the selection, returns the right pane to the overview, and resets the list scroll to the top (`ScrollRect.verticalNormalizedPosition` 1, not 0). An empty match shows `No matching mods.` Scratch (`progenitor.scratch`) is omitted. MODSPAGE1 adds two things to those rows. A **ledgered install nothing has loaded yet is listed** — before this the sidebar was built from loaded plugins and settings-catalog ids only, so a promoted-but-unloaded package appeared nowhere; such a row is appended after the loaded ones, named from its package (`ExampleTeam-PhialStore` → `PhialStore`) or from the plugin file the ledger wrote, and it counts in `status.mods.shown` and the overview sentence like any other installed mod (`ModMenuInventory.LedgerOnlyRows`). POLISH2 (live 2026-09-13, client gen 2): a sidebar row's state is read from those ledger rows rather than counted — `ModsMenu.SidebarStateOf` and the page's `Status:` word both ask `ModPageStates.DecideFromLedger`, which follows `InstalledState`'s own order (the `.uninstall` marker, then a copy this process has loaded, then a `progenitor-reload/` copy on *this* root that it has not loaded — still pending, then a promoted `mod` copy on the local-server root, which is **installed** because that server started with it, `InstalledState.IsPromotedServerLedgerRow`), so a third-party command library and a mod that depends on it, promoted by the restarted local server and read `installed` / `halves.server: loaded` on the Install tab, no longer read `pendload` here. POLISH2 (same check): when a joined server's mod-list claim names a plugin the install ledger already names, the claim is **folded into the ledger row** instead of drawing the plugin twice — the live sidebar listed a Progenitor row named after a library's package and a server row named by the same plugin's GUID (in the shape of `ExampleCommandFramework` and `gg.dyno.ExampleCommandFramework`) for one plugin. The match is `ModMenuInventory.LedgerRowsForPlugin`'s comparison first and then the claim GUID's last dotted segment against the file the ledger wrote or the package's name half (`ModMenuInventory.ClaimNamesLedgeredPlugin`; a bare id and a segment under three characters are never matched that way); the ledger row survives because it is the one that can be uninstalled, it gains the claim's requirement text, and `On this server (N)` counts only the claims no install of ours explains. And each row carries its **warning marker**, drawn the mock's way since KIT1: the red `(!)` in front for a Progenitor mod missing its server half is a part of the row itself (`panel.Button(caption, marker, dot)`, docs/ui.md), so the caption is the mod's name and nothing else and the state is named in the readback rather than spelled in glyphs (`entries[].state` = `installed` | `pendload` | `penduninst`, `entries[].warning`, `entries[].marker`, `entries[].dot`). **No row draws a state dot** (HEAD2, requested 2026-09-13 on the live 3840×1080 client, "lets not use the lights (yellow in this case) on the listing in the menu on the left"): the yellow pending-load and orange pending-uninstall squares KIT1 drew at the right edge are gone, `ModsMenu.SidebarDotOf` answers null for every row so `entries[].dot` is empty, the state stays on the page (`Status:`) and on the Install tab, and the caption gets back the width a dot cost — which is also what stopped a long name (23 characters, a command library's) running under one. An installed row with no warning is exactly its name, as before. `entries[].status` keeps its own meaning, the inventory's status word. **Kit gap:** the sidebar row is one clipped button caption (`MenuRowStyle.CatalogCaption`) and the kit has no second line for it, so the muted `version · provenance word` line is carried on the row's detail page and in the readback (`entries[].subtitle`) rather than under the row; putting it under the row needs a new row kind in `MenuPanelLayout` / `UnityUiRuntime`, which this job does not own.
- **Mods (right, overview):** the default list's count — `5 mods found on this client.` (`ModsSidebarModel.LocalSummary`; MODS2 dropped the developer-rows half of the sentence with the toggle). That sentence and `status.mods.shown` count exactly the same rows — the ones that pass the sidebar search — so they cannot disagree (live client gen 4 read 5 on the page against `hiddenDeveloper` 6, two different snapshots of two different sets); the required and server rows have their own counts and are never in the sentence. Then the server inventory summary, a note that personal options stay on this client, and **Install and update mods on the Install mods tab; remove Progenitor-installed mods from their page here.** (`ModMenuInventory.OverviewActionsLine`, replacing the stale “Installation, updates and coordinated disabling are not available in this menu yet”). Progenitor's own settings are on its mod page (Mods → Required → Progenitor); the Configuration tab's first row links there ([Where Progenitor's settings are drawn](#where-progenitors-settings-are-drawn)).

- **Mods (right, selected mod) — page layout since SETTINGS1 (source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified; [mod-settings.md](mod-settings.md) is authoritative where it differs from the MODSPAGE1 paragraph below).** The first row is the framework-owned **status line**, which a mod cannot change: the on/off switch (stage 3: clickable when it can act, [mod-settings.md](mod-settings.md#stage-3-the-enable-switch)), the status word (worst first: `Failed to load`, `Loaded with problems`, `Disabled`, `Paused for this server`, a restart owed, `Enabled`; `Not on this client`) with a conflict chip when there are conflicts, then the problems line under the bar, the installed version, one sides badge (`Client` / `Server` / `Both`), for a two-sided mod whether its server part runs on this server when that is known, and the action button (**Uninstall** / **Cancel uninstall** / **Cancel install**). The required-server warning sits directly under it. Then the **mod area**: by default the title and `by <author> · <one-liner>` with the release date and **Like** between them, or the mod's own content from `ModMenuRegistration.Header`. Then the provenance notes and custom-page buttons, then **Settings**: the mod's settings drawn inline in sections (each `.Settings(title, …)` group, then **General**, **Keys**, **Actions**), with a search field above ten settings (it hides non-matching rows in place) and **Reset all to defaults** (a confirm dialog that closes with the page). The group buttons and the **All settings and keybinds** button are gone from the page. Readback: `page-framework`, `page-status`, `page-action` and `page-badges` are on the status line's cell row; `page-title`, `page-meta`, `like` and `page-oneliner` on the default mod area's rows; `page-header {kind, error}`; `page-section`, `page-setting`, `page-setting-note`, `page-setting-reset`, `page-settings-search` and `page-settings-reset-all` on the settings rows.
- **Mods (right, selected mod) — the mod page (MODSPAGE1, from the approved mock; source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified):** the name, then `<version> · <released> · by <author>` with the **Like** control, then the listing's one-liner (a mod no listing names falls back to the description its author registered with `ctx.Menu`). Under that the **status label** — a label, never a button — `Status: Installed` / `Status: Pending restart · to load` / `Status: Pending restart · to uninstall`, the action button for that state (**Uninstall** / **Cancel uninstall** / **Cancel install**), and the side badges (`Client` / `Server`, or `Client` + `Server · required` for a Progenitor package whose manifest declares it is needed on both sides). A badge is what is **known**: a plugin no listing names draws none, and MODSPOLISH1 dropped the `Unknown until checked` placeholder that used to stand in for one, because with no one-liner either it was the only cell on the head block's third row and the page drew an empty band under the meta line to hold it (live, a third-party HUD mod, 2026-09-13) — with neither, `HeadBlockLayout.Build` emits no third row and `page-oneliner` / `page-badges` are absent, the way `page-warning` is. The version shown is the **installed** one (the ledger row's, else what the inventory observed); the release date belongs to the listing's current version, which is also why a newer listing still adds the `Update available` line below. The state is decided from evidence in that order (`ModPageStates.Decide`): the pending-uninstall marker, then whether an install-ledger row names the plugin at all, then whether this process actually has it loaded — a hot-loaded copy is never pending, because it is installed the moment it loads and gone the moment it is removed. **Every action asks a y/n first** (SERVERINST2's shape: heading, body, yes, no) and the body says what changes and when: **Uninstall** → “Removed from your client at the next restart of V Rising. It stays loaded until then. Your settings for it are kept.” (a hot-loaded Progenitor mod: “Unloads now; nothing to restart.”, plus “Also removed from your local server at its next restart.” when the server half is installed too); **Reactivate** → “Cancels the pending removal. Nothing changes at the next restart.”; **Cancel install** → “The downloaded file is deleted; nothing loads at the next restart.” Only a `progenitor` row offers an action; `other`, `required` and server-only rows keep their read-only note and no button, which is MODS1's rule and not the mock's (the mock drew Uninstall on other-tool rows as well). The **pending-uninstall state and the three actions go through `Ui/ModUninstallSeam.cs` (`IModUninstallSeam`, assigned to `ModsMenu.UninstallSeam`), which job INSTALL2 implements** — it owns the ledger flag and the `.uninstall` marker beside the reload copy. While that seam is null: `IsUninstallPending` is false, a confirmed **Uninstall** is today's verified ledger-scoped removal (it deletes exactly the ledgered pending and promoted copies and the page reads `Removed` or `Removed — still loaded until this process restarts`), **Cancel install** is that same removal of the written-but-unloaded copy, and **Reactivate** is refused with `This action is not available yet: nothing here can change the pending state.` rather than claimed. A **required-server warning** line — `(!) This mod needs its server component and only the client half is installed here. It will not work on the servers you join until the server has it too.` — is shown only for a manifest-verified package declaring `side: both` whose client half is here with no server half on the local-server root and none in the joined server's mod list; a Thunderstore `Server`+`Client` category pair is not a declaration and never raises it. Below a rule comes **Settings**: when the plugin exposes Progenitor settings (a registered group, or settings-catalog rows) its groups and **All settings and keybinds**; otherwise the one line `This mod does not expose Progenitor settings. Configure it the way its author describes on the store page.` followed by the listing's README rendered one row per markdown block (README2, from the catalog's document cache or `progenitor-cache/readme/…`; `no description available` when no listing names the plugin — the page never fetches a README from a build, the Install tab's prefetch fills that cache).
