The store link sits at the top of that section and opens the listing's `package_url` with `Application.OpenURL` (an http(s) URL only; without a listing the page says `No store page is known for this mod.`). **Like** is per package, stored in `BepInEx/progenitor-cache/likes.json` (`{package: {liked, sentUtc, confirmedUtc, count}}`, `Ui/ModLikes.cs`) and reflected the moment it is pressed. Thunderstore counts a rating only from a signed-in account, so the send needs `[Thunderstore] SessionToken` (empty by default, never logged, sent to nothing but thunderstore.io): with a token the control walks `sending…` → `counted` on a 2xx from `POST https://thunderstore.io/api/experimental/package/{namespace}/{name}/rate/` with `{"target_state":"rated"}`, and a failure keeps the press and says `saved locally`, retried once on the next page open. Without a token nothing is sent at all. The count is the listing's rating score plus one while the press is unconfirmed, and LINK1 parses the v1 `rating_score` onto `CatalogEntry.RatingScore` (the Install rows read it back as `listing.rating`), so that base is now the store's own number and the store file's remembered count is only the fallback for a listing whose provider published none. **LINK1:** a loaded plugin that no install-ledger source, identity record or listing id names is resolved by `Install/PluginListingMatcher.cs` — its display name, else its assembly file stem, spelling the package name or the package's full name with letters and digits only and case-insensitively (`BasinHoverTips` ↔ `ExampleTeam-BasinHoverTips`), and two owners of one name separated only by the loaded version and otherwise refused — and the match is reported (since ABOUTSRC1 nothing of a listing is shown unless this side's install ledger row or a package `manifest.json` for the DLL names its package, and its README only at the listing's own version; see [About text on the mod page](modules.md#about-text-on-the-mod-page)), with the answer cached in `BepInEx/progenitor-cache/identity/loaded/<guid>.json` with its confidence and recomputed when the loaded version changes; a name match is shown and never acted on, so it drives neither Remove nor a dependency decision, and the title row reads back `page-listing {matched: source|identity|id|name|none, key, listingVersion, verified: ledger|manifest|unverified|none, versionMatch, shown}` (`matched`, `key` and `listingVersion` are the candidate listing; `verified`, `versionMatch` and `shown` are what the page displays). Since KIT1 the page is the mock's layout and not only its words: the head is one `panel.HeadBlock` — a two-column block whose right column carries the colored status word, the action button and the side badge pills beside the title, meta line and one-liner instead of under them — the title is drawn at the mock's display size, each y/n is one `panel.Prompt` with No and Yes right-aligned on one row, and the store link is the icon-only `panel.IconLink` (`»`) — beside the **Settings** heading until SETTINGS1 stage 2, which made it a captioned **Store page** button in the header's button row and redesigned the page (status bar, header, settings grid: [mod-settings.md](mod-settings.md#page-design-stage-2)); the pane's own centered title is dropped (`Ui.HidePaneTitle`) because the head block is the page's name, and the body under the head is left-aligned like the Configuration tab's rows; the tones and the blocks are documented in docs/ui.md, and every color is still named in the readback so nothing has to be inferred from a pixel. Several of MODSPAGE1's readback keys now share a row, because the right column sits beside the left one rather than under it: `page-title` and `page-status` arrive on the head's title row, `page-meta`, `like` and `page-action` on its meta row, `page-oneliner` and `page-badges` on the third, each with a `rect` per cell. Under the head comes one **muted provenance block** (MODSPOLISH1, `ModPageCopy.ProvenanceBlock`): the row's read-only sentence, what the server asks of it and — server-only rows — why its loading is unconfirmed, each given its full stop and joined with one space. It replaced three full-size body rows that mostly repeated the head; the sidebar subtitle row (`1.0.19 · another tool`) is gone entirely, because the head's meta line already carries the version and the action button already says whether this menu may remove the file (`ModsSidebarRow.Subtitle` stays the readback's `entries[].subtitle`). The head block's right column — the status word, the action button and the badges — stops one scrollbar band short of the row's right edge (HEAD2, `HeadBlockLayout.RightColumnInset`; the live 3840×1080 page drew them at the pane viewport's own clip, x 2732 on a 1484..2732 row, and the wider **Uninstall** button came out cropped), and every head row carries that inset so the whole block ends on one content edge. **POLISH3 item C:** the `»` store link takes that same inset. It is placed by the generic settings row's control column, which ends flush with the row, so live at 3840×1080 it sat 12 px right of the status word with its right side half under the pane's scrollbar; `MenuPanelLayout.IconLinkCentreX` now subtracts `HeadBlockLayout.RightColumnInset` for square controls only (the icon link is the sole caller of `AttachRightControl`), so the link ends at the head block's content edge and no other pane's control column moves — an editor control still fills its column from the left. **Kit gaps that remain:** a sidebar row still has no second line (MODS1), and `mods_ui rects` cannot measure a cell row — it matches a TMP caption against the readback `label`, which a cell row's key is not — so the head block's geometry is read from `widget_read` instead, where every head-block row and every one of its cells carries a `rect`.
- **Mods (right, settings):** the sidebar stays; `mods_ui select page=settings` still opens a group, or the whole plugin, as a menu-hosted settings pane. The mod page itself no longer links to that pane (SETTINGS1).
- **Mods (right, custom page):** the plugin’s `Page` builder fills the supplied panel.
- **Install mods (LIST2 list view polished by LIST3, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified):** one full-width list-view pane (LIST3: `ModsMenu.BuildInstallDetail` configures it with `MenuPaneMode.FullWidth` — `MenuPanelLayout.ForFrame(frame, sidebar: false, mode)` gives it the whole frame width with no sidebar column, and `UnityPanel` draws no pane title for it, the tab strip already reading "Install mods" — so the list spans ≈ 1624 px at 3840×1080 instead of the old 1248 px detail column; `ModsMenu.BuildInstallPanes` disposes any sidebar pane; the Mods and Configuration panes stay on the sidebar / detail split). Pinned above the list, in order: the toolbar `CellRow` (`toolbar`: the **Search mods** field at its left as an `input` cell ≈ 380 px wide — the kit's `CellKind.Input`, a `TMP_InputField` armed like the Mods search with a placeholder root that hides once the player types, its value the cell's `text`, edits raised per keystroke through the row's `OnChanged(cellKey, …)` — then the count as plain body text (a mono cell drew `149 listings` letter-spaced); there is no sort button, because the column header is the sort control ([Install list columns](#install-list-columns)); **Recently released is the default** (POLISH2, requested 2026-09-13 "change the default sort from Downloads to recent"): a fresh pane opens on it with the newest `date_created` first and undated listings last, the header's sorted marker sits on Released, an unknown readback sort key falls back to it, and `Clear filters` still keeps whichever sort the player chose; the indicators are `CellLayout.SortIndicator` U+25BC and `SortIndicatorAscending` U+25B2, both WGL4, because U+25BE drew as TMP's missing-glyph box live), the filter `CellRow` (`filters`: the muted caption `Side`, the single-choice chips **Any** / **Server** / **Client** / **Both** — all four fixed at one width, the widest side caption plus one glyph of slack (`InstallModsPane.SideChipWidth`), since the glyph estimate under-measured `Any` / `Both` to `A…` / `Bo…` live — the toggle chips **No restart needed** and **Current update (<tag>)** — gold when on — and **Clear filters**, visible only off the defaults) and the `CellHeader` (`header`: Mod · Author · Side · Version · Released · Installed · Downloads · Status; see [Install list columns](#install-list-columns)). The list itself is one two-line (53 px) `CellRow` per listing keyed by the catalog key; its columns, widths, narrow-pane behavior, sort and Status column are described in [Install list columns](#install-list-columns). Search is description-first (then display name, then owner, then the README text once known); raw API metadata is never searched. The count reads `N listings` at the defaults and `F listings filtered · M shown` when narrowed beyond them (filtered = visible at the defaults − visible now; a widening beyond the defaults, such as the update chip off, reads plain `N listings`); **Clear filters** restores no search, any side, the restart chip off and the update chip on, and keeps the sort. Tools (Thunderstore `Tools` category) stay listed; their Status column reads `Tool, not a plugin` with no button; there is no hide-tools filter. The plugin loader is listed the same way (MODS2): a BepInExPack listing — recognized from its Thunderstore metadata by `LoaderPackages` (`owner == "BepInEx"` plus the `BepInExPack` name prefix, or one of its known package full names such as `BepInEx-BepInExPack_V_Rising`; the inspector's `core/` archive layout is the same fact where a package has been inspected) and never from a display name — reads **Installed · loader**, disabled and in the ok tone, with the drawer note `The plugin loader is already installed; Progenitor never writes BepInEx/core.` Its readback state is `loader`, and like a tool it is not installable: the click is ignored and it never counts towards `PendingCount` or the pending bar. Filters and search re-filter the built rows in place (no rebuild, the search field keeps focus); a row click, a sort change and an install phase change rebuild the pane on the next Drain. There is no footer and no pane title (LIST3 items 8 and 9: the strip already reads "Install mods"; Escape and the native tabs close the workspace; the index refreshes itself), so at the 916 px native frame the list shows at least ten rows (`InstallModsPane.VisibleListingRows`; eight fit before). While any listing is pending a two-row bar is pinned under the list — `N mods installed — restart the game to load them.` with a **Restart now** button that only logs (the restart route is RESTART1's; nothing starts or stops a process). The package index refreshes automatically (LIST3 item 10, `InstallIndexRefresher`): when the Mods workspace opens (`ModsMenu.Begin` → `EnsureIndexForOpen`, once per open, not per tab switch, reset on `Close`), the last fetched index under `BepInEx/progenitor-cache/index.json` (its fetch time in `index.stamp`) is handed to the catalog at once and a check runs through the service's own transport and JSON sink (a conditional, compressed `ConditionalIndexFetch` → `InstallCatalogService.LoadIndexFromJson`; see [Thunderstore traffic](#thunderstore-traffic-tstraffic1)), replacing the cached list when it lands; a later open in the same session checks again only when the index on show is older than `InstallIndexRefresher.RefreshInterval` (30 min); a failed refresh over a list on show keeps that list and logs; with nothing on show the pane reads `Loading the package index…` and, on failure, the error with **Retry** — the one manual refresh left. No hash, tier, "managed elsewhere" or framework wording appears anywhere on the tab.
- **Install drawer and flow (LIST2 + DL1, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified):** clicking a row opens its drawer beneath it (a group of kit rows inserted after the row; one drawer at a time; clicking the open row closes it; the row's chevron rotates and the row takes the selected fill) **and scrolls it into view (LIST4 item 3 — **superseded by INSTALLSIDES2: the Install list no longer scrolls on a drawer toggle; see [round 2](#install-rows-per-side-round-2-installsides2)**):** if the row and its drawer already fit below the row's current position nothing moves, and otherwise the list scrolls so the row's top edge sits on **the list viewport's own top edge** — not the pane frame's, and not the row parent's — with the drawer as visible as the content allows (clamped to the content's end on the last row). The row's position down the content is derived from the live viewport rect in Unity screen space (`MenuPanelLayout.RevealOffsetFromScreen`; bottom-origin, y growing upward, so a row further down the content has a *smaller* y and a rect's top edge is `y + height`), and the measurement is taken after the build's rows are placed rather than inside `Build`, where they are still an intermediate layout. Both were defects: measuring a row's `anchoredPosition` against the row parent (whose top is the frame top) and measuring mid-build each left the opened row outside the list with the chooser row's chips off the screen, which is what "clicking the chip does nothing" had become. Worked example from the live client at 3840×1080: viewport `{y 336, height 708}` (spanning y 336⁐1044), the opened row's rect `{y 73, h 53}` (top edge 126, below the list) → the reveal writes an offset of 1044 − 126 = 918 px, leaving that row at `{y 991}` with its top edge on 1044 and the chooser row inside the viewport; **Read full description** re-applies the same rule to the grown drawer. The arithmetic is `MenuPanelLayout.RevealOffsetY`; the measurement and the write are the live `ScrollRect`'s, through `UiReveal`. `mods_ui scroll` is unchanged. There is **one action button per listing** and it is the row's own Install-column button (LIST4 item 1): the drawer head used to repeat the row's title, version and side badge and carry a second, identical Install button under them, which is what the player saw as two stacked buttons. The head is now a `CellRow` (`drawer:<key>`) with one muted cell — the install fact, `Restart required` or `No restart needed` — kept under that key because `widget_read` addresses the `action {…}` object by it; under it the planner reason when the action is refused, then for an installed or pending mod **Configure in Mods ►** (switches the strip to the Mods tab with that plugin selected, `ModsMenu.OpenInstalledFromInstall`), then the meta line `by <author> · released <date> · loads in <my client | the server (private games I host, or a dedicated server) | my client and the server | unknown until checked>` (from the side; a stale tag appends `· ⚠ <tag> — may be incompatible with the current patch`, a warning, never a block), then the README rows (README2 rendering, one row per block, capped at 6 000 characters with **Read full description** revealing the rest in place; this session's fetch renders the markdown per block, a README adopted from disk one row per line) and **More info ►** / **Less info ▼** flipping four facts in place: Dependencies, Package (`zip · <size>`), Update tag, Downloads. **The drawer repeats nothing the row above it already shows (LIST4 item B):** no title, version or side badge, and no second copy of the one-liner — the row's second line is the one place it appears — and a README block whose trimmed, whitespace-collapsed text equals the one-liner, the package name, the display name or the `<owner>-<package>` key is dropped (`InstallModsPane.IsDuplicateReadmeBlock`; a block that merely *starts* with one of those is the description and stays). The meta line stays. All four disclosure captions use the WGL4 pair `►` / `▼` from `InstallModsCopy.GlyphExpand` / `GlyphCollapse`, because U+25B8 and U+25BE drew as TMP's missing-glyph box live (`More info ☐`). The action reads **Install · restart** (or **Install** when the manifest declares `hotLoadable`), **Update · restart** for a newer listing than the installed one, **Installing…** while busy, **Cannot install** with the exact planner reason under it, **Installed · restart to load** for a pending write, **Installed** for a loaded (or otherwise present) mod, and the dashed **Tool — not a plugin**. One click on Install: the drawer opens, `IInstallCatalog.DownloadAndInspectAsync` runs with a page-lifetime token (a completion for another page or a released pane is dropped and logged once; failure returns to Install with the reason so a retry is a new call), then the plan: a blocked plan is **Cannot install** (never a generic "restart required", never the missing hot-load eligibility as the excuse); the target is the `[Install] DefaultTarget` setting's (INSTALLUX1 item 2 — the **Install to** chooser row `target:<key>` and its `client` / `server` / `both` cells are gone), and the confirmation that has been open since the click takes the player's **Yes**: the same rebuild turns the row's button into **Installed · restart to load** and raises the pending bar, and a write that *failed* leaves the reason row under the head with `action.error` set, so the box never disappears without saying what happened (CONF2: the click on `Install` is the intent, so there is no second **Confirm**; the approval is still bound to the plan hash, so changed bytes still refuse with `ApprovalInvalid`). **The write is to pending, except on the admitted hot-load path (HOT1, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified).** A plan whose decision is `ApplyNow` *and* whose admission is `ApplyNowEligible` *and* whose target is a root this process runs is executed by `HotLoadExecutor`: the approved image is written (temp file plus move, hash verified after the move) into `BepInEx/progenitor-reload/<pluginId>.dll` of this process and recorded in that root's `progenitor-install.json` as category `mod` under the same `package:<owner>-<name>@<version>` source the restart route uses, so `PluginReloader` loads it within a second and Remove takes it back through the ordinary ledger path. No pending copy is written on that path and no restart is owed; `ExecuteCallResult` reads back `{path, sha256, hotLoaded: true}`. Every other plan is unchanged: `Unknown` and `Blocked` admissions keep the restart and blocked routes, and a target this process does not run (a local-server folder that is a different process) keeps the `ApplyNow` decision with `executorAvailable: false`, writes that root's pending folder and says so (`the selected local-server folder is not this process, so it installs for the server's next start`). With the `Both` target on a client, the client half hot loads and the server half is pending (`serverPendingPath`). **Ordinary BepInEx packages install on the restart route (INSTORD1, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified).** A package without a `progenitor.json` is accepted when its archive layout is approved and its plugin identity was read from the bytes: the DLLs sit at the zip root or in one folder under `BepInEx/plugins/`, exactly one of them declares `[BepInPlugin]` (the plugin; the others are companions and must declare none), and nothing targets `patchers/`, `core/`, `config/` or any other non-DLL location Progenitor would have to place (a README, manifest, icon, changelog or license at the zip root is ignored, not installed). Anything else keeps its exact existing refusal. Identity comes from `MetadataReader` over the bytes - no assembly is loaded and a Thunderstore `{owner}-{name}` is never read as a plugin GUID - and `[BepInProcess]` gives `loadsIn` (`client` / `server` / `both` / `unknown`): a plugin that names a process the chosen target never runs is refused with that reason. `BepInEx-BepInExPack_V_Rising-*` is satisfied by the running BepInEx; every other Thunderstore dependency must already be on the target - a Progenitor-ledgered install of that package, or a loaded plugin whose GUID a prior inspection of *that package* recorded under `BepInEx/progenitor-cache/identity/<namespace>/<name>/<version>.json` - and is otherwise refused with `requires <namespace>-<name> - install it first`. **A missing dependency is installed with the package instead of refusing it (DEPS1, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified).** Install resolves the listing's dependency closure from the loaded index first (`DependencyClosure.cs`): each `<owner>-<name>-<version>` string recursively, dependencies first, at the index listing's own version — the listed one when it matches, otherwise the newest the index carries, which must still be at least the listed one (an index whose newest copy is *older* refuses and nothing is planned). The loader pack is never a member (it is what is running this process), and a dependency already satisfied on the target - a Progenitor-ledgered install of that package, or a loaded plugin whose GUID a prior inspection of that package recorded - is listed as `present` and is not walked, downloaded or planned again. A cycle, a package the index does not carry, or a malformed dependency string refuses with that named reason and resolves nothing. Every *missing* member is then downloaded, checked and planned with the same target as the package the player clicked - narrowed to the half its own `[BepInProcess]` runs, so a server-only library needed by a client mod is planned for the local server and the plan says so - and the whole set is refused if any member is (`Cannot install — <dep> refused: <reason>`), which is the only reason nothing is written. The confirmation body is one sentence each (`InstallSummary.Describe`): `Client mod by Modix, version 3.1.0. Loads at the next restart of V Rising. Needs ExampleCommandFramework 0.11.0, which will be installed too.` (or `… which is installed.` for a member already there). On confirm the members are written in order, dependencies first and the root last, each by its own decision (pending or hot); the first member that fails stops the set and names itself, and the members already written stay installed and ledgered because they are real installs. Readback: `plan.dependencies[{package, version, satisfiedBy: installed|loaded|closure, decision, reason}]`, `plan.closureCount` and `result.members[{package, version, path, hotLoaded, serverPendingPath}]`. **Remove** of a package another ledgered install lists as a dependency is refused with `removed.dependents[]` naming them, and goes through only when it is asked again with force; the dependents ride along on the forced result too, so the pane can say what was broken. The plugin DLL and its companions are written flat into `BepInEx/progenitor-pending/index/`, on the client root and the validated local-server root as the target says, and each file is recorded in that root's `BepInEx/progenitor-install.json` ledger (category `pending`, source `package:<namespace>-<name>@<version>`); Bootstrap's promote moves them into `BepInEx/progenitor-reload/` at the next start, never into `BepInEx/plugins/`. Such a plugin uses Harmony directly, so it is never hot swapped: it loads at the next start and unloads only at a restart, and the plan reason says so. **Remove** (or **Remove · restart to unload** when it is loaded) is the primary action on a Progenitor-ledgered install, one click like Install (CONF2), with the target chooser when both targets hold it; it deletes the ledgered pending and promoted copies for that package only (its companions included), drops those rows, and leaves the row `none` when nothing was loaded or `removed` ("still loaded until this process restarts") when it was. A hot-loaded package (its live copy is the `mod` row under `progenitor-reload/`) is the `none` case: the reloader unloads the plugin within a second, so the result carries `hotUnloaded: true` and nothing is owed a restart. Nothing outside the ledger is ever touched. **The install ledger survives the promote (INSTORD2, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified):** at catalog open and after every install or remove, each root's `progenitor-install.json` has every `pending` row whose file Bootstrap moved rewritten to its `progenitor-reload/` path as category `mod` (same source, sha and size) and every pending row with no promoted copy of those bytes dropped as promote-lost, and `InstalledState.Scan`/`Detect` read that ledger, so after a restart a promoted package still reads as Progenitor's — `Remove · restart to unload` when the inventory has it loaded, `Remove` with “installed; loads at the next start” (or “installed on the local server; loads at its next start” for the server root) when it does not, and `Update · restart` when the listing is newer than the ledger row's version — with `listing.ledger {client, server, version}`, `listing.files[{path, kind, root}]` and `action.identity {pluginGuid, pluginVersion, loadsIn, version}` (from `progenitor-cache/identity`, no download) readable at rest. A restart plan leaves the row pending (and the pending bar counts it); a hot-load plan with a live executor leaves it installed. READMEs are fetched for the rows on screen and on demand (TSTRAFFIC1; until then every build queued the whole index): `ResumePrefetch` on a build queues only the rows on screen whose README is not known — at most `InstallModsPane.ReadmePrefetchBudget` (the viewport's rows, capped at `MaxReadmePrefetchPerBuild` = 10) — one request in flight, ≥ 250 ms apart through `ReadmePrefetchScheduler`, canceled on pane release; a scroll queues nothing; every other README is fetched when its drawer opens. Each result re-filters in place and persists to `BepInEx/progenitor-cache/readme/<namespace>/<name>/<version>.txt` (plain text, first line `# released <stamp>`; a newer version or stamp in the index misses the cache and refetches); its progress is readback only (`descriptions {state, done, total}`); the toolbar does not show it (DESCCACHE1, requested 2026-09-29: "we shouldn't need to see that number at all in the UI"). The disk cache is checked per listing (`DiskChecked`), including listings that arrive after the first build and again right before a prefetch pass, so a cached README is never downloaded again on a later game start (before DESCCACHE1 a first build over a still-loading index marked the cache adopted for good; live 2026-09-29 the toolbar read `descriptions 49/299` with 310 README files on disk). Source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified. Icons come from `IconCache` (one fetch in flight, 100 ms apart, LRU) through Thunderstore's icon CDN (`ThunderstoreAdapter.TryBuildIconUrl`), requested for **the rows in the current scroll window** plus ten either side (`InstallModsPane.RequestIconWindow`; the window's first row comes from the list's own `ScrollRect` offset through `UiReveal.TryOffset`, and the runtime raises `UiReveal.Scrolled` after every wheel notch so a scroll asks for the new window — before INSTALLUX1 item 7 the request covered only the first `ViewportRows + IconLookahead` rows and ran only from `Build`/`RefilterListings`, which is why 126 of 149 rows read `icon.state missing` on a scrolled-to screen while `progenitor-cache/icons/` held 102 files, and the player saw black squares). Every row **outside** that window is asked through the disk lane alone (`IconCache.RequestFromDiskAsync`): a file already in the disk cache becomes `ready` with no fetch, and a disk miss retires the entry to `missing` rather than queuing a network request, so scrolling never floods the network lane. Icons are disk-cached under `BepInEx/progenitor-cache/icons/<sha256 of url>.png`, and painted in place when they arrive. Icon loading runs two lanes (ICON2): a URL already in that disk cache is decoded on its own lane and never waits behind a network fetch, each network fetch is abandoned after `IconCache.FetchTimeout` (15 s) so one hung request fails its own row instead of parking the queue, a failed URL is asked again on the next `RequestVisibleIcons` past `IconCache.RetryAfter` (30 s), and `ReleaseAll` retires the worker generation so the next request starts a fresh worker. `widget_read` rows keep the LIST1 cell-row shape (`kind: cellrow | cellheader`, `cells[{key, kind, text, tone, visible, rect}]`, `expanded`, `selected`, `rect`; an `input` cell reads back `{kind: input, text, placeholder}` with `text` its value — LIST3) and add: on a listing row `listing {id, state: none|installing|installed|pending|update|tool|blocked|removed, restart, side, loadsIn, label, reason, currentUpdate, searchScope, action {label, state, reason, enabled, restart, phase, progress, error, chooser, plan {decision, target, reason, hash, approved, pluginGuid, loadsIn, companions[], dependencies[{package, satisfiedBy}], ledger {client, server}}, target, result {path, sha256, alreadyPresent}}}` (`action` is null unless this is the opened listing); on the toolbar `count {shown, filtered, total}`, `descriptions {state, done, total}`, `sort`, `atDefaults` and `search`; the `mods_ui` geometry / `rects.detail` readback carries `layout: split | full-width` (full-width on the Install tab) and `contentWidth`; on the filter row `filters {side, noRestart, currentUpdate, currentUpdateTag, currentUpdateSource, atDefaults}`; on every drawer row `drawer: <id>` (the head also `scrolledTo {rowTop, viewportTop, viewportHeight}` — the last reveal's content-pixel geometry, null when nothing was measured: `viewportTop` is the offset the list holds, read back off the `ScrollRect` after the write, and `rowTop` is the opened row's distance down the content from the same read, so the two are equal whenever the reveal moved the row to the top and differ only when it had nothing to do (block already inside) or clamped to the content's end (last row). **Do not compare either against a row `rect` from the same readback:** the reveal measures after this build's rows are placed, but a `widget_read` / `rects` row rect can be sampled before that layout pass and then describes the intermediate layout rather than the screen — live a visibly correct reveal (row at the top of the list, drawer fully shown) reported `scrolledTo {rowTop 313, viewportTop 313}` beside that row's own rect at `{y 691}`, 300 px from where it was drawn. Read the rects on the next readback, or trust the capture — and `action {…}`, which since LIST4 is an **alias kept for one release**: the object's home is now `listing.action` on the row that carries the one button, null on a row that is not the opened one; the first README row `readme {state, chars, reason, blocks}`); on the pending bar `pending {count}`. `widget_click` addresses a row by its key, a button cell by `<rowKey>/<cellKey>` (`<key>/install` — `drawer:<key>/action` is gone with the head's button — `filters/side-server`, `filters/clear`, `header/name`, `toolbar/sort`, `prompt:<key>/promptYes` and `prompt:<key>/promptNo` — the confirmation's two buttons, which is where the chooser row's cells went) or by caption; `mods_ui rects` measures the drawer head as `header-title`, its restart note as `header-reason`, and every cell through the row's `cells[].rect`. `mods_ui hit` now resolves cell rows too (LIST4 item 2): every clickable cell of every live cell row whose rect contains the point is listed in `stripRowHit` as `<rowKey>/<cellKey>` and the topmost names `clickWouldInvoke` when the tab strip did not claim the point — before this the probe read only the tab strip's own rows, so a chooser chip the real mouse could click read back `clickWouldInvoke: null, why: no-hit` at its own rect center and that silence was never evidence about the pointer path.
- **Preferences (was Configuration):** *Three homes (2026-10-06) renamed and regrouped this tab, and the Server tab took the server's settings; [Where Progenitor's settings are drawn](#where-progenitors-settings-are-drawn) is authoritative where it differs from this paragraph, which records the earlier stages: the link row to Progenitor's page comes first, **Mods menu** and **Sources** are drawn with the mod page's row kit, the **Progenitor** group is gone, and the **Local dedicated server** rows below are unchanged.* The settings kit’s **left** alignment (center remains the default for the Mods overview and deny). Three sections — **Sources** (Use Thunderstore on the right of the row; Nexus is a dimmed “not yet supported” line), **Local dedicated server** (a note under the heading first — mods installed to the client also load in the private games this player hosts, server-side mods included, so a dedicated server is only a second install target (`ConfigurationPane.PrivateGameNote`, live-verified private-game staging 2026-09-12); then **Default install target** — a four-value dropdown bound to `[Install] DefaultTarget` (`Follow the mod's side` / `Both` / `My client` / `Local server`) that is the Install tab's whole answer to where a mod goes, INSTALLUX1 item 2; then the path field + Apply + Clear on one line, status under it — the value is normalized on load and on Apply (zero-width/BOM marks, whitespace, quotes stripped; `%VAR%` and a leading `~` expanded), a configured `DedicatedServerPath` is validated when the pane opens, and the status always reads `Validated: <canonical path>` or `Refused: <exact reason> Path: <path>`; Apply and Clear recompose the pane so the server-install rows follow (CONF1, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified) — and suggest-only Steam-library discovery with **Use this folder**, never auto-applied, and — for a validated folder — the **Install Progenitor on this server** toggle: on copies this client’s own `Progenitor.Bootstrap.dll` and `Progenitor.dll` into the server’s `BepInEx/plugins` and `BepInEx/progenitor-core`, verifies the copies by sha256, creates `progenitor-pending/` and `progenitor-reload/`, and writes the ledger `BepInEx/progenitor-install.json` (schema 1: every file Progenitor wrote there with path, sha256, size, category `framework`/`mod`/`pending`, source and timestamp); the status reads “Progenitor <version> installed (server needs a restart)”; re-running with identical hashes is a no-op and a different client image shows **Update** with both hashes. Server-target restart installs from the Install mods tab append their pending file to that ledger. Toggling off opens an in-pane prompt — “This removes Progenitor from the server AND every mod Progenitor installed there (N files: …). Mods installed by other tools are not touched. The server needs a restart.” — with **Remove** / **Cancel** — the pending prompt is keyed by the validated folder, so the recompose that rebuilds the pane (`ModsMenu.BuildConfigurationPanes` constructs a new pane) still shows it, and a click on the toggle is a flip of the real ledger state rather than of the kit’s value (SERVERINST2); Remove deletes ledger `mod` and `pending` files (and a promoted copy of the same bytes under `progenitor-reload/`), then `framework` files, then empty Progenitor folders, then the ledger, and reports any ledger file whose bytes changed and was left in place. Nothing outside the ledger is ever removed. The toggle is absent and the reason shown while `BepInEx/core` is missing, the folder is unwritable, or a `VRisingServer.exe` from inside that folder is running — while it runs the refusal reads “Stop the server first (Stop server below), then install or remove.” and the stop rows are built under it (SERVERINST3, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified): **Stop server** is the graceful route — it is taken only when Progenitor's ledger is in that folder, this client has a command route, and the folder's own `ServerHostSettings.json` (found from the launch record's `-persistentDataPath`, else under the install) names the port this client is currently connected to; and this client has completed a Progenitor handshake with it (`Handshake.LastClientResult == "ok"`, set only from a `PrhQ` Result packet, which nothing but a Progenitor server sends); it sends a `StopServerRequest {schema, nonce}` on Progenitor's own reserved event id `0x50727351` (`'PrsQ'`, `CustomEventPolicy.StopServerEventId`) rather than as chat text, and the server answers `StopServerReply {schema, nonce, accepted, reasonCode}` on the same id. The server re-reads the sender's `User` and authorizes it the way remote installs do (`ServerAdminList.AuthorizeShared`: on the admin list the game loads, `adminauth`, or the private game's host; F21, built and game-free tested, not live-verified), answers `not-admin` before touching the save path, and logs every decision as a `StopServer:` line (refused, accepted, already saving, save failed, a request it could not decode or could not answer); a request that arrives while an accepted stop is still saving is answered `saving` and starts nothing; on accept it replies `saving`, triggers the world save through `Progenitor.Persistence.WorldSaveTrigger`, and quits only once the save file's write stamp has advanced (polled off-frame; `save-timeout` after 15 s, and then it does **not** quit). The client walks `requested → replied → saving → stopped` and watches the process for 20 s; a named refusal (`not-admin`, `save-timeout`, `failed`) is reported as itself and never taken as a reason to kill. The admin-only built-in `.stop-server` is a second entry to the same path. No ledger, no route, no handshake, a port that is not this server's, or 20 s of actual silence ends at **Stop server (force)**, an in-pane prompt — heading `Stop the server process?`, body “This ends VRisingServer.exe running from &lt;folder&gt; without a save. Unsaved progress since the last autosave is lost.”, buttons **Stop** / **Cancel** — whose Stop is a `Kill` on the process matched by full executable path inside the validated folder (never by name, never a process outside it) followed by a ≤ 10 s wait for exit. Either stop that succeeds writes `BepInEx/progenitor-server-launch.json` (schema 1: exe, args, workingDirectory, stoppedUtc, by) from the live process — the argument vector and the working directory are read out of the target's own PEB (`NtQueryInformationProcess(ProcessBasicInformation)` → `PEB.ProcessParameters` → `RTL_USER_PROCESS_PARAMETERS.CommandLine` / `CurrentDirectory.DosPath` with `ReadProcessMemory`, x64 only, a handle opened `PROCESS_QUERY_LIMITED_INFORMATION | PROCESS_VM_READ`, never written to and never signaled); any failure there leaves an argument-less record and the exe's folder rather than a guess — and **Start server** — shown when the folder is validated, nothing runs from it and that record exists — replays exactly that exe, argument vector and working directory with no shell and no window changes, reporting `Started (pid N)` or `Refused: <reason>`; without a record the row reads `Start server unavailable — no launch record yet (stop it once through Progenitor, or start it yourself)`. `mods_ui widget_read` rows for the toggle, its status note, Update, the prompt, `Stop server`, `Stop server (force)`, `Start server` and the force prompt's heading/body/`Stop`/`Cancel` carry `serverInstall {state: not-installed|installed|update-available|refused, version, reason, ledgerFiles, promptOpen, line, process {running, pid, path, graceful, launchRecord, commandLine, connectedTo}, stop {state: idle|requested|replied|saving|stopped|failed|save-timeout|not-admin|force-prompt, reason, states}}` — the port this client is connected to comes from Bootstrap's record of the joined `GameHelper.LaunchGame` address (read through `BootstrapLink.LastConnectPort`, so it survives a Core reload), `connectedTo` reports it as `"<protocol>://<address>:<port>"`, a mismatch names both ports and a Bootstrap that predates the record says so instead (SERVERINST5, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified); `commandLine` is the arguments without the image token, values re-quoted, capped at 512 characters, and `states` is the whole sequence the last stop passed through (SERVERINST4, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified); `widget_click` on the toggle, the prompt buttons and the stop/start rows drives it (SERVERINST1, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified)), and **Progenitor** (**Progenitor settings**, and — MODS2 — **Uninstall Progenitor from this client**; settings organization moved the uninstall to Progenitor's page, Your settings · Private games, as **Remove Progenitor from this PC** [Uninstall] with the kit's confirmation dialog — [Where Progenitor's settings are drawn](#where-progenitors-settings-are-drawn); the request, the marker and the readback below are unchanged). That button opened an in-pane prompt in SERVERINST2's shape — heading `Remove Progenitor from this client?`, body `At the next start of V Rising, Progenitor removes itself, every mod it installed (N files: …), its ledger and caches. Mods installed by other tools and BepInEx stay. The game starts without Progenitor.`, buttons **Remove at next start** / **Cancel**. Nothing is deleted while the game runs, because the files are the ones this process loaded: **Remove at next start** writes `BepInEx/progenitor-uninstall.json` (`{schema: 1, requestedUtc, files[]}`, `Progenitor.Install.ClientUninstall`) naming the ledgered mod / pending / reload files, Core, Bootstrap, the dev bridge, the samples, the caches, the install ledger and the marker itself — paths relative to the BepInEx root, and never `core/`, never a `plugins/` file the ledger does not name, never anything outside `BepInEx/`. **Cancel** deletes the request. Bootstrap honors it at the next start, before Core loads (see [hot-reload.md](hot-reload.md)). While a request is on disk the pane shows the request line and a **Cancel** button. `mods_ui widget_read` rows for the button, its note and the prompt carry `progenitorUninstall {requested, requestedUtc, files, promptOpen, line}` (MODS2, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified). Section headings are larger/bolder with a separator. Keys such as `[Mods] UseThunderstore` are not shown. No installed-mod sidebar. Optional left list is settings groups (Mods menu, Sources, Local server), not a catalog. Start replays the launch record with the inherited `DOORSTOP_*` variables removed from the child's environment, so BepInEx injects in the new server process (live-verified 2026-09-12: Stop, record, Start on the isolated test server).

A thrown page builder disposes even a partial pane (its widgets can hold plugin delegates) and shows a recovery page with **Back to Mods**.

### Clearing a search (SEARCHCLEAR1)

Requested 2026-10-09: text typed for something else landed in the Mods search and left a stale filter ("update", then **No matching mods**). The Mods search (sidebar) and the Install tab's search behave the same way:

| Action | Result |
|---|---|
| Click the **×** at the right of the box | Shown only while the box has text, in the spot where the empty box draws its search mark. It empties the box, shows every row at once (the Install list does not wait for the typing pause), and keeps focus in the box with the caret at the start. |
| **Escape** in a focused box with text | Clears it; the workspace stays. |
| **Escape** in a focused empty box | Leaves the box (focus goes, as a click outside it does; the menu hotkeys work again); the workspace stays. |
| **Escape** with no box focused | Closes the workspace, as before. |
| Close the workspace (Close Mods, the shortcut, another game tab, or an Escape that hides it) | Both searches are emptied, so the next open starts unfiltered. |
| Switch tabs or mod pages while the workspace stays open | Both searches are kept. |

**Remembered Install filters.** **Remember Install filters and sort** stores filters and sort only. The search text is never remembered (`InstallFilterMemory` has no search part), so a remembered session cannot reopen on a stale search.

**How it is built.** The × is the kit's clear glyph (`MenuRowStyle.SearchClearGlyph`, U+00D7, the Keybinds tab's Unbind mark; `x` when the font lacks it) in the search mark's color. It brightens under the pointer and darkens while pressed, using the Quiet skin's 1.3 and 0.7 factors. Its click target is the full 28-unit height of the box and 28 units wide, and the typed text stops short of it. It is a child of the field named `search-clear`. It has no `Button`, no raycast target and no Bootstrap slot. The shown-input pump owns clicks on fields: `FocusFieldAt` tests the × before the field, and the pump sets the tint each frame. A click empties the box through the owned edit path, so the pane's value-changed handler re-filters exactly as typing does. An Install input cell opts in with `CellSpec.Clearable`. Escape is the existing focused-field handling (Bootstrap's 27/17 hook and the pump's Escape edge through `WorkspaceInputPolicy`), with the new stage `LeaveSearch` between `ClearSearch` and `Close`. No key is polled outside the framework input files. While the Install tab is shown, a status read no longer copies the parked Mods search into the Install search. That bug showed the Mods text in the Install box and undid typing there. It was found live during this work and fixed.

**Bridge.** `mods_ui widget_click {"label":"search-clear"}` presses the shown box's × through the click's action (refused while the box is empty). `status.focus.searchClear` and `widget_read.searchClear` read `{field: mods-search|install-search|none, text, focused, shown, glyph, rect, tint: rest|hover|pressed, presses}`. Escape: `mods_ui field_focus {"label":"search"}` (the box shown now, on either tab), `type {"text":"…"}`, then `field_cancel` (one Escape through `Ui.OnBootstrapKey(27, then 17)`). The log has `Ui: search-clear via=…`, `Ui: search-escape branch=clear|leave|close …` and `Ui: search-escape left field=…`.

**Status.** Built and game-free tested (`SearchClearTests`, `ModsMenuTests` Escape and reset cases, `InstallListPolishTests` Install cases, the updated `WorkspaceHostsParityTests` / `UiTextEditTests` / `ModsTabsTests`). **Verified live** 2026-10-09 on the Steam client at the title screen (3840×1080, Core 2026.10.143.5: this branch merged with the pending mod-page branch, hot swapped; Bootstrap unchanged), through the bridge only (handler path, no OS input). Results:

- Mods search: the × shows while the box has text (28×28). `widget_click search-clear` emptied "update" and showed every row with focus kept.
- Escape on "blood" cleared it. A second Escape left the box (`focus.field none`) with the workspace open. A third closed it.
- Close and reopen started empty (`reused-parked` list, empty box). An Escape that hid the workspace with "zzz" in the box reopened empty (`reused-hidden`).
- Preferences and back kept "blood".
- Install search: the × filtered 14 of 309 rows for "update" and emptied the box back to 42 shown with focus kept. Escape cleared, then left. Escape-hide and Close both reopened empty.

Not verified live: the pointer's own click and the hover and press tint (no real mouse was moved), and in-world (FullscreenMenu) hosting.


## Mod page layout (MODPAGE2, 2026-10-09)

**Status: built, game-free tested (`ModPageLayoutTests`, `ModPageTests`, `ModPageUpdatesControlTests` and the updated settings page suites) and verified live on the Steam client at 3840x1080 on 2026-10-09.** Feedback 2026-10-09: "the button locations need a revisit" and "letters have no padding on the margins"; the layout was approved on an in-game prototype the same day. One path draws every mod page (Progenitor's own, a plugin with settings, a mod another tool installed); source `ModPagePane.Layout.cs` (`ModPageSpacing`, `ModPageDetails`).

Top to bottom:

1. **Title** and `by author · one-liner` (a mod's own header content replaces both; a header that throws falls back to them).
2. **Actions bar** (`page-framework`): the switch, status word, version and conflicts at the left; the action (Uninstall, Reactivate, Cancel install) at the right, in its tone. Each asks first in a framework dialog. The Uninstall and Cancel install prompts are destructive (DANGERENTER1, 2026-10-10; source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified): keyboard Enter does not answer them, their **Yes** is drawn in the danger tone and needs a click (with a controller: right, then A). Enter still answers the Reactivate prompt. See [ui.md](ui.md#behavior).
3. The attention banner (`page-warning`) and the problem lines, directly under the bar.
4. **Details** (`page-details`): one grid of rows on the settings grid's columns, beside the description column, which is always shown with it. Rows, each drawn only when it applies: Installs on (the sides chip), Client and Server (the per-side words; Server also carries the joined server's evidence and is rewritten in place), Released, Store page (**Store page**, **Share in chat**), Pages (the mod's custom pages), Managed by (the provenance's first sentence; the whole block in the column), Update (`1.0.0 → 1.4.0` with **Open in Install Mods**), Last action, Kept versions (with **Restore**), **Updates** (the game's dropdown bar; see [auto-update.md](auto-update.md)) with the **Preferences » Updates** link under it, Previous version (**Roll back to …**), Failed versions (**Clear failed versions**), and Settings (`None here. Set it up the way its author describes.`) on a mod with no Progenitor settings. Pointing at a row writes what it is into the column (heading the row's name); pointing at an option of the open Updates list writes that option's description; leaving restores the column's rest text (the settings view's own, or the mod's name and `Point at a row to read what it is.`). Readback: each row's `page-fact {name, description}`; `page-details {column {heading, body}, factsWidth, x1, margin}`.
5. **Settings**, only when the mod has Progenitor settings: the section heading, then the settings view (its header row keeps the search and **Reset all**).
6. **About**: the README of the listing this install came from (this side's ledger row or the package manifest names the package at the listing's version), else the plugin's own text (embedded `README.md`, the README beside the DLL, `[ModAbout]`, its description), or `no description available`; `page-readme.source` says which ([About text on the mod page](modules.md#about-text-on-the-mod-page); source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified).
7. **For developers** (C5), only for a Progenitor plugin loaded on this side (it has a plugin registry entry): see [Works across versions and For developers](#works-across-versions-and-for-developers-c5).

Spacing rules. Every length comes from the settings grid the Preferences rows use (`ModSettingsRowGrid`) or the game's Options numbers (`GameSettingsLook`, game units × 70/58). Kit px at 3840x1080, canvas scale 1:

| Rule | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Page margin: every text line (title, by-line, section headings, scope headings, details names, banner, About, the Updates link) starts this far in from the content's left edge | 19 (the game's 16 units) | `ModSettingsRowGrid.NameInset`, the Preferences row name inset; measured on both pages against the same line |
| Details row pitch; text vertically centered | 70 (the game's 58-unit entry): drawn 62 tall plus the panel's 8 gap | `ModSettingsRowGrid.RowPitch`, `PanelLayout.RowGap` |
| Value/control column x1 | the grid's `ControlLeft` (45 % of the usable width beside the description column), the same x as the Updates dropdown and every settings bar | `ModSettingsRowGrid.For(ModSettingsDescriptionLayout.RowsWidth(content)).ControlLeft` |
| Text values | start 12 right of x1 (the game's 10 units), where a dropdown bar's caption starts | `GameSettingsLook.DropdownCaptionLeft` |
| Name column | ends 8 before x1 | `CellLayout.Gap` (`SettingsAlign.InlineGap`) |
| Values and controls | end at the grid's bar edge, 116 (the game's 96 units) in from the usable right | `ModSettingsRowGrid.BarRight` |
| Gap between buttons in one group | 8 | `SettingsAlign.InlineGap` |
| Section headings (Details, Settings, About) | 24, 16 above, the game's category underline | `SettingsAlign.HeadingExtraTop`, `CellRowSkin.CategoryHeading` |
| Details names | one color, the settings grid's resting name gray | `CellTone.SettingName` = `GameSettingsLook.NameGrey(false)` |
| Actions bar | 48 tall band across the content; the switch starts one margin in from its left, the last action ends one margin in from its right | `ModFrameworkLine.BarHeight`, `ModPageSpacing.ActionsRightInset` |
| About text | at the margin, wrapped at 820 or the content width, whichever is less | `ModPageSpacing.ReadableWidth` |

A cell row's first cell starts at the row's edge (`CellRowSpec.Indent` moves only a joined row's seam) and its caption draws 2 px inside the cell (measured live), so the margin is a first cell `Margin - Gap - 2` wide (`ModPageSpacing.Lead`); a text link's caption sits 6 px inside its button cell, so the Updates link's margin cell is 6 narrower.

### Works across versions and For developers (C5)

**Status: built, game-free tested (`ModCompatDetailsTests`) and verified live in part (2026-10-10, [results](mod-compatibility.md#c5-live-test-2026-10-10)): the page row, For developers and the Server's copy passed. The list marker is now an icon after the version (2026-10-10, user choice after the live test showed the text marker never fit the list): built and game-free tested only, not live-verified.** Mod compatibility C5 ([design](mod-compatibility.md#install-and-mods-pages)). Words and decisions: `ModCompatDetails.cs` (`ModAcrossVersions`, `ModDevDetails`); evidence: `ModsMenu.Compat.cs`.

**Which mods are marked.** A mod that declares `Peers` on `[NetworkCompatibility]` whose range admits a release other than its own version (`*`, `>=1.2.0 <2.0.0`, `1.2.x`, alternatives). No marker for an undeclared mod (its default same minor), `=1.2.0`, or a range that holds only its own version (prereleases are not counted). The range is the loaded copy's attribute when its version is the one shown; otherwise, for an installed copy that is not loaded, its verified inspection (`progenitor-cache/verified`, the ledger's package at that version, when its `progenitor.json` names this plugin). Nothing is shown from a guess.

**Mods list.** The marker is a small loop-arrows icon (two arcs, each ending in an arrowhead; option A of the marker mockup, chosen 2026-10-10) right after the version on the plain `version · sides` second line, never on a problem or state line: `1.3.0 ⟲ · Client and server`. It replaced the text marker (` · Works across versions` / ` · Across versions`), which the live test showed never fits at the normal list width.

- *Drawing.* A procedural texture like the title screen's zip glyph (`LoopGlyph`: 96 × 80, the glyph's own bounding box, white on transparent, 4 × 4 supersampled), made once per Core generation and destroyed on Core Uninstall (`UnityUiRuntime.ReleaseLoopGlyph`). The Image tints it the second line's muted color (`MenuPalette.Muted`). Drawn 13.2 × 11 px (`CellLayout.LoopGlyphHeight`, at the glyph's aspect), centered vertically on the second line (measured from the drawn text; 11.5 px below the cell's middle when it cannot be measured).
- *Placement.* An image cell (`ModsListLayout.CellAcross`, `CellSkin.LoopGlyph`) overlaid on the name cell (`OverlayOf`) and drawn inline (`CellSpec.InlineAfter` = the version's length): 17.2 px wide (`AcrossIconWidth`: a 4 px space, then the glyph at the cell's right end). The second line keeps that room after the version (a TMP `<space>`), so the rest of the line, ` · Client and server`, moves right. The runtime measures the version's drawn width with a hidden measuring label (the caret measurer's rule) and places the icon there; the column model's estimate (`CellLayout.InlinePlacement`: the name cell's left, the 2 px caption inset and the version's estimated width) is the fallback and the game-free answer.
- *Clipping.* The line is clipped as before (`ClipLabel` at 14 px within the name cell less 4), less the icon's room, so the sides still end in an ellipsis, that much sooner (`CellLayout.InlineNoteLine`). When even the version would be cut with the room taken out, there is no icon and the line is clipped as it was without one.
- *The Update tag.* The icon is an overlay: it takes no slot, and the name cell is sized before it (`ModsListLayout.NameWidth`: the row less the diamond, the Update tag and their gaps), so it never moves or covers the Update tag.
- *Hover.* Pointing at the icon writes **Works across versions** over the help text into the open mod page's description column, and leaving it puts the column back (`ModsMenu.HoverAcrossIcon`): the workspace's hover text lives in that column (the page's Details rows, the Keybinds tab); the kit has no floating tooltip box. The text, shared with the page row: *The author says this mod works with its other versions in a declared range, so a server and a player don't need the exact same version. The server's version matching decides at the join.* The icon is a hover-only cell (`CellSpec.Hover` on an image cell, `UiCellRowHandle.IsHoverOnly`): it gets a pointer slot but no click of its own, and a press on it is passed to the row, so it still opens the mod.

**Mod page.** A Details row **Works across versions**, after Installs on / Client / Server and before Released, with the range in words as its value (`Works with 1.2.0 or newer, below 2.0.0`); pointing at it writes what it means into the column (the list icon's text). It is an ordinary Details row (`FactRow`: name at the margin, value at x1 + 12); its value cell is capped at the bar edge (`ModPageSpacing.TextValueWidth`) and ends in an ellipsis when longer.

**For developers.** The page's last section, after About: it is for mod authors, so it never pushes Details, Settings or About down. Collapsed by default; the expanded state is kept per mod while the workspace is open and forgotten when it closes. The heading is a page section heading (24 px at the margin, the category underline, 16 above, like Details and About) with the list's fold marker (`CellKind.Chevron`, turning when expanded) right after the title rather than at the far right, where a marker did not read as a fold (the settings drawers, 2026-10-06). The whole heading takes the click and redraws the page in place, keeping the scroll position (`RebuildModKeepingScroll`). Expanded, its rows are Details grid rows, each described in the column (a long value is repeated there whole). The values are read when the page is drawn; a later offer, message or server table shows on the next redraw:

| Row | Value |
|---|---|
| Declared range | `>=1.2.0 <2.0.0 · 1.2.0 or newer, below 2.0.0`; `None declared (works with 1.4.x by default)`; `None declared (exactly 1.4.0, by its Exact rule)`; `"…" is not a valid range and is ignored (…)` |
| Network | `ServerMustHave · Exact`, `NoNeedForSync · rule not declared`, or `Not declared` |
| Features | offered features with levels, `hello 2 · notes 1`, or `None offered` |
| Messages | per message, `hello reads v2 · sent v2 (min reader 1)` (`no handler`, `not sent`), joined by `; `, or `None registered`. Only what this Core generation registered or sent is known |
| Server's copy | while connected and the server's feature table is known: `1.2.0 · hello 2`, `1.2.0 · no features`, or `Not listed by the server` |

**Bridge.** `mods_ui hover {label, cell, leave?}` puts the pointer on (or, with `leave: true`, off) a hover cell of a visible cell row through the cell's own pointer slot: `label` is the row's key (`mod:<id>`), `cell` the cell's key (`across`). It answers `{label, cell, entered, column {heading, body}}`, the open page's description column. Without `cell`, `hover` is the option hover (OPTIONHOVER1).

**Readback.** List entry (`modsListEntry`): `acrossVersions`, `range` (the declared range when marked), `acrossVersionsShown` (`icon` when the icon is drawn, else `none`), `acrossIconAfter` (the text the icon follows, the version; empty when none) and `secondLineDrawn` (the line's text as drawn, clipped with the icon's room; the icon is not a character of it); the entry row's `cells` list the icon as `{key: across, kind: image, skin: loop-glyph, overlayOf: name, inlineAfter, hover: true}`; `mods_ui status` `entries[]` adds `acrossVersions`. Page: `page-compat {acrossVersions, range, words}` and `page-fact {name, description}` on the row; `page-dev {heading, expanded, rows[{key, name, value}]}` on the heading (rows listed even while collapsed); `page-dev-row {key, name, value}` and `page-fact` on each expanded row. `widget_click pane=content label=page-section-dev` toggles the section.

**Acceptance procedure — steps 2 to 4 run 2026-10-10** (results in [mod-compatibility.md](mod-compatibility.md#c5-live-test-2026-10-10)); step 1 was rewritten for the icon and has not been run. On the Steam client with a test plugin declaring `Peers` (for example `Progenitor.CompatTest` built with `-p:CompatTestVariant=Range`) and one declaring nothing (`-p:CompatTestVariant=Default`):

1. `mods_ui widget_read pane=list`: the Range plugin's entry reads `acrossVersions: true`, `range` its `Peers`, `acrossVersionsShown: icon`, `acrossIconAfter` its version, and its row's `across` cell has a `rect` that starts just right of the version and ends left of the Update tag (if any); the Default plugin reads `acrossVersions: false`, `acrossVersionsShown: none` and has no `across` cell. Capture the list (focus-free): the muted loop-arrows icon sits right after the version, vertically centered on the second line, about as tall as its digits; ` · Client and server` follows it (ellipsized as before when narrow), nothing overlaps, and the Update tag is in its place. `mods_ui hover {label: mod:<id>, cell: across}` answers `column {heading: Works across versions, body: <the help text>}`, and `widget_read pane=content` shows the same in `page-details.column`; `hover {label, cell, leave: true}` puts the rest text back. Then, only with the user's OK for a foreground pointer test, a real hover shows the same, and a click on the icon opens that mod.
2. Open the Range plugin's page: `page-compat.words` reads `Works with …`; capture the Details grid: the row's name at the margin and its value at x1 like the rows around it.
3. `page-dev.expanded` is false; `widget_click pane=content label=page-section-dev`, then `page-dev.expanded` is true and the `page-dev-row` values match the plugin (features, messages after it sent one); capture the heading's turned marker and the rows. Open another mod and come back: still expanded. Close and reopen the workspace: collapsed.
4. Joined to the isolated test server running the plugin: the `Server's copy` row shows the server's version and features.

## Updates (AUTOUPDATE1, AUTOUPDATE2)

The title screen shows a short line under MODS in the game's own subtitle style (`2 UPDATES AVAILABLE`, `1 MOD UPDATED`, `UPDATE FAILED`; nothing otherwise), and the Install tab shows an update list with **Update all**, **Update** and **Keep this version** above its column header, plus a line saying what Automatic mode did. A Progenitor-installed mod's page has **Updates** (Global / Automatic / Notify / Off / Keep), **Roll back** and its failed versions. Built and game-free tested; the contract and the live results are in [auto-update.md](auto-update.md#where-updates-show).

## Thunderstore traffic (TSTRAFFIC1)

Decided 2026-10-08: cut Progenitor's requests to Thunderstore. **Built and game-free tested (`ThunderstoreTrafficTests`, `InstallListViewTests.Build_prefetches_readmes_for_the_rows_on_screen_only_never_the_whole_index`), not live-verified.**

**Measured 2026-10-08** (curl against `https://thunderstore.io/c/v-rising/api/v1/package/`): the index body is 1,080,986 bytes; 140,911 bytes with gzip; a conditional request whose `If-Modified-Since` equals the response's `Last-Modified` answers **304 with no body**. The response carries `last-modified`, `cache-control: max-age=30` and `vary: accept-encoding`, and no `ETag`. Thunderstore publishes no rate limit or polling interval.

**Index checks are conditional and compressed.** Every index read sends `Accept-Encoding: gzip` (not `deflate`, whose zlib-wrapped and raw forms servers disagree on, and not Brotli; any other encoding in a response is refused) and, when a cached copy exists, `If-Modified-Since` (and `If-None-Match` if an `ETag` was ever sent). `CatalogFetcher` decodes the body itself; the size cap (`CatalogFetcher.IndexMaxBytes`, 32 MiB) counts the **decoded** bytes, so a small body that inflates past it fails with `the download exceeded the size ceiling`. A 304 to a request without validators is an error. The check runs off the game's main thread. The cache is `BepInEx/progenitor-cache/`:

| File | Content |
|---|---|
| `index.json` | The last index body, UTF-8 without a BOM. |
| `index.stamp` | When it was last fetched **or confirmed current by a 304** (ISO-8601 UTC); the 30-minute open rule reads it. Format unchanged, so an older Core still reads it. |
| `index.validators` | `url:` the body came from, `last-modified:`, `etag:` (when sent) and `sha256:` of the body bytes as written. The validators are sent only when the URL equals the request's and the hash equals the file on disk; a body replaced by anyone else (or re-saved with a BOM) goes out unconditional once. |

These names belong to the default index URL. Any other index URL (a server's `[RemoteInstall] ThunderstoreBaseUrl` mirror) keeps its own `index-<12 hex of the URL's SHA-256>.json` / `.stamp` / `.validators`, so a mirror and thunderstore.io never share or overwrite a copy. A 200 replaces all three (only when the body is a JSON array), each through a temp file moved over the old one; a 304 re-stamps the copy and hands the cached body on. A server keeps the same files under its own BepInEx folder.

**Request schedule.** Nothing polls on a timer.

| Who | When it checks the index | Interval |
|---|---|---|
| Install tab (client, `InstallIndexRefresher`) | First Mods workspace open per session; a later open only when the copy on show is older than 30 min; **Retry** on the error surface | 30 min (unchanged) |
| Join screens (client, `JoinPackageIndex`) | When the screens open and the last read is older than 10 min | 10 min (unchanged), now conditional |
| Install-window availability (server, `ServerPackageAvailability`) | When the catalog is re-signed, on a refusal, or for a package not read yet, once the last read is older than 30 min | **30 min** (was 10) |
| Remote install (server, `RemoteInstallService`) | **Every** install or update an admin starts; an uninstall reuses a read for 30 min | **30 min** for uninstall (was 10) |

**Failure backoff.** After 3 failed checks in a row, an owner (each row above is one) skips automatic checks for 5 min, doubling per further failure up to 60 min; a success clears it. The pane's **Retry** and an admin's install still go out. 429 `Retry-After` is honored inside each request as before (at most 3 retries, hints under 2 min).

**READMEs; no changelogs.** A list build fetches only the READMEs of the rows on screen (at most 10, see the Install list above); any other README is fetched when its drawer opens. Thunderstore **changelogs are not requested at all**: nothing displays them, so a listing's document set reads the changelog as unavailable (`the changelog is not fetched`); a local listing's changelog still comes from its own zip. README requests ask for gzip. Under `progenitor-cache/readme/<namespace>/<name>/`: `<version>.md` (README markdown), keyed by the listing's release stamp like the `.txt`; a README 404 (or 410) is remembered in `<version>.readme.missing` for **24 hours or until the release stamp changes**, so it is not asked again on the next game start. A 5xx, timeout or cancellation is not remembered.

**One HTTP client.** The Install tab, the join screens and the server's availability read share one `HttpClient` (`SharedCatalogHttp`, ref-counted leases: the join screens release theirs on teardown and the server's read with its reader; the Mods menu holds one lease per Core generation and reuses it when its catalog is rebuilt). The remote installer keeps its own handler (its connect timeout follows `[RemoteInstall] ConnectTimeoutSeconds`). The User-Agent is unchanged: `InstallReasons.UserAgent` (Progenitor and its version).

**Log lines** (BepInEx log of the process that asked; one per network request):

| Line | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `Thunderstore: index check (<owner>, conditional) HTTP 304 not modified, 0 body bytes; reused the cached copy (N chars).` | Index unchanged; no body crossed the network. |
| `Thunderstore: index check (<owner>, <conditional\|unconditional>) HTTP 200, W bytes on the wire (gzip), D decoded; cached; Last-Modified <date>.` | A new or changed index. |
| `Thunderstore: index check (<owner>, …) failed (HTTP s): <reason>; n in a row[; automatic checks paused until HH:mm UTC].` | A failed check. |
| `Thunderstore: index check (<owner>) skipped: … paused after n failures in a row …` | A backoff skip (no request). |
| `Thunderstore: <ns>-<name> <version> readme HTTP 200, W bytes on the wire (gzip), D decoded.` / `… failed (HTTP 404): …; remembered for 24 h or until a new release.` | One README request. |

`<owner>` is `install tab`, `join`, `server availability` or `remote install`. Icon fetches are not logged here (they are disk-cached forever per URL).

### Traffic acceptance procedure (run 2026-10-08)

On the test client with the Mods workspace closed, note the last line of `BepInEx/LogOutput.log`, then count only lines starting `Thunderstore:` after it.

1. **First Install-tab open of a session.** Open the Mods workspace and the Install tab. Expect exactly one `index check (install tab, …)` line: `conditional … HTTP 304 … 0 body bytes` when `progenitor-cache/index.validators` exists, otherwise `unconditional … HTTP 200, ~141000 bytes on the wire (gzip), ~1081000 decoded`. Expect at most 10 `readme` lines (none for rows already cached) and no `changelog` line and no further `readme` lines while the tab stays open or scrolls.
2. **Reopen.** Close and reopen within 30 minutes: no `index check` line. After 30 minutes: one `conditional … HTTP 304` line.
3. **Join.** Join a server that names a Thunderstore package: one `index check (join, conditional) HTTP 304` line (or none when the join screens read within the last 10 minutes).
4. **Server join.** On the test server's log, a player join that opens the install window: at most one `index check (server availability, …)` line per 30 minutes.
5. **Drawer.** Open a listing whose README is not cached: one `readme` line and no `changelog` line; reopen it after a game restart: none.

**Live results (2026-10-08, Steam client and the isolated test server, Core from this branch).**

- **Step 1.** With no validators file yet: `index check (install tab, unconditional) HTTP 200, 140901 bytes on the wire (gzip), 1080987 decoded; cached`. After a Core swap: `conditional … HTTP 304 not modified, 0 body bytes`. No `readme` lines: the rows on screen were already cached.
- **Step 2.** Close and reopen within 30 minutes: no request.
- **Step 3.** Joining the test server: the join screens had nothing to show (`JoinFlow: nothing to show on this join`), so no `join` check was made. A join that opens the screens has not been run.
- **Step 4.** That join made no `server availability` check on the server (nothing to check). Not run with a missing package.
- **Step 5.** `<a third-party command library> 0.11.0 readme HTTP 200, 4453 bytes on the wire (gzip), 11576 decoded`, and no changelog request.

**Two defects found and fixed in the run.**
- **The first open made no check.** The open queued the check, and the tab select right after it queued another. The second call canceled the first one's token but was handed the same running task, so the only request was canceled before it was sent, and nothing was logged. A load now joins a check in flight instead of canceling it.
- **The second extra check.** Joining or leaving a local server rebuilds the catalog. The rebuilt refresher forgot this session's check, and the rebuilt pane queued a load without asking whether a check was due. The rebuilt refresher now inherits the session (`InheritSession`; test `Index_refresher_rebuilt_with_the_catalog_keeps_the_session_check`). A rebuilt pane is served the cached index when no check is due. Live: leaving the server and reopening the Install tab made no request.

## Install view: Simple and Advanced (INSTALLSIMPLE1)

Status: **source-checked, built (Debug and Release) and game-free tested.** Tests: `InstallSimpleViewTests`. Decided 2026-10-04.

**Live (2026-10-04, Steam client 3840x1080 joined to the isolated test server as its admin, Core 2026.10.63–66 from this branch).**
- **Default view.** Simple was the default, with the chips All 132 / Installed 4 / Updates 0.
- **Columns.** After the follow-ups they read Mod, Released ▼, Downloads, Version, Status, then the button at the right.
- **Targets matched the rule.** Client mods acted on this PC. Server-only mods (listed because the player was admin) acted on the server. HotDemo, which this PC had, read `! Missing on server`.
- **Install.** A bridge click and a real mouse click on a third-party mod's Install both opened "Install <mod> <version>?" (restart note). Dismissed with No.
- **View switch.** The switch persisted `[Mods] InstallView` (Advanced, then Simple), and Advanced drew today's screen.
- **Players joining.** The button left the Install toolbar. On Progenitor's mod page (`page overview`), Server settings showed **Players joining this server** with the log line `JoinAdmin: Players joining row considered: shown True (serverSupports True, helloAdmin True, remote-install status authorized)`, and a real click on **Open** opened the popup.

Not live-verified: a both-sides Install or Uninstall, the narrow-pane drops, and the non-admin case.

The Install mods tab has two views, picked by a two-part switch, **Simple | Advanced**, at the left of the search row (the kit's chip buttons; the current view on the gold selected-chip face). **Simple is the default.** The choice is remembered in `[Mods] InstallView` (`Simple` or `Advanced`, `BepInEx/config/progenitor.cfg`, personal to this BepInEx profile); an empty or unknown value reads Simple. **Advanced** is the per-side screen described in the sections below, unchanged.

**What Simple shows.** One row per mod, left to right: icon, **Mod** (the name over `one-liner · owner`, with the update note leading the second line as in Advanced), **Released**, **Downloads**, **Version**, **Status** and one button at the far right, the status words immediately left of the button (keys in order: `expand`, `icon`, `name`, `released`, `downloads`, `version`, `installStatus`, `install`). Released and Downloads are Advanced's own cells, formats, widths and header captions. Hidden: the Client and Server columns, the server picker, the Side and Only filter groups, and the Author and Installed-date columns. Kept: the search field, the drawer (expand chevron, description, **Read full description**) and the pending bar. One filter row holds the Show chips **All N**, **Installed N** and **Updates N** (single choice; **Updates** takes the amber attention rim while N > 0).

**Sort and narrow panes.** As in Advanced: the default is Released, newest first; a header click sorts a new column in its natural direction (Mod A to Z, Released newest first, Downloads most first) and a second click on the sorted column reverses it; listings with no date or count sort last. Version and Status do not sort. On a pane narrower than the row needs, Released and then Downloads drop (Advanced's `InstallColumns.DropPriority`) before Mod shrinks below its minimum, the active sort column never drops, and Status and the button never drop. Simple's Show choice and sort are its own; Advanced's filters and sort are untouched by them.

**Server context in Simple view.** A dedicated row below search shows the selected server name and connection/management state. It reads **Local management** or **Remote management** only on that route; a connected server with no usable route names the reason instead (for example **Server doesn't run Progenitor**, **Server has remote installs off** or **Checking what this server allows…**). While connected, **Server settings »** opens the Server tab for admins and read-only users; edit permissions are unchanged. Its widget address is `simple-server/server-settings`. A visible explanation describes destination selection from requirements and installed copies, points to the confirmation for exact changes, and directs per-side control to Advanced. The existing `serverSettingsLink` readback reports availability. Verified with the test client at 1920x1080 and 3840x1080 on the 2026-10-15 build, before the port to current main; the link opened the Server tab. The port is built and game-free tested only.

**Server settings header.** The Server tab pins the full server name and connection, management and access status above its settings; the management part follows the same rule as Simple view. Names wrap instead of being shortened in the middle. The description column starts with **Setting help** and explains a setting only after hover or selection. Connected admin layout verified at 1920x1080 and 3840x1080 on the 2026-10-15 build before the port (the port is game-free tested only); other resolutions and read-only appearance were not visually checked in this batch.

**What Simple leaves to Advanced.** Libraries, tools and listings that are not installable mods. Libraries still install automatically as a mod's dependency.
- *Tools and non-mods*: the existing rule (`InstallModsPane.IsTool`): the Thunderstore `Tools` tag, a known mod-manager app, or a package over the download limit ([FIX-NONMODS](#listings-that-are-not-installable-mods-fix-nonmods)). The loader and Progenitor's own listings are hidden in both views.
- *Libraries* (`InstallLibraries`): V Rising's Thunderstore community has no `Libraries` category (the cached index of 2026-10-04 carries only `Mods`, `Server`, `Client`, `Tools` and the update tags). A listing is a library when it is tagged `Libraries` or `Library`, or when another listing in the index depends on it **and** its one-liner calls it a framework, library or API (whole words). In today's index that is, for example, command frameworks and UI libraries. A depended-on mod that does not describe itself so (a gameplay overhaul, a camera mod) stays listed.
- *Other game updates*: like Advanced's default `Current game update` filter, Simple lists only listings tagged for the current update (when it is known). A mod with a copy on a side Simple acts on stays listed, so it can be updated or removed.

**Where the button acts** (`InstallModsPane.SimpleActsOnServer`). Simple points the per-side decision at the connected server, never at the Advanced picker's choice:

| Situation | The button acts on |
|---|---|
| Not connected | This PC only |
| Connected, not that server's admin (the route is `none`, e.g. `not-admin`) | This PC only |
| Connected and the existing Server target's route to that server is open (the **remote** route, which the server grants only to an authorized admin, or the **local-folder** route when the joined server is this PC's configured folder), and the mod has a server half | This PC **and** the connected server, with one click (Install, Update and Uninstall alike) |

A server-only mod is listed only when Simple acts on the server (then its button acts on the server); otherwise it is hidden. No new authorization path: the route is `ServerRouteSelector`'s and the server's own remote-install status.
