A README cached before this change (text only) is shown at once and fetched once for its markdown; a failed fetch keeps the text and is not retried. Game-free tested (`InstallListViewTests`). Live 2026-10-03: after the fix the first open fetched Almanacs's markdown once (headings bold), and after a further Core hot swap the headings came straight from the disk cache.

**NSFW filter.** `CatalogEntry.HasNsfwContent` comes from Thunderstore's `has_nsfw_content`. Preferences → **Sources and downloads** → **Show mods with NSFW content** (`[Mods] ShowNsfwContent`, personal, default off). While off, flagged mods are hidden from the Install list unless installed or pending on this PC, on the local server folder, or on the connected server (remote rows).

**Mod page.** A mod page whose mod has a catalog listing shows the same two side cells in its framework band (`Client: Installed 1.2.0`, `Server: Not installed · Ask a server admin`), in the row's tones, replacing the old server-part sentence. The band says `Server`, not the picked server's name: live on 2026-10-03, HotDemo's `Server · Progenitor Forms Test: Installed …` was cut at the cell's width. The name stays in the readback (`serverColumn`) and on the Settings section's `Applies to everyone on …` line (checked live 2026-10-03). The band never says `Not installed` for want of evidence: a page opened before the Install tab takes the same installed-state scan the tab takes (ledgers, files, inventory; no network) when the pane has none, and draws no side cells when there is still none (`ModsMenu.PageSideCells`). Live 2026-10-03 on a fresh Core generation, HotDemo read `Client: Not installed · Server: Not installed` until the Install tab had been opened once; the fix is game-free tested, not re-checked live. The page keeps its own single action button.

**Readback.** Listings: `client`, `server` (`{kind, text, tone, buttons, note}`), `shortcut`, `drawerOpen`, `nsfw`. Toolbar: `picker {picked, chosen, entries[{id, kind, caption, name, path, detail}], error, target, route, block}`, `nsfw {shown}`. Drawer body row (`drawerBody:<key>`): `drawerBody {height, lines, contentEstimate, listViewport, blockShare}` and the scroll block's `scroll {content, viewport, offset, scrolls}`. (Superseded: the reveal readback `scrolledTo` / `toTop` is replaced by `anchoredTo`.). Mod page band: `sideClient`, `sideServer`, `serverColumn`. Bridge clicks: `<key>/expand`, `<key>/client-a`, `<key>/client-b`, `<key>/server-a`, `<key>/server-b`, `<key>/both`, `toolbar/serverPick`, `drawer:<key>/collapse`.

**Kit additions** ([ui.md](ui.md)): `CellKind.ScrollText` with `CellLine` kinds (Body, Heading, Muted, Fact, Warn, Code); `CellSkin.Quiet` (box-less underlined caption), `Expander` (the game's dropdown arrow, turned 90° when closed), `Dropdown` (the game's dropdown bar with arrow); `CellSpec.OverlayOf`, `HeightOverride`, `NoteReserve`; `CellRowSpec` `joinAbove` / `indent`; `RevealOffsetY(..., toTop)`. No palette tokens changed.

### Per-side rows acceptance procedure (not run)

Authorized test session only, test client at the title screen, Mods workspace on **Install mods**; read with `mods_ui widget_read` and `rects` (focus-free), screenshot each step.

1. Header: columns `expand, icon, name, side, client, server, released, downloads`, `rowWidth` 1236, nothing dropped, Server header `Server · <pick>`.
2. Picker: `widget_click toolbar/serverPick`; expect the popup with the entries above; pick each; Server cells and header follow; a bad folder shows the refusal.
3. States: find a row for each table line (installed, outdated, pending, removal pending, managed, both-sided missing/older, unknown, `No server`, `Same as client`, `Not loaded there`); click `client-a` / `server-a` / `both` and confirm each box names the right side, then cancel.
4. Drawer: `widget_click <key>/expand`; the row stays where it was (INSTALLSIDES2); `drawerBody.blockShare` about 0.8 (lower only when the text is short); the next row visible; rail, fill and seam drawn; wheel over the body scrolls it (`scroll.offset` changes) and at its end the list moves; **Collapse** closes; opening another closes the first.
5. NSFW: with the switch off, flagged uninstalled mods are absent; turn it on, they appear and their drawer shows `NSFW content`.
6. Mod page of a listed mod: the band shows both side cells matching the Install row.

Leave the client at the title screen with the workspace closed.

## Install list columns

**Source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified** (job INSTALLCOLS1). The column model is `InstallColumns` ([`src/Progenitor.Core/Ui/InstallColumns.cs`](../src/Progenitor.Core/Ui/InstallColumns.cs)); `InstallModsPane.RowCells` and `HeaderCells` build every listing row and the header from it, so they share one key set and one set of widths. Tests: `InstallColumnsTests`, `InstallListViewTests`, `InstallFilterBlockTests`, `InstallListPolishTests`, `CellRowTests`.

The list was reviewed at 3840×1080 on 2026-09-29 ("I think we can do better on our column management here. Also maybe cancel should be cancel uninstall."). The Released column came and went with the sort, a dead gap sat before Downloads while Author truncated, versions and dates were drawn letter-spaced, an Update column said `Oakveil` on every row, the status words floated between two columns, a button said a bare `Cancel`, and a `Sort:` button repeated the header's own sort mark.

**Columns.** The set is always the same, in this order, whatever the sort or the row's state. Widths are at the menu's design scale. The live Install row is **1236 px** at 3840×1080 and at 1600×1024 alike (the workspace is scaled; readback `columns.rowWidth`, header rect `w1236`), and every column fits it: Mod gets 246 px and nothing drops.

| Column | Cells | Width | Content | Header click | Drops on a narrow pane |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Mod | `icon`, `name` | 36 px + all remaining width (never under 240 px) | Icon (`IconCache`, placeholder until it lands); name over the one-liner; the update note leads the second line | A to Z | Never |
| Author | `author` | 140 px | Package owner, else author; muted; ellipsis only past about 15 characters | A to Z | 3rd |
| Side | `side` | 72 px | Side badge (`PackageSideDerivation`, or the inspected `[BepInProcess]`) | — | Never |
| Version | `version` | 70 px | Plain text | — | Never |
| Released | `released` | 98 px | Short date: the version's `date_created`, else the package's `date_updated`, else `—` | Newest first | 1st |
| Installed | `installed` | 104 px | Short date this machine installed the mod; blank when it is not installed | Newest first | 2nd |
| Downloads | `downloads` | 118 px, right-aligned | Sum of the provider's per-version counts (`123,456`; `1.2M` from a million), else `—` | Most first | 4th |
| Status | `installStatus`, `install` | 96 + 8 + 172 = 276 px, right-aligned | State text, then the one button (captions at 16 px) | — | Never |

**No header is ever cut.** Every column is at least `InstallColumns.HeaderCaptionWidth` of its caption: the upper-case caption at the 13 px header size, measured per capital and scaled to the game's face (calibrated on the live `RELEASED…` / `DOWNLO…` truncations at 84 px), plus, for a sortable column, the space and ▼ / ▲ slot whether or not it is sorted, so the layout never shifts with the sort. Headers are aligned like their columns (Downloads right). The `STATUS` caption sits over the state-text slot, where the column starts, so the slot left empty on an Install row reads as part of the Status column. Version, dates and counts are plain text cells (`CellKind.Muted`), never the mono treatment that drew `1 . 0 . 0`. Dates read `Sep 23`, or month and two-digit year in another year (`Sep '25`), in US English whatever the machine's culture; the readback carries the full date. Released is the release's UTC calendar date; Installed is the local date of the install time. The drawer's meta line keeps `YYYY-MM-DD`.

**Installed date source.** `InstallTimes` ([`src/Progenitor.Core/Install/InstallTimes.cs`](../src/Progenitor.Core/Install/InstallTimes.cs)) is the one rule; the Keybinds tab's "first installed wins" is to use the same helper (`InstallTimes.Of(pluginId, pluginFilePath, gameRoot)`), so the two cannot disagree. In order:

1. The install ledger (`BepInEx/progenitor-install.json`): the earliest `writtenUtc` of the rows that name the mod. The Install tab matches rows to the listing with `LedgerSpelling`; `InstallTimes.LedgerTime` matches by the row's `pluginId` or file name. A pending copy that the local server promotes at its next start has its row rewritten, so such an install reads that start's time.
2. The plugin file's **creation** time. For a mod another tool installed this is the loaded plugin's file (`PluginInfo.Location`); otherwise the Progenitor file the scan found. Windows sets the creation time when a file is copied or extracted, while the last-write time survives a copy and is often the author's build time.
3. The file's last-write time, only when the creation time cannot be read or is implausible (before 2000, or more than a day in the future).

Rows that are not installed (no state, a tool, installing, blocked, removed) show nothing. The readback says which source answered.

**Update note.** A listing that does not carry the current update's tag shows a warn-tone note at the start of the second line under its name: `Built for Gloomrot` (the author's last tag) or `No update tag`, followed by ` · ` and the one-liner. Nothing is shown when it carries the current tag, or when the current update is unknown. The Update column is gone, and the note never appears in the Status column. The drawer's Update tag fact still lists every tag the author set.

**Status column.** The state text sits immediately left of the button, right-aligned in a 96 px slot, on at most two short lines (`InstallColumns.StatusLines` breaks at ` · `, else at the last space that fits: `Removed at` / `next start`). A row with no button gives its text the whole 276 px column; its button cell stays in the model, hidden and unclickable. Every button says what it does; none reads a bare `Cancel`.

| Row | State text | Button |
|---|---|---|
| Not installed | — | **Install**, or **Install · restart** when the listing owes a restart (unchanged meaning) |
| Installed by Progenitor, or another tool's mod the preloader can remove | `Installed` (green) | **Uninstall** |
| Update available | `Installed` | **Update · restart** |
| Waiting in `progenitor-pending/` | `Loads at next start` (yellow) | **Cancel install** (only on a ledgered copy) |
| Removed at the next start, ledgered or another tool's | `Removed at next start` (orange) | **Cancel uninstall** |
| Managed elsewhere | `Managed elsewhere` or `Managed by <tool>`, and the short reason on the second line when it fits the column whole, else only on the drawer note (muted) | None |
| Thunderstore tool | `Tool, not a plugin` (muted) | None |
| Removed this session, still loaded | `Unloads at next start` (orange) | **Install** |

The mod page's status bar uses the same words: **Cancel uninstall** for a waiting removal (ledgered or not) and **Cancel install** for a pending install. The confirmations are unchanged (`Keep <name> installed?` / **Yes, keep it** for a ledgered pending uninstall).

**Sort.** The header is the only sort control; the toolbar's `Sort:` button is gone (nothing else read it). Mod, Author, Released, Installed and Downloads sort; Side, Version and Status do not. A click on a new column sorts it in its natural direction (A to Z for Mod and Author; newest or most first for Released, Installed and Downloads). A second click on the sorted column reverses it. Only the active header carries the indicator: ▼ descending, ▲ ascending. Listings with no value for the column (undated, unpublished, not installed, no author) sort last in both directions. The sort and its direction stay in memory for the catalog for the process (never on disk); a new pane keeps them and **Clear filters** keeps them. The default is still Recently released, newest first. A sort key the bridge or an older build sends that no longer exists, such as `likes` or `rating` from before stage 3, falls back to that default.

**Narrow panes.** Only a pane narrower than the live 1236 px row drops anything. When the row cannot hold every column with Mod at 240 px, optional columns drop in `InstallColumns.DropPriority` order: Released, Installed, Author, Downloads (at a 1050 px row, Released and Installed). The **active sort column is never dropped**, so its header and ▼/▲ always show which sort is on; the next column in the order drops in its place. The Status column and its button never shrink or drop. The readback names what dropped.

**Readback.** New keys; every existing key keeps its meaning:

- Header: `sort`, `sortDirection` (`asc` / `desc`), `columns {set, keys, rowWidth, widths, dropped, dropPriority, sortable}`, resolved at the current workspace frame's Install row width. The header row's own readback adds `sortAscending`.
- Toolbar: `sort` and `sortDirection`. The `toolbar/sort` cell no longer exists.
- Listing: `status` (the state text on one line), `button`, `installed {date, fullDate, utc, source}` (source `ledger` / `fileCreated` / `fileWritten` / `none`), `released` (the drawn short date), `releasedDate` (`YYYY-MM-DD`), `downloads` (the full count), `updateNote`. `listing.actions` keeps its names (`install`, `uninstall`, `cancel`, `reactivate`, `cancel-removal`, or none).
- A two-line cell's readback adds `noteLead` when it has one.

**Kit additions** (see [ui.md](ui.md#cell-rows-positioned-cells-from-a-column-model-pending-list2-live-acceptance)): `CellWidth.Fill`, `CellSpec.DropOrder`, `CellSpec.NoteLead` / `NoteLeadTone`, and `IUiCellHeader.SetSorted(key, ascending)` with `SortAscending`. No new colors: the note uses the existing warn tone, the managed text the muted tone, and ▲ the header's existing sorted-mark color.

### Install list acceptance procedure (not run)

Run only in an authorized test session, on the test client at the title screen with the Mods workspace open on **Install mods**. Read state with `mods_ui widget_read` (focus-free).

1. At 3840×1080, read the header. Expect `columns.rowWidth` 1236; the columns Mod, Author, Side, Version, Released, Installed, Downloads, Status all visible; `columns.dropped` empty; `widths.name` 246; `RELEASED ▼`, `INSTALLED` and `DOWNLOADS` drawn whole (no ellipsis) whichever column is sorted; the RELEASED header carrying ▼; no `Sort:` button on the toolbar; no empty band wider than the 96 px state-text slot between DOWNLOADS and the buttons.
2. Click each sortable header twice. Expect the same column set after every click, the rows reordered, the indicator only on the clicked header, ▼ then ▲ (Mod and Author: ▲ then ▼), and undated or not-installed rows staying last.
3. Look at a row whose author is `ExampleTeam` or `TheMistRealm`. Expect the full name, versions such as `2.42.82` and dates such as `Sep 23` / `Sep '25` without letter spacing or ellipsis, and the captions **Install · restart**, **Uninstall**, **Cancel uninstall** and **Update · restart** whole.
4. Turn **Current update** off. Expect `Built for <tag>` in the warn tone under the name of rows missing the current tag, and nothing on current rows.
5. Find an installed mod, a pending install, a pending uninstall and a managed-elsewhere mod. Expect the state text and button pairs in the Status table, the right edges aligned, and `listing.installed.source` set.
6. At 1600×1024, read the header. Expect the same 1236 px row and nothing dropped.

Leave the client at the title screen with the workspace closed.

## Server installs: one Server target

Status: **source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified.** The Install tab has one server target, **Server** (and **Both** = client + server). It never offers "Local server" and "This server" as separate choices; the route is picked for it, in this order (`ServerRouteSelector.Select`):

| Order | Route | When | Needs admin |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Local folder** | F23 (live 2026-10-07; built and game-free tested in `LocalOnlyListingTests`, `InstallHotLoadTests`, not live-verified): while this client hosts a private game (Bootstrap's `IsHostingPrivateGame`), the hosted child's own folder (the game folder's bundled server that the hosted launcher stages) is the local folder, ahead of `[Mods] DedicatedServerPath`. Installs and uninstalls write to its BepInEx and hot load where they can, the boxes name it `your private game`, and the route reads `local: your private game (<folder>)`. Review 2026-10-07 (M5): only while the connection is this machine with a completed handshake (or there is none), and `[Install] TreatLocalServerAsRemote` still forces the remote route; a stale hosting flag never routes another server's installs into the game folder. The hosted folder is read at most every 2 s (L6), and Stop server to apply uses it on that route (L7). F34 (live 2026-10-08; built and game-free tested in `LocalOnlyListingTests`, not live-verified): every server-side read the Install rows and the Mods page make (the ledger, pending installs and removals, the reload folder) comes from that same folder while hosting, never from the configured dedicated server's (`ModsMenu.ServerReadRoot`); before, the rows showed the dedicated server's pending installs and its Stop server to apply for the private game. A plugin the hosted launcher staged into the private game's `BepInEx/progenitor-reload` (a copy of the client's) counts as installed there, since the hosted child loads that folder. Review M-e (2026-10-08): the catalog's write root and these reads use the private game's folder only while the route decision makes it the local route (`ServerRouteSelector.HostedApplies`: hosting, `TreatLocalServerAsRemote` off, and no connection or a local one with a completed handshake). F34 live follow-up: the Server cell read `Missing: the client has it` for HotDemo although the private game loaded the copy staged from this client's reload folder, because the cell reads install ledgers only; a server reload file with the same name as this listing's client reload file now reads as installed there (removed with this client's copy, not from the Server cell). F35: the Server column header and the picker name it `Your private game` (not the folder's name, `VRising_Server`), and the removal log reads `(your private game)`. Otherwise, while connected to a server on this PC, the folder whose own server holds the joined port (auto-detected, LOCALDETECT1, below). Otherwise: `[Install] DedicatedServerPath` is validated **and** the client is not connected, or is connected to that server. "Connected to that server" is the Stop server check — the folder's `ServerHostSettings.json` port equals the joined port, plus a completed Progenitor handshake (`ServerProcessControl.GracefulAvailable`) — and the joined address is this machine (loopback or one of its interface addresses, `LocalAddress.IsThisMachine`), because another machine's server on the same port must not receive local-folder installs. LOCALROUTE1 (2026-10-07; built and game-free tested, not live-verified): a public address (joined through the router, as the game's Continue does) counts too when the loopback rejoin's own rule verifies it: the folder's server runs on this PC under the connected server's name and operator key (`JoinLocalRoute.IsLocalServer`; readback `route.inputs.verifiedThisPcServer`, log `Install: the connected server … is the configured folder's server (…)`). `[Install] TreatLocalServerAsRemote` still forces such a server remote. Review 2026-10-07: the folder's facts (its server process, its command line, its settings and operator key files) and this machine's interface addresses are computed on the thread pool and cached (`LocalFolderFactsCache`, refreshed every 30 s and at each join's result; `ServerProcessControl.RunningCache`, 3 s). The frame never scans a process. Until the first facts arrive, the public address reads as not verified (the remote route). | No. Works with the server stopped. |
| 2 | **Remote** | Connected to a different Progenitor server whose status says remote installs are on and this player is authorized: on that server's `adminlist.txt` (no `adminauth` needed) or `adminauth`ed. | Admin list membership. |
| 3 | **None** | Anything else. Install falls back to the client exactly as before; server-side rows show one line saying why. | — |

**Any local Progenitor server is local automatically** (LOCALDETECT1, decided 2026-10-08). Status: source-checked, built and game-free tested in `LocalServerDetectTests` and `InstallSidesPaneTests`. Live 2026-10-10 (Steam client, test servers on 9976 and 9986): detection reached `localSource=detected` with the right folder; that run also found the two defects fixed below (the configured folder as the route while joining another port, and a box naming the detected server over a client write). The fixed build (Core cb7e0256) was live-verified the same day: joining 9986, the route sampled once a second read none (detection pending, then held until the handshake), then `detected` with the 9986 folder, and never the 9976 folder; the box read "Install ReloadExample 0.1.0 on Progenitor Headless Test?" and the log planned it "for local server: ApplyNow", wrote it into the 9986 folder's `progenitor-reload` and the server loaded it live; Uninstall named the same server and the server unloaded it cleanly; nothing was written to 9976 or the client; joining 9976 then read `configured` with its folder and never the 9986 one. Not live-verified: a public-address join to a detected server, the operator-key mismatch refusal, and an elevated or default-save server being refused. When the client joins a server at an address on this PC (loopback or one of its interface addresses), Progenitor finds the server folder itself, so a second local server on another port gets its own paths without changing `[Mods] DedicatedServerPath`:

1. The process that holds the joined UDP port is read from the system's UDP table (the table Stop server already uses for "the folder's own server on the joined port"). Exactly one process may hold it, and it must be a `VRisingServer.exe` whose full image path could be read. A name match alone never counts.
2. The process's command line must have been read. An unreadable one (an elevated server, for example) is a refusal: its arguments could name the live server's data.
3. The live-server fence runs before any file in that folder is read: the folder, the exe, the process's working directory and its arguments (a `-persistentDataPath` into the live server's data counts) must not name the live dedicated server (`ServerProcessControl.IsLiveServer`). The route checks the folder again, and so does every write root that comes from it.
4. The exe's folder must validate as a dedicated-server folder (`LocalServerFolder.Validate`: `VRisingServer.exe`, `BepInEx`, writable, no escaping link), and the exe must be that folder's own.
5. The server must run on its own save folder: a `-persistentDataPath` that is not the default one (`ServerProcessControl.SaveFolderRefusal`, the same rule Start server applies).
6. The process's own settings must name the joined port: its `-persistentDataPath` settings file, then the folder's own copies.
7. Progenitor must be installed there: `BepInEx/plugins/Progenitor.Bootstrap.dll`, directly or one package folder down.

The route then treats that folder as this connection's local folder, provided the Progenitor handshake completed and the connected server's signed catalog does not contradict it: a known operator key that differs from the folder's `BepInEx/config/progenitor-operator.pub`, or a known server name that differs from the detected server's, is a refusal (the catalog's default name `V Rising` names no server). Installs and uninstalls write to its BepInEx, the server-side rows read its ledger and pending folders, and the Install boxes' **Installs on** / **Removes from** row, the Server column header and the picker's connected entry name it under its own name and folder. All of these follow the connection: joining another local server on another port detects that server's folder. A folder whose own server holds the joined port also wins over a configured folder that matches by its settings port alone, because two folders can name one port but only one server can hold it. The configured folder stays the fallback. It is the route when nothing is connected, and it is used while connected when detection finds nothing or is ambiguous. `[Install] TreatLocalServerAsRemote` still forces a detected server onto the remote route. **Save, stop and start again** stays limited to the configured folder: a detected server offers **Stop server to apply**, gated by `ServerProcessControl.GracefulAvailableDetected` (the ordinary gate, plus a readable command line and the server's own save folder at the moment of the stop). An install, update or uninstall confirmation is pinned to the server folder it was opened against: if the folder changes while the box is open, its Yes writes nothing and the pane reads `The server folder changed while this box was open (you joined another server), so nothing was written. Open it again to act on the new server.`

The detection reads the process table and files, so it runs on the thread pool and is cached per joined port (`LocalServerDetectCache`, refreshed every 10 s). Each join's result drops the cached answer at once and starts a fresh detection at once (not at the next read): until it lands, the server reads as not detected, never as the folder of the server that held the port before. The fresh detection reuses the dropped answer only when the same process (pid and full image path, the port's only holder) still holds the port. While the client is joining or in a game (any client state but the main menu and the disconnect screen), the configured folder is the route only when its port is the joined port; with detection pending or refused on another port the route is not local (remote or none). An install box, its target and its plan come from the same route: a server target this pane cannot write is the client fallback in the box too, and when where an install goes changed between its Yes and the write, nothing is written and the pane reads `Where this install goes changed after you confirmed it (the server connection changed), so nothing was written. Open it again to see where it goes now.` A read still running across a join boundary or a Core unload is discarded when it lands. Each change is logged once: `Install: local server auto-detected for joined port <port>: <folder> (pid <pid> … holds port <port>, its own settings name that port, and Progenitor is installed there).` or `Install: no local server folder auto-detected for joined port <port> (<reason>).` The `remote_install` readback carries `route.localSource` (`hosted` / `configured` / `detected` / `none`), `route.localFolder`, and `route.inputs.detectedFolder`, `detectedPort`, `detectedName` and `detectedNote`. When detection does not apply, the route's `why` ends with `not auto-detected: <reason>`.

**Live test recipe (acceptance procedure, not run).** Use two isolated test servers on this PC with Progenitor installed, on different game ports, neither of them the live server. Set `[Mods] DedicatedServerPath` to the first server's folder.
1. Join the second server at `127.0.0.1:<its port>`. Wait about 10 s. Read `remote_install` and expect `route.kind = local`, `route.localSource = detected` and `route.localFolder` = the second server's folder. The client log should have the `auto-detected` line.
2. Open the Install tab. The Server column header and an install box's **Installs on** row should name the second server. Install a disposable sample to the server and confirm that its file lands in the second server's `BepInEx` and not the first's. Remove it again.
3. Leave the game and join the first server. Expect `route.localSource = configured` (or `detected` with the same folder) and the paths of the first server.
4. Clean up: remove the sample from both servers' reload folders and return the client to the title screen.

**Status words.** The Status column shows a short label from one table (`InstallStatusLabels`), at most two lines beside the button, and the full sentence goes to the drawer note: `Pending game restart`, `Removal pending game restart` (client), `Pending server restart`, `Removal pending server restart`, `Installed on server (remote)` / `Installed on server (local)` (server), `Sending…`, `Downloading…`, `Verifying…`, `Installing…`, `Removing…` (a remote step), and for a row with no route or a failed step `Needs admin list`, `Not allowed`, `Remote installs off`, `No Progenitor on server`, `Checking server…`, `Can't reach Thunderstore`, `Download failed`, `Package check failed`, `Server busy`, `Too many installs`, `Version differs`, `Not on Thunderstore`, `Needed by others`, `Server install failed`. The Status text column is 140 px (15 characters a line at the 16 px face); one line for every label would need about 200 px, which the Mod column cannot give at the live 1236 px row (it now keeps 202 px; floor 200). The drawer sentences include `Server installs need you on this server's admin list` with the help `Ask the host to add your SteamID to the server's adminlist.txt; remote installs then work as soon as you join. (An admin who is not on that file can also run adminauth in the console.)` and `Remote installs unavailable: the server can't reach Thunderstore (<cause>).` A remote step's result (refused, failed, done) stays on its row until the next operation on it.

**Remote route.** Install opens the usual confirmation with `Installs on the server you are connected to…`; nothing is downloaded on the client. **Yes** sends one request; the row's Status then follows the server's steps (`Sent to server…`, `Downloading on server…`, `Verifying on server…`, `Installing on server…`) and the button reads **Cancel** until the write step starts. It ends as `Installed on server (remote)` (hot-loaded), `Pending server restart` (pending lane, with **Save and stop server** in the drawer — the same confirmation and request as [Stop server to apply](#stop-server-to-apply-f20); the host or panel must start the server again, Progenitor never restarts it), or `Server install failed` with the plain reason in the drawer (for example `The server can't reach Thunderstore (timed out). Your host may block outbound connections.`). **Both** writes the client half locally first and sends the server half after that write succeeds.

**Rows read the server's ledger.** For an authorized player the server sends its ledger rows (the `list` request, sent again every time the Install tab opens); others see today's rows. A copy only the server holds names the route that wrote it: `Installed on server (remote)` or `Installed on server (local)`; a pending copy reads `Pending server restart` with the route in the drawer sentence. A server copy at a different version than the listing offers **Update** through the remote route. **Uninstall** / **Cancel install** on such a row is a remote uninstall: a hot copy unloads now, a pending copy is deleted, a promoted copy is marked for the server's next start. Only ledger files are ever removed.

**Readback.** `mods_ui` listings gain `action.statusText`, `action.remote` and `action.route {kind, block, reason, toConnectedServer, localInstall, serverRow, latest}`. The `remote_install` bridge op ([bridge-ops-client.md](bridge-ops-client.md#remote_install)) reads and drives the same channel. The server side, the wire and the settings are in [Mod package capabilities](mod-package-capabilities.md#admin-remote-installs).

### Buttons and the pending action row (UI polish, 2026-10-01)

Status: **source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified.** An Install or Update button says where it writes, never when: `Install` (this client), `Install on server` (local folder or remote route), `Install on both`, and `Update` / `Update on server` / `Update on both`. The restart a write owes is the Status label after it (`Pending game restart`, `Pending server restart`). The old `Install · restart` / `Update · restart` captions are no longer shown.

The pending bar is one compact action row pinned under the list, like the settings page's action row: the short sentence at the right (`1 mod installed — restart the local server to apply. »`, or `… the connected server …` on the remote route; pressing it still lists the pending mods) and a key-cap **Stop server to apply** when a server restart applies the changes ([below](#stop-server-to-apply-f20)). A change that needs only a game restart gets no button here; the pending-restart bar above the list offers **Restart now** for it ([INSTALLRESTART1](#restart-for-pending-installs-installrestart1)). Copies waiting on the connected server (remote route) count toward the row. Readback: `pending` keeps its fields; the row is `install-pending` with cells `pending-text` and `pending-restart`.

#### Stop server to apply (F20)

Status: **built and game-free tested, not live-verified.**

**Save, stop and start again (F30, live 2026-10-08; built and game-free tested in `InstallListViewTests`, not live-verified).** On the local route to the configured, validated `[Mods] DedicatedServerPath` folder whose server runs now (not a private game this client hosts, never a remote server), Progenitor can start the server again from the launch record the stop writes (as Preferences › Local server › **Start server** does). There the bar's button, the drawer's button and the box's yes read **Save, stop and start again**, the heading `Restart <server>?`, and **Starts again** `Yes: Progenitor starts it again from its folder once it has stopped, and you can rejoin when it's back.` Once the stop reaches `stopped`, a background thread waits up to 60 s for the old process to exit and starts the folder's server (`ServerProcessControl.Start`; the frame never scans a process). The footer reads `<server> stopped. Starting it again…`, then `<server> stopped and started again. Rejoin it when it's back.`, or `<server> stopped, but Progenitor couldn't start it again: <reason>. Start it again with Start server under Preferences › Local server.` Log: `Install: <server> stopped; starting it again from its folder.` and `Install: start again started (pid N).` / `failed: <reason>`. Where Progenitor cannot start the server, the button stays the honest **Stop server to apply** described below. **Review 2026-10-08 (H1, M-a to M-e, L-a to L-c; built and game-free tested in `ServerProcessControlTests`, `InstallListViewTests`, `LocalServerFolderTests`, `LocalOnlyListingTests`, `InstallRestartTests`, not live-verified):**
- **The live dedicated server is fenced.** Every stop, force stop, start, the graceful-route check, `Save, stop and start again`, folder validation and the server picker refuse the live dedicated server, by folder, launch-record exe, working directory or argument, and a running process's path or command line (`WorldRestorePaths.IsLiveServerPath` / `IsLiveServerCommandLine`, `HostedPathRules.IsForbiddenLiveServerPath`, the fence world saves, staging, the hosted launcher and world restores already used). Folder validation reads `That folder is the live dedicated server. Progenitor never installs into, stops or starts it; choose a test server folder.`; the picker never lists it, even when discovery finds it running.
- **Only a known launch is replayed.** The restart is offered only while the folder's server is seen with a pid and a read command line, and it replays the record this stop wrote from that process. A stop whose command line could not be read never replaces a record that has arguments.
- **The wait for exit fails closed.** The start waits on the stopped process itself (its pid, its full path re-matched), then needs two clean reads of the process list. A read error, or a server process whose path cannot be read, refuses with `The process list could not be read, so Progenitor can't tell whether that server is running.`
- **One start at a time.** A process-wide start lock covers the whole wait and start; Preferences' **Start server** meanwhile reads `Refused: A start of that server is already in progress.` Every start reads the process list now (not the cached answer), and the new process is stored in the cache at once.
- **Exactly the folder's server.** A record must start `<folder>\VRisingServer.exe` (after canonicalization) with a working directory inside the folder.
- **Its own console.** The server starts in its own console, minimized and not activated (`CREATE_NEW_CONSOLE`, `SW_SHOWMINNOACTIVE`), with the client's `DOORSTOP_*` variables removed.
- **Read again at Yes.** The stop gate and the restart offer are checked again when Yes is pressed, on the one root the stop uses.
- **Follow-up review 2026-10-08 (M1 to M3, L1 to L4; built and game-free tested in `LiveServerFenceTests`, `ServerProcessControlTests`, `StopServerTransportTests`, not live-verified):**
  - *The local stop goes to the folder's own server.* Besides the folder's port, the handshake and this machine, the folder's server must be running and be the process bound to the joined UDP port (the system's UDP table, read at most every 2 s). Otherwise `Port N belongs to another process than this folder's server, so Progenitor sends no stop there.`, or `Progenitor can't tell which process uses the joined port, so it sends no stop.`
  - *A start names its own save folder.* A launch record without `-persistentDataPath` is refused (`The launch record has no -persistentDataPath, …`), and so is one under the default save folder (`%USERPROFILE%\AppData\LocalLow\Stunlock Studios\VRisingServer`), which the live server may use. A record with no arguments is never written. "Save, stop and start again" is offered only when the running server's command line would pass these checks.
  - *The fence resolves the live root.* When the live root is itself a junction or symbolic link, its target is fenced too; a root that exists but cannot be resolved makes the fence refuse (fail closed). Resolving reads only the link's metadata. Administrative shares count as this machine by name, fully qualified name, loopback or one of this machine's addresses; arguments are read quoted, after `=`, and relative to the working directory.
  - *The server's environment.* `SteamAppId` is the server's (1604030), and the client's `SteamGameId` and overlay ids are removed. The start leaves the client's job object when the job allows it (`CREATE_BREAKAWAY_FROM_JOB`); otherwise it starts inside it and the log says so. Not verified live: whether a console delegated to Windows Terminal honors the minimized, not-activated start.
  - *Cheaper caption reads.* The fence and the replay check behind the button's caption are cached for 30 s per root and process; the stop and the start always check afresh.
  - *One start across Core generations.* The start lock is also a named mutex in this session's namespace.
  - *Final review (2026-10-08; built and game-free tested in `LiveServerFenceTests`, not live-verified).* A resolved path goes through the same administrative-share rule as a typed one, so a mapped drive or a link onto `\\localhost\C$` or `\\<this machine's address>\C$` resolves back to the local drive and is fenced, and so is a live root that resolves to such a share. The UDP owner read retries a table that grew (error 122) and reads "unknown" unless both address families were read. `-persistentDataPath=value` is read for the host settings too, a following `-flag` is never taken as the value, and a save-folder comparison that cannot be made refuses the start. The stop gate checks the running process against the fence uncached.
  - *Deploying this needs Bootstrap.* `WorldRestore/*.cs` (the fence) is compiled into Bootstrap too, so these fence changes reach a process only after a Bootstrap rebuild and a restart of the client and the test server; a Core hot swap alone leaves Bootstrap's copy of the fence on the previous rules.

The button stops the server; Progenitor does not start it again. It was called **Restart server now** until F20 (live 2026-10-07), when a press sent a stop request with no confirmation, a second press sent a second one, and nothing showed the server's answer.

1. **Confirmation.** A press opens the dialog kit's box: heading `Stop <server>?`, facts **Server** (the server's name and folder or address), **Players online** and **Starts again**, the note `The server saves the world, then quits. Everyone on it is disconnected, you included.`, and **Save and stop** / **Cancel** (`install-stop-buttons`, cells `stopYes` / `stopNo`).
   - **Players online** lists the connected `User` entities in this client's world, which include this player (`2 (this client's list, you included): A, B`, at most six names, then `and N more`). Without a client world it reads `Unknown: this client cannot see who is connected there.`
   - **Starts again** reads `Not by itself. Start it with Start server under Preferences › Local server, or the way you usually start it.` on the local route, and `Not by Progenitor. Your host, panel or script starts it again.` on the remote route.
   - On the local route the box is offered only when Preferences' **Stop server** would take the graceful route (`ServerProcessControl.GracefulAvailable`: a command route, the folder's port is the joined one, and a completed Progenitor handshake). Otherwise it names the reason, points to Preferences › Local server, and has only **Close** (`stopClose`).
2. **One request.** **Save and stop** sends one `StopServerRequest` with a fresh nonce. While it is out, the button reads `Stopping…` in the disabled tone and a press sends nothing. The drawer's **Save and stop server** uses the same box and the same request.
3. **The answer.** The pane reads the reply for that nonce every 0.5 s. The footer sentence is replaced by the request's line:

| State | Footer |
|---|---|
| `waiting` | `Asking <server> to save and stop…` |
| `stopping` | `<server> accepted: saving the world, then stopping…` |
| `stopped` | `<server> stopped.` then how it starts again. The server's process exited (local route), or this client lost its connection to it |
| `refused` | `<server> refused: <reason>`, one sentence per reply reason (`not-admin`, `save-failed`, `save-timeout`, `no-save-path`, `no-quit-path`, `unsupported-request`) |
| `no-answer` | `The server did not answer the stop request. <server> was not stopped by Progenitor.` (no reply within 10 s) |
| `still-running` | `<server> accepted the stop but is still running after 45 seconds.` |
| `not-sent` | `The stop request could not be sent to <server>.` |

Every state except `waiting` and `stopping` enables the button again.

**Launch record.** On the local route, the process seen before the stop is kept. When it exits, its launch record is written, as Preferences' Stop server does, so **Start server** there can start it again.

The client log has one `StopServer:` line per step and one per reply received. The server authorizes the request the way remote installs do (the admin list, `adminauth`, or the private game's host) and logs every decision as a `StopServer:` line (F21). Readback: `pending.stop {state, inFlight, nonce, line, states, dialogOpen}` on the `install-pending` row.

`Pending server restart` still takes two lines in the 140 px Status text column: one line needs about 198 px, and tightening the cell's 4 px padding cannot close that gap.

`mods_ui search` on the Install tab filters the Install rows (the same path as typing in its search field) and now answers `{tab, search, shown, filtered, total}`, so a search that seems to do nothing shows which tab it filtered and the counts after it.

### Authenticate as admin

Status: **source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified; the game call is off by default.** Remote installs, administrator edits of server settings ([below](#settings-rows)) and Stop server's graceful request authorize by admin-list membership, but adminOnly commands still gate on `User.IsAdmin`, which only the game's own `adminauth` sets. `.stop-server` uses the stop channel's rule (`AuthorizeShared`: admin list, adminauth, or the host) since review L3 (2026-10-07). The stop channel answers at most 5 requests per sender a minute (`rate-limited`, logged, L1), and an accepted stop whose process has not quit after 120 s no longer blocks a new one (L2). The server's status therefore also says, for the receiving player, `onAdminList` and `adminAuthed`.

Where such a control is locked (Stop server while connected to that server), a player with `onAdminList` and not `adminAuthed` sees **Authenticate as admin**. It runs the game's own command — `Engine.Console.StunConsole.Commands.TryCommand("adminauth", false)`, the call the game's debug menu makes — so the server's `AdminAuthSystem` checks its list and sets `IsAdmin`; Progenitor never sets it. The button shows `Authenticating…`, then the next status settles it: `You are now authenticated as admin on this server.`, or after 8 seconds `The server did not accept adminauth…`. Live-verified 2026-10-01 on the Steam client and the isolated test server (an admin-list member reconnected without adminauth, the bridge drove the button path, the server's `User.IsAdmin` flipped to true within seconds and the status read `done`), so `[Ui] AdminAuthButton` defaults to `true`; off, the same place says `You're on this server's admin list. Type adminauth in the game's console…`. A player not on the list sees `Ask the host to add your SteamID to adminlist.txt` and no button. Bridge: `remote_install {"action":"adminauth"}` drives the same path; `status` reads back `adminAuth {buttonEnabled, state, message, offer}`.

### Test the remote route against a local server (REMOTETEST1)

**Live (2026-10-03, Steam client in game on the isolated test server):** with the server's `ThunderstoreBaseUrl = https://thunderstore.invalid` (restart) the server logged `RemoteInstall: Thunderstore unreachable (dns)`, the forced-remote route read `block: unreachable`, a bridge remote install was refused with "Remote installs unavailable: the server can't reach Thunderstore (name lookup failed).", and restoring `https://thunderstore.io` (restart) logged `Thunderstore reachable`. That run, on main 841fc4bb, showed the wrong cell words ("Not installed · Ask a server admin") fixed by 78fe803f; the new per-block words are game-free tested only. With 78fe803f, editing `TreatLocalServerAsRemote` false → true in the cfg while the tab was open switched the route to `remote (forced by TreatLocalServerAsRemote)` within 4 s (log `… is now true (read from the edited config file; no restart needed)`) and back to `local` after the reverse edit, with no reopen or hot swap.

**Live (2026-10-03, Steam client and isolated test server, branch fix/remote-followups 0d5f7256 then 7cf47563):** CastleLink's Client and Server cells start at the same x as every other row (1600 / 2048 at 3840×1080); with `TreatLocalServerAsRemote = true`, emptying the test server's adminlist switched the route to `not-admin` within 5 s without reopening the tab, and HotDemo's held Server cell showed a disabled `Admin only`; restoring the adminlist switched back to `remote … / none` within 5 s with `Uninstall`; no over-budget re-filter during either change; grayed cells read `No server side` (three client-only mods) and `No client side` (a server-only mod). **Not live-verified:** the no-retry refusal on `Not on Thunderstore` and its reset on rejoin, and Thunderstore unreachable.

**Live (2026-10-03, Steam client and isolated test server, Core client gen 19 / server gen 54, branch feat/treat-local-as-remote 6a64acbe):** with `TreatLocalServerAsRemote = true` the route read `remote (forced by TreatLocalServerAsRemote)` and logged once; an adminlist member was `authorized` without adminauth (by design); with the test server's adminlist emptied, the route read `none (forced …)` / `not-admin`, Server cells showed a dim `Admin only` (e.g. CastleLink) and the tab did not loop (0 rebuilds in 6 s; the c9ff12a0 loop is fixed); remote Uninstall of an already-marked HotDemo (bridge `remote_install`) logged on the server `trusted hotLoadable copy, unloaded now` and `Reload: unloaded Progenitor Hot Demo: clean`, client `finished: Removed: unloaded by the server's reloader`; remote Install of a plain BepInEx plugin package from Thunderstore staged `progenitor-pending\index\<plugin>.dll` (`Pending server restart`) and remote Cancel deleted it; remote Install of the local-index-only HotDemo was refused `NotThunderstore` (by design); no over-budget re-filter during a remote request (bug 2 fixed). **Not live-verified / open:** Thunderstore unreachable; `Admin only` is missing on installed rows (HotDemo showed `Installed 0.1.0` with no lock); the route does not re-check authorization until the tab rebuilds; a `Not on Thunderstore` server cell still offers Install; a row whose subtitle reads `No update tag · by …` (CastleLink) draws its side cells about 25 px left of the other rows.

Status: **source-checked, built (Debug and Release) and game-free tested; not live-verified.** Tests: `TreatLocalServerAsRemoteTests`.

`[Install] TreatLocalServerAsRemote` (client, `BepInEx/config/progenitor.cfg`, default `false`) is a test / advanced switch. Edits to the running game's cfg take effect without a restart: the route decision reads this one key off the file whenever the file's write time moved (at most once a second, a plain text read, no `ConfigFile.Reload`, no watcher), logs `Install: [Install] TreatLocalServerAsRemote is now …`, and the open tab follows within about a second. (REMOTEUNREACH1, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified. The first live check found that an edit reached the game only after a Core hot swap, because BepInEx reads the file once.)

When it is on and you are connected to the configured local dedicated server (same port, this machine, handshake ok), that server takes the **remote** route instead of the local folder:

- **Picker:** it still shows one `Connected: <name>` entry, and other server folders on this PC keep the local route.
- **Readback:** the picker readback's `route` is `remote (forced by TreatLocalServerAsRemote)`. `listing.route.effective` and the bridge's `remote_install status` (`route.effective`, `route.forcedRemote`) say the same, and `why` names the forced route.
- **No folder access:** the catalog stops seeing the local server root (`InstallCatalogService.ServerRootSuppressed`), so no plan, write, removal, marker, reconcile or scan touches that folder or its ledger. The client log says so once each time the switch starts forcing the remote route: `Install: route remote (forced by TreatLocalServerAsRemote) for the connected server …; the local folder … is not written.`
- **What the Server column uses:** the server's own rows (`remote_install` status rows, then the handshake). The admin rules apply: `Admin only` / `Authenticate as admin` until you are an authorized admin. Install, update, uninstall and cancel are remote-install requests; the server downloads from Thunderstore itself.
- **Not connected:** with the switch on, server management stays disabled and reads **Connect to manage**. Local folder inventory and actions are suppressed, including through the server picker. A confirmation opened before the disconnect is refused when confirmed, and a server request already in flight keeps its progress and **Cancel**. Turn the switch off to manage a configured local dedicated server from the title screen.

Every removal, mark, unmark and remote step now logs, as installs do:

- `Install: removed <key> (<side>): <files>`
- `Install: nothing removed for <key> (…)`
- `Install: marked for removal at the next start <key> …`
- `Install: unmarked (kept) <key> …`
- `Install: remote <Op> <key> <version> sent to the connected server (request n)`
- `Install: remote <Op> <key> finished: <Stage> (<Reason>): <detail>`

Live, a confirmed `Cancel installing <mod>?` used to log nothing.

**Live round 1 (2026-10-03, client gen 17 / server gen 52, c9ff12a0).**

Worked:

- the forced-route readback and its log line;
- remote Uninstall of HotDemo;
- a remote install of a plain BepInEx plugin package from Thunderstore (`RestartServerRequired`, pending copy, cell `Pending server restart`);
- a remote cancel (`Removed: the pending copy was deleted`).

**Live round 2 (PR #129, main d3a82445).** Verified:

- no rebuild loop;
- the Admin only lock;
- a hot remote uninstall of the already-marked HotDemo;
- a remote Thunderstore install and cancel of a plain BepInEx plugin package;
- no re-filter churn.

That pass left four open items. All are fixed now (REMOTEFOLLOW1; source-checked, built and game-free tested in `RemoteFollowupsTests`; not re-verified live):

- **Admin lock on held rows.** For a non-admin, a Server cell for a copy the server holds (installed, pending, removal pending) read `Installed 0.1.0` with no lock and no button. The handshake view carries no Uninstall of its own. It now shows the dim **Admin only** (or **Authenticate as admin**) like the actionable rows. A copy another tool manages gets no lock.
- **Authorization refresh.** The server only sends its status when asked, so a restored adminlist left the route `not-admin` until the tab was reopened. An open Install tab now re-asks every 4 s while the route is blocked by authorization (not admin, not allowed, no answer yet) and every 30 s otherwise (`RemoteInstallClient.RequestStatusIfDue`). The answer re-decides the route and refreshes the Server cells in place, or with one rebuild when the route changed.
- **No Install the server would refuse again.** A remote refusal that is a fact about the package or version (`Not on Thunderstore`, not found, version not found, not V Rising, bad package or version, plan refused; `InstallStatusLabels.WouldRepeat`) now reads muted with no Install button. Transient failures (unreachable, download failed, busy, rate limited) keep their retry. The refusal is dropped when the connection ends, so the next connection can try again.
- **Column offset.** The **Admin only** button's width included the lock glyph's room, but the state words' width did not subtract it. A row with that button was therefore about 25 px wider than its column, and the Mod cell shrank; CastleLink's Client and Server cells sat left of every other row. Both widths now come from one function (`InstallModsPane.SideButtonCellWidth` / `SideTextWidthFor`), and a side cell spans exactly its column for every button combination.

**Unreachable Thunderstore (live, after PR #130).** With the server's `ThunderstoreBaseUrl` pointed at a dead host, the route read `remote (forced…) / unreachable` correctly. The Server cells, though, read `Not installed · Ask a server admin` for an authorized admin. Every route block used that note. It is now each block's own words (`SideCells.BlockNote`): `Server can't reach Thunderstore`, `Remote installs off`, `No Progenitor on server`, `Checking server…`, and `Ask a server admin` only for not-admin / not-allowed. Installed rows keep their state (`Installed 1.0.0`, and Uninstall still works over an unreachable route). A three-part state such as `1.0.0 · update 2.0.0 · Server can't reach Thunderstore` now breaks at its last separator, so both lines fit (`InstallColumns.StatusLines`). REMOTEUNREACH1, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified.

Round 1 found three defects, now fixed and verified live in round 2:

- **Rebuild loop.** The Mods menu decided whether to rebuild its catalog by comparing `ServerBepInExRoot` with the configured root. While forced, that property is null, so every Install-tab build discarded the catalog and the tab alternated "Loading the package index…" / "The package index is not loaded." about ten times a second. It kept looping after the switch went off, because the route was only re-decided by a full list build, which never came. The comparison now uses `InstallCatalogService.BuiltFor` (the configured root in every route state).
- **Re-filter churn.** Every remote status or progress packet asked for a full rebuild, which re-filtered about 290 rows every 1–2 s. `NoteRemoteChanged` now rewrites the rows' cells in place. It rebuilds only when the route, the pending bar's counts, or a side cell's needed buttons changed. The readback count is `RemoteRefreshCounts`.
- **Server trust rule.** The server's remote Uninstall marked HotDemo for the next restart. It now applies the trusted-hotLoadable rule (`HotLoadTrust.CanUnloadHot`): a promoted copy in the server's `progenitor-reload/` whose bytes are a verified hotLoadable, clean ProgenitorPlugin is deleted, and the server's own reloader unloads it (`finished: Removed: unloaded by the server's reloader`). This also happens when an earlier request had already marked it. If the server never inspected that version, it downloads and inspects the package once for the check. A remote install of a trusted hotLoadable package was already the hot lane, through the server's own admission.

### Players joining (JOINADMIN1)

Status: **source-checked, built and game-free tested (`JoinAdminTests`, `InstallSimpleViewTests`); not live-verified in its new place.** The Install tab has **no** Players joining button (decided 2026-10-04: "more like admin stuff"; it was a toolbar chip, cell `joinAdmin`). The entry point is a row on Progenitor's own mod page (Mods → Required → Progenitor), under **Server settings** · **Mods players need**: **Players joining this server** with the button **Open** (`JoinServerSettings.PlayersJoiningLabel`). It shows only for an admin of the joined server, the rule the toolbar button used (`JoinAdminClient.IsAdmin`): the server said it has the panel (its hello after the handshake) and this player is on its admin list or `adminauth`ed. The row is re-checked when the page is built and, on an open page, whenever the hello, a panel answer or the server's remote-install status arrives (`ModsMenu.NoteSettingsScopeChanged`). While the server has the panel but the rule says no, the page asks the server for a fresh remote-install status on the Install tab's 4 s blocked beat (the Install tab used to keep that status fresh while it was open) and logs its decision with the inputs the first time the row is evaluated and whenever they change: `JoinAdmin: Players joining row considered: shown True (serverSupports True, helloAdmin True, remote-install status none)`. The row is on Progenitor's **mod page** (`mods_ui select` with `page` `overview`), under **Mods players need** (settings organization stage 5); the separate raw settings pane (`page=settings`) lists config entries only and never shows a page's buttons. Settings search matches a button row by its caption and help, not by its row label; the help names "Players joining", so searching that finds it. **Open** opens the [Players joining this server](join-flow.md#players-joining-admins-stage-3) popup unchanged: recommended client mods, the install window, and the refused-join alert (the shares for refused players were removed 2026-10-05). A non-admin never sees the row. The `join_admin` bridge op ([bridge-ops-join.md](bridge-ops-join.md#join_admin)) opens and drives the popup as before.

The alert's **Update on server** is this tab's own server update: `InstallModsPane.ServerUpdateFor(pluginId, start)` finds the mod's listing (`FindEntryForPlugin`), offers it only when the row's Server cell has **Update**, and on press clears the filters, searches for the listing and runs `OnSideClick(key, server: true, Update)`, which opens the same confirmation as the row's button. The recommend picker searches this tab's catalog (client-only listings by `PackageSideDerivation`). Readback: the panel through `join_admin read` ([bridge-ops-join.md](bridge-ops-join.md#join_admin)); the row on Progenitor's page through `mods_ui widget_read`.

### Per-side status (both-sided mods)

Status: **source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified.** One decision (`SideStatusRules.Decide`) gives each listing's two halves; the Install row and the mod page head block both use it, so they never disagree.

- **Client half:** installed, pending game restart, removal pending or absent (the client ledger and inventory).
- **Server half:** installed, pending server restart, removal pending, absent or unknown, from the first source that applies: the local server folder's ledger (when the local route applies, or a local server is configured and no remote route is in use), the connected server's remote-install rows (authorized players; they include pending), the connected server's handshake list (loaded mods only), else unknown.
- **Needs both** comes only from a manifest-verified `side: both` declaration — the mod page's required-server rule. A listing Thunderstore merely tags Server + Client shows its sides in the readback as information, with no warning.

For a declared both-sided mod the Status column reads `Missing on server` (button **Install on server** when a server route exists; otherwise no button and the drawer says the route block's own words (`Ask a server admin` only for not-admin / not-allowed; `Server can't reach Thunderstore`, `Remote installs off`, `No Progenitor on server`, `Checking server…`) or `No server to install to`), `Missing on client` (**Install on client**: the normal client install), `Installed`, `Both pending restart`, the pending label of the side still waiting, `Server: unknown` (muted, no warning) or `Version mismatch` (**Update server** / **Update client** for the older side). Uninstall of such a row removes every half a route reaches, and the confirmation names them (`Removes it from this client and the server (remote).`) and any side it cannot reach (`Left in place: the server (no route to it from here).`).

The mod page warns both ways: the existing `(!) This mod needs its server component…` when the server half is missing, and `(!) The server has this mod and this client does not…` when the client half is. Its server evidence includes the connected server's remote-install rows.

Readback: Install rows gain `sides {client, server, serverSource: local|remote|handshake|unknown, needsBoth, mismatch, label, action}`.

## Mods list (MODLIST1)

**Status: source-checked and game-free tested (`ModStage3Tests`, `ModsProvenanceTests`, `ModPageTests`), not live-verified.** The list column follows the coordinator's mockup approved 2026-09-29. Decisions are in `ModsListLayout` (game-free); the drawing is `UnityUiRuntime`'s.

- **An entry** is one two-line cell row (`mod:<plugin id>`, 48 px): a status **diamond**, the mod's name left-aligned over a muted **second line**, and an **Update** tag at the right when the Install tab's listing is newer than the installed version (the same check as the page's `Update available` line; Progenitor-installed mods only).
- **Works across versions** (C5; the icon built and game-free tested 2026-10-10, not live-verified): a mod whose declared `Peers` range admits other versions has a small muted loop-arrows icon right after the version on its plain `version · sides` line (`1.3.0 ⟲ · Client and server`); never on a problem or state line, and none when even the version would be cut. Hovering it describes it in the page's column. Rules, layout and readback: [Works across versions and For developers](#works-across-versions-and-for-developers-c5).
- **The diamond** is a small square turned 45 degrees, drawn from images (the game font has no U+25C6, and a non-WGL4 glyph drew as TMP's missing-glyph box live): green filled — running, no problems; amber filled — a warning or a conflict (also a missing server half); red filled — failed to load or another critical problem; gray filled — turned off by the player (or turning off at the next start); a **hollow outline** — not running now, and not the player's doing (paused for this server, resuming, waiting for a restart to load, turning on at the next start, only on the server). Colors are the existing green, amber, red and muted gray.
- **The second line** is `version · sides` (`1.0.19 · Client`, `Version unknown` when none is known), replaced by the problem or the state in words when there is one: `Failed to load`, `Key F9 also used by ClanResourceHUD`, `Loaded with problems`, `Paused for this server`, `Disabled`, `Turns off at next start`, `Pending restart · to load`, `Needs its server part`. The words are the page's status words (both are composed by `ModFrameworkLine` from the same facts, `ModsMenu.FrameworkFactsFor`), so the list, the status bar and the dialogs never disagree; color is never the only signal.
- **The selected entry** has the slim accent bar at its left edge and a soft accent-tinted fill; hover is a subtle light tint (`CellRowSpec.Soft`). The solid gold fill and the near-black hover are gone.
- **The top of the column**, pinned under the search field: the summary line `9 mods · 2 need attention` (the player's mods; attention counts every local entry with a warning or a critical problem; the second half is left out when none do), then the filter chips **All** / **Needs attention** / **Disabled** (the chosen one in the accent). A chip rebuilds the list; the filter is kept while the workspace is open.
- **Groups** have headings: `Installed 9` (not collapsible), `On this server 2 ►` and `Required by Progenitor 10 ►` (collapsible, collapsed by default as before; the marker turns when expanded). A heading is a cell row (`group-installed` / `group-server` / `group-required`); `widget_click pane=list label=group-required` toggles it.
- **Readback** (`mods_ui widget_read pane=list`): an entry carries `modsListEntry {id, name, group, diamond (running|warning|failed|disabled|waiting), diamondShape (filled|hollow), diamondTone, secondLine, needsAttention, disabled, update, selected, align: "left"}`; a heading `modsListGroup {group, caption, count, collapsible, expanded}` (the server and required headings also `captionLegacy`, MODS2's `On this server (N)` / `Required (N)`); the summary `modsListSummary {text, mods, attention, filter}`; the chips `modsListFilter {filter, options}`. `mods_ui status` `entries[]` keeps every earlier field and adds `diamond`, `secondLine`, `needsAttention`, `disabled`, `update` and `passesFilter`; `marker` and `warning` still report the missing-server-half warning, which the list now shows as an amber diamond and `Needs its server part`. A row is clicked by its key (`widget_click pane=list label=mod:<id>`), no longer by its caption.
- **The join screens use the same switch** (JOINTURNON): a mod the player turned off here that a server requires (or recommends) is offered **Turn on** on the join screens instead of a download, and that press, the bridge's `join_ui mod_switch`, and an install of a turned-off Progenitor mod all go through `ModSwitch.Set` and the same enable state, so the list shows the change afterwards. A mod paused for the joined server is not "turned off" there. Source-checked and game-free tested; not live-verified ([join-flow.md](join-flow.md#10-installed-but-turned-off-jointurnon)).
- **Planned, not built:** game UI sounds, transitions. Keyboard and controller navigation: [NAV1](ui.md#controller-and-keyboard-navigation-nav1) (verified live at the title screen, not in a world).

## Framework plugins and reload-folder mods (FWALLOW1)

**Status: source-checked, built and game-free tested (`FrameworkAllowlistTests`, `ModsProvenanceTests`), not live-verified.** User report, 2026-10-06, Mods → "Server-led sample": "I can't uninstall it, I think because it is test and registered as required." `ModMenuInventory.IsFrameworkId` used to treat every id under `progenitor.*` as a framework part, so the samples sat in `Required by Progenitor` with "Part of Progenitor. Required; not removable from this menu." and no Uninstall.

- **Required is an explicit allowlist.** `ModMenuInventory.FrameworkIds` is exactly `progenitor` (Core; Bootstrap carries the same GUID), `progenitor.dev` (the dev bridge) and `progenitor.preloader` (the patcher), compared exactly and case-insensitively. A test pins that it names the same plugins as the switch's refusal list (`ModEnableState.RequiredIds`, which adds the loader's `bepinex`) and the handshake's always-allowed list (`ClientModJudge.ProgenitorIds`). Neither of those reads the menu's list, so the handshake, required-by-server and client-policy logic do not depend on this classification. Only the Mods list asked `IsFrameworkId` (in `BuildSidebar` and `LedgerOnlyRows`); both now get the exact test.
- **Everything else is a normal mod**, even under `progenitor`: samples, examples, apivalidation, exercise and test plugins. The ledger is still asked first (MODSPOLISH1), so a ledgered install stays `progenitor`. A plugin with no ledger row whose DLL is a top-level file in `BepInEx/progenitor-reload` is `reload` (`ModProvenance.Reload`). `ReloadFolderPlugins.Map` reads each DLL's `[BepInPlugin]` GUIDs from metadata without loading it, cached by path, size and write time, once per list build. A GUID that two files declare is left out, so nothing is offered for it. Other rows fall through to `server` / `other` as before.
- **A `reload` row** is listed under **Installed**, with the subtitle word `progenitor-reload`. The switch is the hot-loaded plugin's (`ModOwner.Progenitor`, Bootstrap's suspend), and the page offers **Uninstall**. Its provenance line is `Example mod shipped with Progenitor's source.` for an id under `progenitor.sample*` or one containing `example`, otherwise `Installed in BepInEx/progenitor-reload.` It never says "Part of Progenitor".
- **Uninstall for a `reload` row** opens the usual prompt with its own body: `Installed outside Progenitor's Install tab. Deletes <file>.dll from BepInEx/progenitor-reload; it unloads now, nothing to restart. Your settings for it are kept.` When the DLL declares other plugins, the body adds `The same file also holds <ids>, which go with it.` **Yes, uninstall** calls `ReloadFolderPlugins.Remove`, not the ledger seam. It scans the folder again and deletes only the one DLL that declares the plugin. It refuses when no file or more than one file declares the plugin, when the file differs from the one the page was built with, when the path is not a plain `.dll` directly in the reload folder (subfolders, `..` and links are refused), or when the plugin or anything else in that file is a framework id. Bootstrap's reloader sees the file gone at its next scan and unloads the plugin with the `Removal` reason, which also ends a pause. The page note is `Removed`, or the refusal sentence.
- **Core, Bootstrap, the dev bridge and the preloader stay protected.** Their rows are required with no action, their switch is refused, and `ReloadFolderPlugins.Remove` refuses them even when a copy sits in the reload folder.
- **Readback** (`mods_ui status`, `entries[]`) adds `framework` (true for the loader and the allowlist), `removable` (the page offers Uninstall; for another tool's ordinary plugin, its files are identified), `provenanceLine` (the page's muted note, the same line for an ordinary plugin: see [mod-settings.md](mod-settings.md#ordinary-bepinex-plugins)) and `reloadFile` (the DLL path for a `reload` row, else empty). `provenance` gains the value `reload`.
- **Not changed:** the preloader's ordinary-plugin rule (`PluginFileActions.Identify` still refuses any id starting with `progenitor` with "It is part of Progenitor or BepInEx."). It covers only DLLs in `BepInEx/plugins` that the next-start switch would rename, not reload-folder plugins.

### Live check (not run)

Use SampleCompanion, since SampleServerLed was moved aside to `<tools>\tsrepro\serverled-aside`. Deploy this branch's Core first, after checking the client is not joining (no hot swap while a `Client_*` world exists), and confirm `Progenitor.SampleCompanion.dll` is in the client's `BepInEx/progenitor-reload`.

1. `mods_ui open`, then `mods_ui status`. In `entries[]`, `progenitor.samplecompanion` reads `group: mods`, `provenance: reload`, `framework: false`, `removable: true`, `provenanceLine: "Example mod shipped with Progenitor's source."` and `reloadFile` ending `progenitor-reload\Progenitor.SampleCompanion.dll`. `progenitor` and `progenitor.dev` read `group: required`, `framework: true`, `removable: false`. `status.mods.required` counts only the loader, Core and the dev bridge.
2. `widget_click pane=list label=mod:progenitor.samplecompanion`, then `widget_read pane=content`. The provenance row is the example line, `page-framework.switch` is `visible: true`, `interactive: true`, `mode: live`, and the action row is `Uninstall`.
3. Click the switch (`page-framework/switch`) to turn it off: the diamond goes gray and the log shows Bootstrap's suspend. Click it again to turn it on: the plugin loads again.
4. Click **Uninstall**. Read the prompt body: it says `Installed outside Progenitor's Install tab` and names `Progenitor.SampleCompanion.dll`. Click **No, keep it** once, and confirm the file is still there. Then click **Uninstall** and **Yes, uninstall**: the page note reads `Removed`, the file is gone from `progenitor-reload`, and the log shows `Reload: Progenitor.SampleCompanion.dll removed, unloading (unload reason=Removal)`. Within about two seconds, the entry leaves the list.
5. Open Progenitor's row and the dev bridge's row: no action row, and the switch reads `mode: refused`.
6. Restore: copy `Progenitor.SampleCompanion.dll` back into `progenitor-reload` (it loads on the next scan), close the workspace, and leave the client at the title screen.

### Local test listings on the remote route (LOCALONLY1)

**Status: built and game-free tested (`InstallSidesPaneTests.A_local_test_listing_installs_on_this_client_only_on_the_remote_route`), not live-verified.** Live 2026-10-07: a both-sides listing that only this PC's local test source carries (`[Install] LocalIndexPath`) sent its server half to the connected server, which refused it as not on Thunderstore, and nothing reached this client. On the remote route such a listing now installs this client's copy only (the install's target is the client whatever `[Install] DefaultTarget` says), never offers **Install on both**, and never sends a server install; its Server cell reads `Local test source only: the server can't fetch it`, and the drawer says why. On the local-folder route nothing changes (the folder is written from this PC). A server half that fails never undoes a client half already written: the client half is written first.

**The server's own local test source (F16, live 2026-10-07; built and game-free tested in `InstallSidesPaneTests`, `RemoteInstallServiceTests`, not live-verified).** When the connected server's own `[Install] LocalIndexPath` lists the package, its status says so (a `local:<key>` row with that index's version). The Server cell then offers Install or Update as for any listing, and the drawer reads `The server installs it from its own local test source (<version>).` The request carries the version that index has. `Local test source only: the server can't fetch it` stays for a listing the server does not offer. A client-only listing (its side read from its zip, LOCALSIDE1) has no server side and never reads that line.

### Local test listings: side before inspection (LOCALSIDE1)

**Status: built and game-free tested (`InstallHotLoadTests`: `A_local_listing_takes_its_zips_declared_side_before_any_inspection`, `A_local_zip_without_a_readable_side_leaves_the_listings_own_signals`, `A_nested_progenitor_json_is_not_read_as_the_packages_side`, `A_rewritten_local_zip_is_read_again_and_an_inspection_still_wins`), not live-verified.** Live 2026-10-07: server-only local packages (ReloadExample, TimedBuffExample) and client-only ones listed both sides and offered **Install on both** until the package was inspected, because the local index's `Client` + `Server` category tags were the only signal.

- When the local index is read (`LocalIndexSource.Load`), each listing whose `download_url` is a zip under the folder takes the `side` that zip's root `progenitor.json` declares. The read uses the inspector's own rules (`PackageInspector.ReadDeclaredSide`: zip-root entry only, the inspector's JSON size cap, its `progenitor.json` parser) and checks nothing else.
- It never throws. A zip that is missing, larger than 64 MiB or unreadable, or that has no `progenitor.json`, no `side` or a malformed one, leaves the listing's own signals (categories, then the one-liner; see `PackageSideDerivation`).
- Results are cached by zip path, size and last write time, so a rebuilt package is read again on the next index load.
- Precedence is unchanged. The zip's side is only the listing's starting value: a verified-manifest record from an earlier inspection replaces it, and so does an inspection made this session, including after a re-merge.

### Local test listings: README per package, and the page title (F11)

**Status: built and game-free tested (`InstallHotLoadTests`: `Two_local_listings_in_one_folder_each_show_their_own_readme`, `A_local_zip_without_a_readme_is_unavailable_when_the_folder_lists_several`, `A_rebuilt_local_zip_serves_its_new_readme`; `HeadBlockTests.A_short_title_gets_the_rest_of_its_row_not_its_estimate`), not live-verified.** Live 2026-10-07, two packages served from one local folder: ReloadExample's Mods page showed TimedBuffExample's README, and its title read `ReloadExamp…`.

- **README.** Every local listing used to read the one `README.md` beside `index.json`. `tools/make-dummy-package.ps1 -Append` overwrites that file with the last package's README. A local listing's README and changelog now come from its own zip: the zip-root `README.md` and `CHANGELOG.md`, each capped at 256 KiB (`LocalIndexSource.DocumentsOfZip`). The read is cached by zip path, size and last write time. The files beside `index.json` are used only when the index lists a single package. Otherwise a document the zip lacks is unavailable (`LocalDocumentUnavailable`).
- **Same text on every route.** `InstallCatalogService.TryGetCachedListingDocuments` answers a local key from the zip too, so the Mods page (`ModsMenu.ReadmeFor`) and the Install drawer never fall back to a disk-cached README for a local package. The pane writes the correct text over any cached copy the next time it adopts the README.
- **Title.** The head block's title cell (`HeadBlockLayout.Build`, used by the Mods page head) was an auto cell. That width is the `AverageGlyphEm` caption estimate, about 247 px for `ReloadExample` at the 34 px display size, which is narrower than the game font draws it. TMP therefore ellipsized the name although the row had plenty of room. This was not a deliberate narrow header. The title now fills the row up to the status column. Its floor is its estimate, capped at 55% of the content (`HeadBlockLayout.TitleFloor`), so on a narrow pane the status cells shrink first, as before. At both detail-pane fixtures a 13-character name gets more than 1.5 times its estimate.

## Uninstall on each side (SRVUNINST1)
