The background is a native icon's **plain tile** image when an icon keeps its tile and its glyph in separate images (one image with a sprite covering the icon, another smaller one; `TitleLogsTile.PickBackground`, by component reference: `note: native background <object>`); otherwise the tile draws its own white rounded rectangle (`RoundedTile`, 64 px with a 16 px corner, 9-sliced so the corner is 16% of the tile height, about 7.5 px of the live 48: `note: drawn background`), tinted that blue; a borrowed native tile keeps its own color. Live 2026-10-10 the first version borrowed the first icon's own image and drew the mail icon's envelope behind the zip: the icons are single sprites with the glyph baked in. Hover shows `Collect logs for a help post` in the game's own tooltip box, as the official icons do (user, 2026-10-10): each hovered frame the tile calls `TooltipManagerComponent.SetTooltip(text, position, BottomLeft)` at its position plus the first native icon's `LinkButton.TooltipOffset`, the call and placement `LinkButton.Update` makes for theirs; the game's manager draws the box and clears it on every frame nobody sets it. If that call throws, the hover text goes back to the line above the row. Verified live 2026-10-10: the tooltip was checked by eye on the title screen (it matches the official icons' box and placement), and the tile's blue was sampled at rgb(31, 50, 70) against the icons' rgb(34, 55, 75). A click runs Collect logs, and the line above the row shows its progress and then the outcome for 8 s (`TitleLogsTile.Line`). The outcome is shortened for this spot (`TitleLogsTile.ResultText`): after a save `Logs saved to your Desktop (1.1 MB).` (or the support folder, or a configured folder's last segment; no file name, never a path) and, only when it fits, a second line `Drag the zip into your #technical-support post.`; a failure or refusal is the status's first line alone, already redacted. The line is centered over the tile, sized from each line's measured single-line width in the final font and size (live 2026-10-10 a width read too small cut the line to `...to your Deskto...`), cut with an ellipsis only when a line is wider than the 16:9 content (`TitleLogsTile.FitLines`), and moved back inside the game's 16:9 content when it would cross it (`TitleLogsTile.CenterOverTile`, `ContentSpan`: on 5120×1440 the content is x 1280..3840), 6 units above the row (user, 2026-10-10: left-aligned with the row it read as the row's line); its allowance is twice the row's width (at most 900 units, 520 when the row cannot be read), in the kit's body font at 17 px with no extra letter spacing, muted; it shows two lines when they fit under the menu's last entry (EXIT's drawn glyphs, the rect the MODS entry's clearance measures), else one (`TitleLogsTile.PlaceLine`). Live 2026-10-10 the first version was the full status on one letter-spaced line, right-aligned to the tile, and ran about 1000 px left into the pillarbox at 5120×1440. Clicks and hover go through the Bootstrap slot table (`UiSlotTable`), never input polling. Its `Button` takes the native link buttons' navigation mode (read from the first native `Selectable` in the row; `Explicit` and none found become `None`), so pad navigation of the title menu is unchanged; readback `navigation`, `nativeNavigation`. Its size comes from the first native icon: the rect size and `LayoutElement` sizes on an axis that does not stretch, else that icon's `sizeDelta` and its own `LayoutElement` only (`TitleLogsTile.LayoutAsk`). The tile lives and dies with the MODS entry: dropped on every `Drop` and Core unload, rediscovered with the title view, swept as an owned root (the sweep maps the name to this generation's tile, so it keeps its own and destroys an older generation's leftover; the result line is a child of the tile), hidden with the title hold, and ignored by the MODS entry's icon-row clearance (`TryReadIconRow`).

Readback: `mods_ui` with `scene` returns `logsTile`: `present`, `note`, `rect` (client pixels, top-left origin), `center` (for a focus-free `click_at`), `activeInHierarchy`, `siblingIndex`/`siblings`, `tint`/`glyphTint`, `background` (`sprite`, `type`, `pixelsPerUnitMultiplier`), `hovered`, `line`, `lineShown`, `linePlace` (left, bottom, width, lines, EXIT's bottom), `lineRect` and `lineSpacing` while the line shows, and `icons`: each native icon measured, with its `children` names and its `images` (`path` under the icon, `sprite`, `spriteW`/`spriteH`, `spriteBorder`, `type`, `w`/`h`, `color`, `active`/`enabled`), so the row's art can be read without a capture.

Status: source-checked, built and game-free tested (`SupportLandingAndTileTests`: name, legacy retention, README, Discord open, the line rule, the glyph), 2026-10-10. Not live-verified: the tile's placement, sprite clone, hover tooltip and click on the title screen.

**Server.** Admins type [`.progenitor logs`](commands.md#progenitor-logs-server-logs).

**Server logs for admins (`'PrSL'`).** When this client is on a Progenitor server (handshake ok) and is an admin there (`JoinAdminRows.IsAdmin`, the admin channel's own answer), Get help shows **Include this server's logs** (`[Support] IncludeServerLogs`, on by default). With it on, **Collect logs** first asks the server for its bundle, then builds the client's zip with the server's zip merged entry by entry under `server/`, then reveals and copies it as usual. While it waits, **Last bundle** reads `Asking the server for its logs...`, `The server is collecting its logs...` and `Getting server logs... 1.2 / 3.4 MB`. The server part never stops the client's bundle: when it is missing, the zip is still written, summary.txt says `Server logs: not included: <reason>` and the status line ends `Server logs not included: <reason>.`

- **Capability.** The server declares `Handshake.Capability.ServerLogs` (bit `4194304`) on every handshake request and refresh; the client notes it at each join (`ServerLogsCaps`) and asks only a server that declared it. An older server is answered at once as `this server's Progenitor is too old to send its logs`; a server that declared it but never answers is given up after 15 s with the same meaning.
- **Authorization.** The server re-reads the sender's `User` and allows the admin list, `adminauth` or the private game's host (`ServerAdminList.AuthorizeShared`, the rule of `.progenitor logs` and remote installs); anyone else gets a refusal, `you are not an admin of this server`. One transfer per admin at a time (`the server is already sending you its logs`) and at least 10 s between an admin's requests. Every refusal is answered (the receive gate already limits a sender's packets), so the client always says why; only the log line is rate-limited per sender like the receive gate's drop lines, one a minute, and the next line counts the ones held back (`refused: NotAdmin (50 more refused since the last report)`).
- **What crosses the wire.** Only the server's own bundle, built on the server by the same code as `.progenitor logs` (the server's redactor, the server's exclusions), capped at 20 MB by halving the server's per-log cap. Nothing about other players beyond what that bundle already keeps redacted. The server also keeps that zip in its own `BepInEx/progenitor-support`.
- **Wire.** One reserved id, `'PrSL'` = `0x5072534C` (`ServerLogsWire`), both directions, each packet at most the 15 KiB custom-event payload: a version byte (1), a kind byte, the transfer id (int32 LE). Client to server: Request (1), Ack (2, + int32 chunks received in order), Cancel (3). Server to client: Accepted (10), Start (11, + int32 length, int32 chunk count, 32-byte SHA-256), Chunk (12, + int32 index, then up to 15,350 bytes), Refuse (13, + code byte, UTF-8 reason of at most 400 bytes; codes: not-admin 1, busy 2, rate-limited 3, failed 4, too-large 5, cancelled 6). Anything else is dropped.
- **Pacing.** The server sends at most 2 packets every 0.1 s per transfer, 20 packets a second (about 300 KB/s): the same per-connection envelope Progenitor's server receive gate allows in the other direction (`[Networking] ReceivePacketsPerSecond`, default 20, of up to 15 KiB). At most 40 chunks (about 600 KB) go out ahead of the client's last Ack; the client acks every 8 chunks, again after 1 s without a new chunk, and three times at the end, 0.5 s apart (a few packets a second toward the server at most, well under that gate). A typical 1 to 3 MB bundle takes 3 to 10 s, 20 MB about 70 s.
- **Loss.** A packet the transport does not take (`SendToUser` false) is not counted as sent and goes again on the next pump. When the window is full (or everything was sent) and no Ack advanced for 2 s, the server sends again from the last acknowledged chunk (go-back-N; the client ignores chunks it has), so one lost chunk or Ack recovers in seconds rather than ending at the 30 s stall. The server completes on the first Ack that covers every chunk.
- **Integrity.** The client allocates only after a Start within the 20 MB cap whose chunk count matches its length, places each chunk by index (a wrong-sized chunk ends the fetch), and uses the zip only when its length and SHA-256 match. Both hashes are computed off the main thread: the server's on the build worker before it hands the zip to the sender, the client's by the bundle build on its worker. The client reads the server's zip once per build, at most 500 entries and 80 MB uncompressed (past either cap it stops and summary.txt says so). Every build writes a uniquely named temp file and moves it to the first free bundle name, so a `.progenitor logs` and a `'PrSL'` build at the same moment never collide.
- **Ending.** The server drops a transfer when its admin disconnects (re-read each pump), after 30 s without Ack progress, after 120 s of building, on the admin's Cancel and on Core unload; except for a disconnected admin it then sends a best-effort Refuse (cancelled) with the reason, so the client stops waiting and its line says `the server stopped the transfer: <reason>`. The client gives up after 15 s without an answer, 150 s of building or 30 s without a chunk (and sends Cancel), when it leaves the server, and on Core unload (Cancel sent; then nothing is built and the line reads `Collect logs stopped: Progenitor is unloading.`). A request the client cannot send ends the fetch at once. Each fetch ends exactly once.
- **Server log.** One line per request and per outcome, naming the admin by the last four digits of the SteamID only: `Support: server logs for admin …1234 requested; building the bundle.`, `… sent: <bytes> bytes in <n> chunks, <s> s.`, `… refused: NotAdmin`, `… dropped: the admin disconnected.`

Status: source-checked, built and game-free tested (`SupportNamesAndDiagnosisTests`: learned and known player names, the formats, the stop-list, 2,000 names over 4 MB, each evidence type, thin evidence, shared namespaces, 16 MB, the README guide and summary; `ServerLogsTransferTests`: framing, reassembly, pacing and window, a refused send, a lost chunk, lost Acks and a lost final Ack, the repeated final Ack, the refusal rate limit, SHA-256 mismatch in the transfer and in the build, the server zip's entry and size caps, concurrent builds, admin refusal, busy and rate limit, the 20 MB cap on both sides, cancel, disconnect, stall, unload, the too-old path, the merge under `server/`), 2026-10-10. **Live (2026-10-10, Steam client joined to the isolated test server at 127.0.0.1:9976, both on branch commit 2aad9e52d, the client's account on that save's admin list; clicks through bridge `mods_ui`):** in a world, **Include this server's logs** showed under Get help, on; **Collect logs** wrote one 683 KB zip and **Last bundle** read `… The server's logs are inside, under server/.`; the server logged `Support: server logs for admin …7076 requested; building the bundle.` and `sent: 193078 bytes in 13 chunks, 1.5 s.`; `server/` held 16 files (its BepInEx, audit, `-logFile` and settings files) and summary.txt read `Server logs: included under server/ (16 files, 189 KB as sent; redacted by the server).`; a scan of the unpacked zip found no SteamID in any form, character name, Windows user or profile path, computer name or IP address other than 127.0.0.1. The server's own summary named Progenitor from its one session's startup errors (no older session to compare with); that error is removed on main by the system-index fix. **Not live-verified:** a non-admin's refusal, an older server, loss recovery on a real connection, a transfer interrupted by leaving or a Core swap, and a bundle near the 20 MB cap.

Status: source-checked, built (`dotnet build Progenitor.sln -c Debug`) and game-free tested (`SupportRedactorTests`, `SupportBundleTests`, `SessionLogFilesTests`, `ProgenitorPageTests`), 2026-10-10. **Live (2026-10-10, Steam client at the title screen, branch claude/progenitor-log-collection-a881e5, commits e9a54de98 to the diagnosis fixes; clicks through bridge `mods_ui widget_click`):** the preloader's `session.log` matched `LogOutput.log` line for line after a restart (preloader lines once, header first); after the client was force-killed at the title screen, the next start rotated it to `session-1.log` ending on the killed session's last line, and a later start kept it as `session-2.log`; **Collect logs** wrote the zip to the (OneDrive-synced) Desktop in about a second (365–441 KB) and **Last bundle** read `Saved … and opened its folder; its path is on the clipboard. …`; the running isolated test server was collected as `local-server-1/` (its BepInEx, audit and `-logFile` logs); a scan of the unpacked zip found no SteamID64, `{Steam N}` id, Steam persona name, character name, Windows user or profile path, computer name or IP address other than 127.0.0.1. Two defects found live and fixed the same day: the persona and character names (then the `{Steam N}` form) were left in, and four startup errors present in every session named Progenitor as likely involved; the final zip reads `No mod stands out.` and suggests `Unknown mod`. **Not live-verified:** that Explorer opened with the zip selected and the clipboard held its path (not observed; the click was focus-free), **Save bundles in** with a custom or unwritable folder, the hosted server's logs, and the server command.

**Acceptance procedure — not run.**

1. Deploy Core, Bootstrap and `Progenitor.Preloader.dll` (to `BepInEx/patchers`) to the client; start it twice. Expect `BepInEx/progenitor-logs/session.log` and `session-1.log`, each starting with the header line and carrying the same lines as that session's `LogOutput.log`, preloader lines included once.
2. At the title screen open Mods → Progenitor → Get help → **Collect logs**. Expect the zip on the Desktop, Explorer with it selected, the path on the clipboard and the **Last bundle** line above; open the zip and check the contents and redaction listed here against the real logs.
3. Click **Collect logs** six more times; expect seven zips on the Desktop (none deleted). Set **Save bundles in** to a folder that cannot be written; collect; expect the zip in `BepInEx/progenitor-support` and the line naming why. Clear the setting and delete the test zips.
4. Host a private game, collect again; expect `hosted-server/`.
5. Start the isolated test server, join it, collect; expect `local-server-1/` with its logs and `this client is connected to it` in summary.txt.
6. On the isolated test server, as an admin (admin list or `adminauth`), with **Include this server's logs** on, click **Collect logs**; expect the progress line, then a zip with `server/` holding the server's bundle and the server's log lines `Support: server logs for admin …<last four> requested` and `… sent`. As a player who is not an admin, expect no row. Against a server without this build, expect the client's zip and `Server logs not included: this server's Progenitor is too old to send its logs.`
7. On the isolated test server, as an admin, type `.progenitor logs`; expect the two replies and the zip under the server's `BepInEx/progenitor-support`. As a non-admin expect the `.progenitor` usage line.

## Where Progenitor's settings are drawn

The spec and its decisions are [settings-organization.md](settings-organization.md); the current placement is its [Three homes (2026-10-06)](settings-organization.md#three-homes-2026-10-06) section. This section describes what is built. **Status: built and game-free tested (`ThreeHomesModelTests`, `ThreeHomesMenuTests`, `ConfigurationOrganizationTests`, `ProgenitorPageTests`, `ServerSettingsPageTests`); not live-verified.**

Each setting has exactly one home, on one of three tabs:

| Tab | What it answers | Groups |
|---|---|---|
| **Preferences** (was Configuration; after Keybinds) | How the Mods window, and Progenitor's filter in the game's Join Game browser, work on this PC. Nothing here changes a server. | Mods tab, Install Mods tab, Sources and downloads, Join Game, Local server |
| **Mods** → a mod's page | What the mod does. | Progenitor's page: Your settings (Keys, Photo mode, Build menu, Equipment, Chat, Private games, Get help) and On this server. A mod author's page: its own Your settings and Server settings, unchanged. |
| **Server** (the last tab) | How the server you're on is set up. | When players join, Mods players need, Server list, Server rules, Players without Progenitor, Operator |

**One stored value, many places (built, game-free tested in `SettingPlacesTests`, not live-verified).** `Ui/SettingPlaces.cs` is the table of Progenitor's own settings: each one's setting id, its home, its mirrors, its store and its file and key. A mirror never holds a copy: every place reads and writes the same `ConfigEntry` (or, for an admin-channel setting, the server's state over `'PrJa'`). The homes are `preferences`, `server-tab/<group>` and `progenitor-page/<group>`; the mirrors `install-toolbar`, `keybinds-tab` and `players-joining-panel`; `cfg` is not drawn.

- **Readback.** Every settings row carries `settingId`, `home` and `alsoAt: [place…]`, with `value` read from the store when the row is read. These rows are every `page-setting` (any page or tab), every Keybinds-tab `keys-row` of a mod key, and the Install tab's `view` readback (`[Mods] InstallView`). A mod author's row is at home on `mod-page/<pluginId>`; its keybinds are also at `keybinds-tab`.
- **`mods_ui settings_places`.** It returns `{settingId: {home, places[], value, display, known, store, source}}` for every placed setting of Progenitor's own, each read from its store once. It is a light read (no workspace status). Settings that stay in their cfg file are left out, except the ones the 2026-10-06 trim hid (below): those read `home cfg`, `places []`, `cfgOnly true` and `hidden` (the date and the reason).

**Settings kept in the cfg file only (decided 2026-10-06: "No user will care to change those").** No menu draws these; each keeps its key, default and behavior, and a search does not find them. **Reset all** acts on drawn rows only, so it leaves them as the file has them. Shared `progenitor.cfg`: `[Workspace] BackgroundIntensity`, `[Workspace] BackgroundContrast`, `[Ui] AdminAuthButton`, `[Install] CurrentUpdateTag`. Core's `progenitor.ui.cfg`: `[Ui] MenuHistoryInnerTabs`, `[Ui] CaptureMode`, `[Keybinds] EnforceGameWins`. The read-only Nexus Mods note is gone from Sources and downloads. Details: [settings-organization.md](settings-organization.md#trim-2026-10-06).

### Preferences

Top to bottom:

1. **Preferences** as the game's section header with its red rule, then the purpose line: `How this Mods window works on this PC. What a mod does is on its page in Mods; how the server you're on is set up is in Server.` (readback `preferences-purpose`), and the row **Mod settings and the server's setup** with **Mods »** and **Server »** (`link-purpose`, cells `mods` and `server`; readback `page-link {targets: [mods, server-tab], landing: top}`).
2. **Mods tab** (a subheading; called Look and navigation until the 2026-10-06 trim): **Edit other mods' settings** (`[Settings] EditOtherModsSettings`, ui.cfg).
3. **Install Mods tab** (a subheading): **Install view** (`[Mods] InstallView`; the Install toolbar's switch is the same value) and **Remember Install filters and sort** (`[Mods] RememberInstallFilters`, off by default).
4. **Sources and downloads** (a subheading): **Use Thunderstore**, **Show mods with NSFW content** (`[Mods]`), **Download limit** in MB (`[Install] MaxDownloadMB`, 8 to 1,024), and **Advanced sources** with **Expand** / **Collapse**. Expanded, it shows **Package index path** (`[Mods] IndexPath`) and **Keep the in-game frame canonical** (`[Workspace] CanonicalInGameFrame`). Neither the fold nor its two rows count toward the group's settings, so the group stays a subheading when the fold is open.

Every Preferences group has four settings or fewer, so Preferences draws no drawers.
5. **Join Game** (a subheading; BROWSERFILTER1, built and game-free tested, not live-verified): **Show only Progenitor servers** (`[ServerBrowser] ProgenitorOnly`, ui.cfg, personal, default off). It is the same switch as the **Show Only › Progenitor Servers** checkbox Progenitor adds to the game's Join Game browser ([server-list-description.md](server-list-description.md#progenitor-servers-in-join-game)).
6. **Local server** (a subheading): **Server folder** as a settings row (its name in the label column and the path field over the rest of the row, cell row `local-server-folder`, readback `localServerFolder {label, draft, validated}`), then **Apply** and **Clear** as standard key caps on the line under it (`local-server-folder-actions/apply`, `/clear`), its **Status** as a muted info row (`Validated: …` or `Refused: …`; readback `localServerStatus {label, text}`, cell row `local-server-status`; these rows are settings-grid rows whose label starts on the grid's name column, like every other row's name; each reads back `labelColumn {labelLeft, rowLeft, labelOffset, gridNameInset, layoutOffset}`, screen pixels for the drawn label and row, and the inset a settings row's name has), found folders, **Install Progenitor on this server** with its **Progenitor on this server** status row (`Server running — stop it…` while the server runs; the whole sentence is the hover description), Stop / Start server, **Admin list: Add me**.

Each value is the one entry its module reads, so every row applies live.

- **Edit other mods' settings is live.** A change applies at once: the catalog's foreign rows follow it, and an open mod page is drawn again. The Keybinds tab follows the catalog's key version.
- **Remember Install filters and sort.** Off (the default), every session starts from the defaults. On, the menu saves the Install tab's filters and sort (both views) in `[Mods] InstallFiltersRemembered` when you leave the Install tab, when the workspace closes and when Core unloads. The stored text is versioned (`v1;…`); a part that does not parse keeps its default. The search text is not part of it (SEARCHCLEAR1).
- The raw **Progenitor settings** pane is not reachable from here; `mods_ui select page=settings` still opens a raw pane for debugging.

### Progenitor's page

Mods → Required → Progenitor. The page draws Progenitor's page registration except the Server tab's groups (`ProgenitorPage.SectionsFor`).

1. **Your settings** (`This PC only`):
   - **Keys** (a drawer, with **Open in Keybinds**): every Core keybind. Each key row is at home here with the Keybinds tab as its mirror. (`[Keybinds] EnforceGameWins` is cfg-only since 2026-10-06.)
   - **Photo mode**: **Photo mode**, **Hide the HUD on entry**, **Hide nameplates with the HUD**, **Look speed** (`[PhotoMode]`, ui.cfg).
   - **Build menu**: **Build menu search**, **Clear the search on close**, **Breadcrumbs** (`[BuildSearch]`).
   - **Equipment**: **Dye all on hold** (`[DyeAll] Enabled`; [Dye all](modules.md#dye-all)). A hold is always 1 second and dyes only what shows: a transmog where one shows, never a hidden slot.
   - **Private games**: **Load Progenitor in the private games I host** (`[Hosted] Enabled`), and **Remove Progenitor from this PC** [**Uninstall**] in the danger tone, which gives way to **Removal requested** and **Cancel the removal** [**Keep Progenitor**] while a request is on disk (readback `progenitorUninstall {requested, requestedUtc, files, promptOpen, line}`).
   - **Get help**: **Collect logs for a help post** [**Collect logs**], **Last bundle**, **Open the logs folder** [**Open folder**] **Save bundles in** (`[Support] OutputFolder`, empty: the Desktop) and, for an admin of the joined Progenitor server, **Include this server's logs** (`[Support] IncludeServerLogs`, on) ([Get help](#get-help-collect-logs-support1)).
2. **On this server** (the scope heading, with `Applies to everyone on <server> · admins edit, others read only`): **Allow photo mode on this server** (`[PhotoMode] AllowOnThisServer`) , **Ally-safe hits** (`[Ai] AllySafeHits`) and **Remote chest preview** (`[ContainerPreview] RemotePreview`: Off, PvE servers only (the default) or On), all on the admin channel, so only an admin of the joined server sees and edits them; then, for everyone, `This server's setup is in the Server tab.` with **Server »** (`link-server/open`; readback `page-link {target: server-tab}`). Nothing else server-related is on the page.

The rows are placed by section and key (`SettingsPageBuilder.SettingRef`, Core only) and found when the page is drawn; a row whose module has not bound its entry yet is left out until it is. The page draws no row homed elsewhere.

### The Server tab

`ServerTabPane`, over the Server tab's groups of Progenitor's page registration (the same rows, the same server rules, the same channels as before). The title is the joined server's name as the game's section header with its red rule, then `How this server is set up · admins edit, others read only` (readback `server-tab-head {state, title, line, canEdit, groups, context}`).

Under that line (SERVERLINK1, requested 2026-10-07: "at the top, add a link to the progenitor MOD server side settings") a text link, **Progenitor's own server settings »**, drawn as a link (`CellSkin.Link`: link blue, underlined, no box, brighter on hover; the click target is the caption's own width). It opens the Mods tab on Progenitor's page scrolled to **On this server** (Photo mode, Ally-safe hits; the left list highlights it), through the same jump as the page's left list, so it is a place in the history and **Back** returns to the Server tab; that group ends with the page's own link back, `This server's setup is in the Server tab.` **Server »**. A player who sees none of those settings (they show only to an admin of the joined server) gets **Progenitor's page »** with the note `Progenitor's own server settings are for this server's admins.` and lands on the page's top. Bridge: `click_at {"row":"link-progenitor-server/open"}`; readback `page-link {target: progenitor-page, landing: on-this-server|top, label, note, skin: link, lastLanding}`. The link is underlined at all times (the kit's link look, as the join screens' web links), not only on hover. Source-checked and game-free tested (`ThreeHomesMenuTests`); not live-verified.

1. **When players join** (a drawer): **Server message** [**Show**] and **This server's mods** [**View**] for every player, then the server message's rows (Message, Preview, Countdown now, Next reset, Show the message, Show the mods page; RESETWIPE1 2026-10-06: Repeat, Repeat every, Countdown precision and After the reset left the menus, and Next reset is the read-only next wipe while the game schedules wipes, [join-flow.md](join-flow.md#server-message-settings-admins)).
2. **Mods players need** (a drawer since mod compatibility C3 made it five settings): **Players joining this server** [**Open**], **Version matching** (a radio list, the popup's mirror; read-only while the server's `[ClientPolicy] VersionMatching` forces it) and a read-only **Rules for one mod** line (`None`, or each mod and its rule; set in the popup: [client-mod-policy.md](client-mod-policy.md#version-matching-mod-compatibility-c3); source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified), **Mods break rules while playing**, **Seconds to fix a mod** (REQRULE5: Disconnect them's delay, `[ClientPolicy] DisconnectGraceSeconds`, 10 to 300, 60 by default; [client-mod-policy.md](client-mod-policy.md#disconnect-delay-reqrule5)) and **If mods can't be checked** (mirrors of the popup's choices: one stored value each; REQRULE7: If mods can't be checked is grayed with `Only applies with Only approved mods.` while the mod rule is Any mods, [client-mod-policy.md](client-mod-policy.md)).
3. **Server list** (a drawer): **Server name**, the description, the room left, the Progenitor signature, **Listed as** and the listing **Status**.
4. **Server rules · applied at the next restart** (a drawer; listed as **Server rules**): the **Pending changes** row, a status row (the Local server group's muted info-row look, never a setting's field: PENDING1, user review 2026-10-06): `None. Changes here apply when the server restarts.` in the muted tone, or `2 changes apply at the next restart: Clan size 4 → 3, Game mode PvP → PvE` in gold (at most three named and about 90 characters, then `and N more`), with every change listed in its hover description; a pending password reads `Password changed`, never a value. The same wording read-only for a player who is not an admin. Rows with a pending change keep their **Restart** chip. Readback: the row's `style status`, `tone muted|gold`, and `pendingRestart {count, items [{key, loaded, next}], line, attention}` (a password item has no values). Source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified; then a preset warning when one is missing, the rules, and the read-only lines (Listed as Merciless, Territory, Days running, Standard settings, Next scheduled wipe). The rules, writes, confirmations and sources are in [settings-organization.md](settings-organization.md#vanilla-server-settings-server-rules).
5. **Players without Progenitor**: **Message without Progenitor**, then the **Players without Progenitor** radio list (KEEPOUT1, 2026-10-06; `[Networking] RequireProgenitor`, admin-only, lockable; **Let them play** (default: they play unless a server mod needs Progenitor on clients or the client mod rules need a check) or **Keep them out** (they get the message and are disconnected after Seconds before disconnect, whatever the server's mods need; a 0 there counts as 10 s so the message still reaches them); it applies from the next join), and **Seconds before disconnect** (`[Networking] HandshakeAnswerTimeoutSeconds`, 0 to 300; it applies under Keep them out, when a server mod needs Progenitor on clients, or when the client mod rules need a check). The message (NOPROGMSG1, user review 2026-10-06; source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified) is a text area with **Apply** under it. Empty means the default: the field shows `NoAnswerNoticeText.DefaultTemplate` as a muted hint (never as a value) and **Length** reads `Using the default message.`; with the admins' own text **Length** reads `212 / 300` (the warning tone over 300) and **Default message** [**Use the default**] clears it through the normal write and audit. Under the field, always: **Placeholders** `{mods} — the mods they're missing · {seconds} — seconds until they're disconnected` (plain text: the kit's text area cannot insert at the cursor) and **Players see** `…`, the chat line built by `NoAnswerNoticeText.Build` with `{mods}` as Progenitor and `{seconds}` as 20 from the field's text as typed (each end of an edit) or the stored value, so the printable-ASCII and `< >` → `( )` rules show. A player who is not an admin reads the effective message (the default when empty). The hover description lists the placeholders and the limits. Readback: the field's `placeholder`, and `noProgenitorMessage {usingDefault, chars, max, over, preview}` on the **Players see** row; status rows read `style status` and `tone`.
6. **Operator** (a drawer): **Remote mod installs**, **Admins who may install mods**, **First notice after (seconds)**, **Repeat notice every (seconds)**, **Creation time limit (seconds)**. Every admin of the server may change all of these (decided 2026-10-06; per-role permissions are planned, not built).

- **Buttons.** Every button on the tab is the game's steel menu button: **Show**, **View**, **Open**, **Preview**, **Change**, the **Apply** under a text area and the inline **Apply** beside a one-line field (the server rules' fields, Server name, the Operator fields). INLINEAPPLY1 ([ui.md](ui.md#borrowed-game-art-across-core-swaps-buttonart1-source-checked-and-game-free-tested-not-live-verified)); readback `inlineButtons [{caption, art: game-button}]` on each row. Source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified.
- **Dropdowns.** Every row drawn as a dropdown (Game mode, Difficulty, Castle heart damage, Wipe on, Mods break rules while playing, If mods can't be checked, Preferences › Install view, and a mod's own enum settings) opens its option list on click and writes once on a pick; Escape, a click outside or the current value closes it with no write ([mod-settings.md](mod-settings.md)). Readback: the row's `dropdown {open, options [{value, label, description, current}], highlight, hovered}`. A segmented choice (Repeat None / Days / Weeks) keeps its own segmented look. DROPDOWN1; source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified.
- **Hovered options** (OPTIONHOVER1, requested 2026-10-06). While the pointer is over an option of an open dropdown list (Game mode, Difficulty, Castle heart damage, Wipe on) or a radio line (Show the message, Show the mods page, Mods break rules while playing, If mods can't be checked), the description column shows the setting's name, the option and what it does, then the setting's timing note (`Applies at the next restart.`); leaving the options or closing the list brings the setting's own description back. The open list covers the rows under it completely (an opaque back, its own sorting order re-applied on open). Bridge: `mods_ui hover {label, option}` / `hover {label, leave: true}`, and `widget_read`'s top-level `details` ([option descriptions](mod-settings.md#option-descriptions)). Source-checked and game-free tested (`OptionHoverDetailsTests`), not live-verified.
- **A player who is not an admin** sees the same header and groups. Server-wide ConfigSync rows and the server rules read `Admin only · <value>` with no field or **Apply**; the password reads only `Set` or `Not set`. Rows on the admin channel are never sent to a non-admin, so a group made only of them (Mods players need, Operator) shows `Admin only` · `Only an admin of this server sees these settings.` instead.
- **Not connected, a server without Progenitor, or waiting (SERVEREMPTY1; source-checked, built (Debug and Release) and game-free tested, not live-verified).** The title **Server**, then one explanation: not connected, `You're not on a server. When you join one that runs Progenitor, its setup shows here: admins edit it, everyone else can read it.`; on a server without Progenitor, `This server doesn't run Progenitor, so its setup can't be shown or changed here. On a server that runs it, admins edit it and everyone else can read it.`; while the handshake answers, `Waiting for the server to answer… Its setup shows here once it does.` Under it, one muted line per group: its name in gold and what it holds (When players join · message, resets, join screens; Mods players need · version matching, required and approved mods, mid-session loads; Server list · name, description, listing; Server rules · mode, clan size, password; Players without Progenitor · message, seconds before disconnect; Operator · remote installs, who may install). The left list shows the six groups dimmed (the muted tone, a plain row: no hover, no highlight, no jump; `mods_ui section` refuses them).
- **Last server** (not connected only). `Last server: <name> · <address>` with **Join** (the settings rows' key-cap button) for the last server this client joined, from `BepInEx/config/progenitor-last-server.json` ([join-flow.md](join-flow.md#the-last-server-serverempty1)). Hidden when there is no record or while this client hosts a private game; **Join** is grayed while a join is in progress (Join now's reconnect, or a client world on its way in) and checked again when pressed. **Join** connects as Join now does (the **Joining <server>** page, then `LaunchGame` with the recorded address, port and transport) and closes the Mods window. Join always connects without a password first (JOINPW1); only when the server refuses with `IncorrectPassword` does it open the game's own password prompt for that address (`GameHelper.LaunchPasswordProtectedGame`), where the player, or a mod such as RetainedPassword, enters it. A cancelled prompt just ends. `needsPassword` in the record and the readback is a hint updated from each outcome, never the decision; Progenitor never stores the password ([join-flow.md](join-flow.md#the-last-server-serverempty1)).
- **Readback and bridge.** The explanation line reads back `server-tab-empty {state, line}` and `serverEmpty {state, lastServer {name, address} | null, joinShown, joinEnabled, groups}`; each group line `serverEmptyGroup {group, slug, holds}` (row `server-empty/<slug>`); the Last server row `serverLast {name, address, needsPassword, joinShown, joinEnabled, state: ready|busy, lastPress}`; a dimmed left-list row `sectionNav {…, dimmed: true}`. Press Join with `widget_click label=server-last/join`. The tab is drawn again when the joined server's answer changes what it shows (a join, a leave, admin or not, the lock list) or, not connected, when the last server or its Join state changed at that redraw.
- **Admin-channel rows.** Switches and choices send each change; a number or the list has a field with **Apply** on its row. The help line reads `Waiting for the server…`, then `Saved by the server.`, the server's note or its refusal. Before the server's state arrives the value reads `…`. Confirmations: **Turn off remote installs?** and **Remove yourself from the list?**; **No** sends nothing.
- **Locked in the server's config file.** Any server setting the joined server's `[ServerSettings] LockedInGame` names (a setting id, a group word, or `*`) is drawn read-only wherever it is drawn (this tab or Progenitor's page), for admins too, with `Locked in the server's config file (ServerSettings LockedInGame: <entry>).` and a **Locked** marker. The group words are unchanged by the move: `gameplay` names the two On this server toggles, and **If mods can't be checked** stays in both `mods-players-need` and `no-progenitor`. The server refuses a locked edit anyway, with an audit line.
- **Readback.** Admin-channel rows are `page-setting` rows with `kind: admin-setting`, `key`, `control`, `value`, `display`, `known`, `locked`, `lockedBy`, `requestPending`, `status`, `store: join-admin` and `settingId` / `home` / `alsoAt`.
- The Install tab's **Server settings »** link (Simple view, an admin acting on the joined server) opens this tab.

### Drawers and the left list

- **Drawers.** On Preferences, the Server tab and Progenitor's page, a group of **more than four** settings is a drawer (since the 2026-10-06 trim, none on Preferences). Its header, from the left: the fold marker (the game's dropdown arrow in the subheading's gold at the title's size, pointing down when open and right when closed; the kit's `▼` / `»` glyphs, which the game font has, when the arrow was not borrowed), the gold small-caps title, and the muted count as `(12)` (the settings a player can see in it). The whole header lights with the game's list-row hover tint and takes the click, as does the marker; it opens or closes the drawer in place; the rows are hidden or shown, nothing is rebuilt. Groups of four or fewer are plain subheadings in the same title style, set in by the marker's width so the titles line up. Links, dividers, the Advanced fold and its two rows, and the admin-only note are not counted. Mod authors' pages draw no drawers.
- **Remembered.** The closed drawers are stored in `[Ui] ClosedDrawers` of `progenitor.ui.cfg` as `<tab>/<group slug>` keys separated by commas (`server/operator,progenitor/keys`). Empty (the default) means every drawer is open. It is read on every use, so it survives a Core reload and a restart. A search on a mod page looks inside closed drawers.
- **The left list.** Preferences and the Server tab list their groups on the left; Progenitor's groups are listed under its entry in the Mods list while its page is open (a jump there opens the Required group). A click scrolls the content so the group's heading is at the top, opens the group if it is a closed drawer, and highlights the item. The highlight then follows the scroll: the group whose heading is at, or last passed, the top of the view. A group near the end of a short page keeps the highlight its click gave it until you scroll.
- **Back and forward.** Each left-list jump is a place: `preferences@<group>`, `server@<group>`, `progenitor-page@<group>` (`nav_state` `current.place`). **Navigate back** and **forward** return to the group and open its drawer again. The Server tab itself is the place `server`.
- **A stable header.** A value change rewrites a drawer's count in place; the header's cells are re-placed at the width the last layout pass placed the row at (`MenuPanelLayout.InPlaceCellRowWidth`), never the full content width (live 2026-10-06 that cut the marker and the start of the title off: `ION (5)`). Every layout pass places the row again at its own width.
- **Readback and bridge.** A drawer header reads back `page-section {title, group, drawer: true, open, count, key, chevron {cell, state: open|closed, points: down|right, side: left, tone: gold, shown}, titleShown, heading {chevronShown, titleShown, chevronLeft, titleLeft, titleWidth, titleNeeds}}`; `chevron.shown` and `titleShown` are measured from the drawn rects (the marker inside the row; the title inside the row and at least as wide as its full text in small caps); a subheading `drawer: false` with its `count`. A left-list row reads back `sectionNav {tab, group, slug, highlighted, jumped}`. `nav_state` carries `sectionNav {tab, groups, highlighted, jumped, pinned, drawers[], lastJump {tab, group, found, openedDrawer, measured, landed, …}, storedClosedDrawers}`. Click a left-list item with `widget_click label=nav/<group>` (caption or slug) or `mods_ui section {group, tab?}`; toggle a drawer with `widget_click label=drawer/<group>` (or its marker, `drawer-<slug>/chevron`) or `mods_ui drawer {group, open?}` (see [bridge-ops-ui.md](bridge-ops-ui.md#recipe-three-homes)).

## Inventory

`ModMenuInventory` is read-only current evidence. It does not scan disk, transfer files or change plugin lifecycle. Catalog claims are never treated as handshake requirements.

| Evidence | Status shown |
|---|---|
| Core (`LocalRank == 3`) | `Running` |
| Chainloader instance present | `Loaded on this client` (activity not checked) |
| Reload-owned assembly, no instance | `Loaded on this client` (activation unavailable) |
| Handshake `CopyProvided` only | `Reported on this client` (installation and loading unconfirmed) |
| Catalog or server-asked only | `Listed by server` (no loaded client copy observed) |
| Paused by [per-server mod pause](#per-server-mod-pause) (Bootstrap's paused set) | `Paused for this server` (the sidebar subtitle says so too) |

**Install row states around a hot-loaded plugin (HOT3, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified).** A ledgered `progenitor-reload/` copy on a root this process runs is `installed` as soon as the reloader reports its plugin loaded (`PluginReloader.LoadedPluginNames`, matched on letters and digits against the listing's plugin id, display name, package name or cached plugin GUID) rather than only when the Mods-tab inventory snapshot the pane was built with happens to name it, and its row owes no restart; a removal the reloader answered with an unload (`hotUnloaded`) puts the row straight back to `none` / **Install** instead of the `removed` state, which is kept for the restart route.

**Install tab row states and confirmations (INSTALL2, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified).** The Install tab follows the approved mock. The sentences marked **POLISH1** below fix four defects observed live on the client at deploy #70 (2026-09-13, readback via `mods_ui widget_read`) and the sentences marked **POLISH2** the polish items of the check after it; the fixes themselves are source-checked and game-free tested, not yet live-verified.

- **Hidden listings.** Any loader pack (`LoaderPackages.KnownLoaderPackages`, plus owner `BepInEx` with a `BepInExPack` name prefix) and Progenitor's own packages (`Progenitor-Progenitor`, `Progenitor-Progenitor_VRising`) have no row at all: they are dropped in `InstallModsPane.AllEntries`, so no filter, search, count, sort or pending total can reach them. A mod that merely has "Progenitor" in its name is somebody else's mod and stays listed.
- **One caption and tone per state.** `Install` / `Install · restart` in gold; **Installed** in green for a loaded or promoted install, with ` · new` appended for a mod this session hot loaded (the cell kit has no tag element — the readback carries it as `listing.newTag`); **Installed · restart to load** in yellow for a pending install; an uninstalled-but-pending row is orange, its one-liner reads `Uninstalled · removed at the next restart` and its button is **Reactivate**; `Cannot install` is unchanged. The old **Remove** / **Remove · restart to unload** captions are gone: the row states the fact and the confirmation states the consequence. POLISH1 (live defect C, deploy #70): a mod installed to the local server reads green **Installed** with an `Uninstall …?` prompt, and drops out of the pending bar, once that server has restarted and promoted it — the evidence is a `progenitor-reload/` copy on the server root named by a reconciled `mod` ledger row (`InstalledState.ServerCopyPromoted`, `halves.server: loaded`), and the Install tab reconciles both ledgers at the top of every build so the promote is seen in the session that follows it (`InstallCatalogService.ReconcileChangedLedgers`, one `progenitor-install.json` stamp per root, re-scanning the pane's snapshot only when a ledger moved). POLISH1 (live defect D): after a restart a mod that was hot loaded earlier is hot-unloadable again — and so owes no restart — when the reloader reports its ledgered `mod` copy loaded *and* the local reviewed policy covers those exact bytes for same-session activation on this side (`HotUnloadQualification.Covers`, the same record `AdmissionEvaluator` requires); the ` · new` tag does not come back with it, because the tag means "arrived while you were playing".
- **Confirmations.** Every action opens a y/n box in the drawer under its row: `Install <name> <version>?` / `Uninstall <name> <version>?` / `Keep <name> installed?` (Reactivate) / `Cancel installing <name>?`. The body is `InstallSummary.Describe` — side, owner, version, when it loads, and one sentence per dependency ("Needs ExampleCommandFramework 0.11.0, which will be installed too" / "which is installed") — and the box carries **no target chooser** (INSTALLUX1 item 2: the target comes from a Configuration setting). Buttons are `No` and `Yes, install` / `Yes, uninstall` / `Yes, keep it` / `Yes, cancel`. The Uninstall and Cancel install boxes are destructive (DANGERENTER1): Enter does not answer them and their Yes is drawn in the danger tone. An install's **Yes** writes the whole planned set, dependencies first (`InstallCatalogService.ExecuteWithDependencies`); **No** closes the box and drops the download's approval. When other ledgered installs need the package, the uninstall body adds `Other installed mods need it: <list>.` and its Yes reads **Yes, uninstall anyway** and passes `force`. POLISH2 (live 2026-09-13): the uninstall body names the restart it actually owes, by the halves the file is on — `Removed at the next restart of V Rising` for the client, `… of the local server` for a server-only install (AutoLatchGates read the client sentence live, the one restart that would not have removed it) and `… of V Rising and the local server` for both (`InstallModsCopy.UninstallRestartBodyFor`, sides from `InstallModsPane.InstalledSidesOf` — the same side rule as the pending bar's); the row's own `restart` is unchanged, because a restart is still owed.
- **One-click install (INSTALLUX1, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified).** Decided 2026-09-13: "when they click install a popup opens and confirms, install MOD (mod name) y/n. then that is it, it installs to the location based on their settings." So the Install click opens the confirmation **at once** — before the download, before the inspection — with the heading it will keep (`Install <name> <version>?`), the `Yes, install` caption it will keep, and a body that says what the listing alone supports (`<Side> mod by <owner>, version <v>.`) followed by `Checking the package…` (`InstallSummary.DescribeChecking`). The download and the dependency-closure plan run behind the open box; when they land the body becomes the full `InstallSummary.Describe` and the box is unchanged in shape. A **Yes** pressed while it is still checking is remembered and honored once the moment the plan lands (`InstallModsPane.PromptYes` → `_promptYesPending` → `OpenInstallPrompt`), so the player never clicks twice. A refusal — a failed download, a blocked plan, a refused closure member — replaces the body with `Cannot install — <reason>` and leaves the single button **Close**; the box does not vanish under the player. The `<Side>` word is the **listing's** side (`PackageSideDerivation`, or the inspected `progenitor.json` / `[BepInProcess]` once the zip has been read), never the target's, because the target is now a setting and calling a server mod a "Client mod" would be false.
- **Confirmations name the server, as facts (CONFIRMTARGET1 and 2, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified).** Requested 2026-10-01: "Since this is a server install it should say what server it is installing it to." On the CONFIRMTARGET1 build (live on dda963fb) the box was a centered six-line paragraph; the next request was: "Make it more readable." The install, update and uninstall boxes now have three parts.
  - **Title.** One short line: `Install RustyRepairs 1.2.2?`, `Update RustyRepairs to 1.2.3?` (an update row: the list's `Update` state, a side decision that updates a half, or a remote update), `Uninstall RustyRepairs?`. REQRULE6 (user report 2026-10-08) adds where it goes: `Install HotDemo 0.1.0 on Progenitor Forms Test?`, `… on this game?`, `… on this game and Progenitor Forms Test?`, `Uninstall HotDemo from …?`, `Cancel installing HotDemo on …?`, `Keep HotDemo installed on …?` (`InstallModsPane.PromptTouches`: the side buttons, the Server cell, the remote-only box, a whole-row uninstall or cancel by the halves the ledgers name, a whole-row Cancel install of a copy pending on the connected server (review M2), and a whole-row Keep installed by the halves that are marked (review M1)). A box offering two answers (`Yes, install on both`) keeps the plain title; its first fact names both answers. The Mods page's uninstall title follows its plan's removers the same way (`ModUninstallPlan.TargetWords`).
  - **Facts.** One left-aligned label/value row per fact, with labels muted and values bright (the [DIALOGS2 facts block](ui.md#facts)). Rows that do not apply are left out:
    - `Mod`: `RustyRepairs 1.2.2 by iTestTeam`.
    - `Installs on` / `Removes from` (REQRULE6, first after `Mod`): `The server only — this game already has it`, `The server only — not this game`, `The server only — this game keeps its copy` (uninstall), `The server only` (a server-only mod), `This game only`, `This game and Progenitor Forms Test`, or for a box with two answers `This game only, or this game and Progenitor Forms Test with Yes, install on both` (`Progenitor Forms Test only, or …` from the Server cell). Live 2026-10-08: a Simple view Install of a mod `Missing on server` showed `Not changed: This client: its copy stays as it is` and it was not clear that it installs only the server's copy; a server-only box no longer has a `Not changed` line (this row says it), and a this-game-only box keeps its `Not changed` line about the server.
    - `Server`: the server's name, else `Your local server` or `The connected server`, then its kind: `(on this PC)` for the configured local folder (the box shows `Folder`) or `(remote)` (the box shows `Address`, never a folder); `Progenitor Forms Test (on this PC; only with Yes, install on both)` in the two-answer box. This row appears only when the step touches a server (`InstallModsPane.PromptServerFor`).
    - `Folder`: the local server's folder, cut in the middle past 60 characters. Local route only.
    - `Address`: the connected server's `address:port` from Bootstrap's join record. Remote route only.
    - (`Also on: This game` is gone: F39 item 3, it repeated the `Installs on` line.)
    - `Takes effect`: `Now (hot-loaded)`, `At the next game restart`, `At the next server restart`, `At the next game and server restart`, `Now on this game; at the next server restart on the server` (a mixed plan), `Checking the package…` before the plan lands, or `Now if it hot-loads, else at the next server restart` for a remote install. An uninstall reads `Now (unloads)` (with the server half's pending or loaded variant), or the restart its sides wait on. F33 (live 2026-10-08; built and game-free tested in `InstallListViewTests`, not live-verified): on the remote route the effect comes from the server's own status row: a hot copy reads `Now (unloads)` (uninstall) or `Now (hot-loaded)` (install or update), a copy loaded at the server's start `At the next server restart` (uninstall), a pending copy `Now (cancels the pending server install)` (uninstall) or `At the next server restart`; only a row the status does not settle keeps `Now if it hot-loads, else at the next server restart`. A remote install the server takes from its own local index adds `Source` `<server>'s local test source (<version>)`.
    - `Server copy`: the remote half of a client install whose server half goes to the connected server.
    - `Requires`: one row per dependency (the later rows have an empty label). `ExampleCommandFramework 0.10.4 — installed on that server` (green), `— installed on this game or that server` for Client and server, `— installed` for a client install, `— will be installed alongside`, or `— missing (blocks the install)` (red) when the plan names a refusal for it. A missing dependency the install cannot write still refuses the plan, and the box then shows the refusal.
    - `Your settings`: `Kept`, on an uninstall.
  - **Note.** At most one short sentence, and only when something needs saying:
    - an install: the refusal (`Cannot install — <reason>`), the fallback to this game when no server route exists, or the restart the change waits on (`The server must be restarted for this to load.`, `The game must be restarted …`, `The game and the server must be restarted …`);
    - an uninstall: the dependents warning (`Other installed mods need it: …`), a half left in place (`Left in place: the server (no route to it from here).`), or `It stays loaded until the server restarts.` / `… the game restarts.` / `… the game and the server restart.`
  - The Yes caption mirrors the action: `Yes, install`, `Yes, update`, `Yes, uninstall` (`Yes, uninstall anyway` with dependents). No and the button keys are unchanged. Reactivate and cancel-install keep their one-line body.
  - **The server's name** follows the server's own precedence: the `-serverName` argument on the running process's command line, then the same argument in the launch record, then the `"Name"` in the folder's `ServerHostSettings.json` (found like the port: the running process's or the launch record's `-persistentDataPath` first, then the install's own settings folders), then nothing. Live on dda963fb, `-serverName "Progenitor Forms Test"` overrode the file's `Progenitor Test`, and the server advertised the command-line name. The remote-install channel and the handshake carry no server name, so a connected server is named by its `address:port` (no protocol change).
  - **Readback.** `action.prompt` gains `facts` (`[{label, value, tone}]`), `note` and `update`; `mods_ui status` `dialog.facts` / `dialog.note` read the open dialog. `action.prompt.body` keeps the sentence summary the bridge has always returned. That summary's first line names the server (`Server: <name> — local folder <folder>`, `Server: connected at <address:port>`, or `On this game and on your local server <name> (<folder>).`), its restart sentence names it (`Loads at the next restart of Progenitor Forms Test.`), and its dependency sentences say where they were found (`…, which is installed on that server.` / `…, which is not on that server yet, so it will be installed alongside.`).
  - **Other places that name the server.** A remote step's progress line (`Downloading on 203.0.113.5:9876…`, `Pending restart of …`, `Removed from … at its next restart.`) and a row under the pending bar whose change waits only on a server (`Pending restart of <name> · to load` / `· to uninstall`). The bridge's `words` field is unchanged.
- **Confirmations are a centered dialog, and the row carries a status word plus one button (INSTALLUX2, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified).** Feedback 2026-09-13 on the INSTALLUX1 build with HotDemo installed to Both: "I click install on and it expands like the old behavior asking me if I want to stall like this instead of popping up a dialogue. It does not show up in the MODs menu as installed. I then have to click install / new to start the uninstall. That is bad UX. Let's get rid of the install new features. Just change it to an uninstall button. and make sure it is listed in the MODs section." Three changes follow.
  - **The box is a modal dialog**, not an in-row expansion: `Ui.Dialog` (see [ui.md](ui.md)) - the dialog kit's chrome, in the middle of the screen over a raycast-blocking backdrop, content-sized, revealed only after the runtime's layout pass. Install, uninstall, reactivate, cancel-install and a refusal all use it; the row keeps its one-line hint and its drawer and draws none of the confirmation. Escape is **No**. The published handles are unchanged - the buttons are still `prompt:<key>/promptYes` and `/promptNo`, and a refusal's single button is `prompt:<key>/promptClose` - so a bridge client clicks the same things; `action.prompt` keeps all its keys and gains `presentation: "dialog"`, and `mods_ui status` gains `dialog {open, key, kind}`.
  - **The Status column is a state text and a button.** The `new` tag is gone entirely (no caption ever reads `Installed - new`; the `listing.newTag` readback key stays and is always `false`). The texts and buttons per state are in the Status table of [Install list columns](#install-list-columns); a row with a state but no action this pane can take draws the text alone and no button. The column is a fixed 276 px, headed `Status`, and its right edge is still one scrollbar band inside the row edge. `listing.state` is unchanged; `listing.actions[]` names the buttons actually drawn (`["install"]`, `["uninstall"]`, `["cancel"]`, `["reactivate"]`, or `[]`).
  - **Only mods this manager installed offer Uninstall (MANAGED1, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified).** An installed row is labeled managed elsewhere when either of these is true: the Mods inventory has the plugin loaded and no Progenitor file of it is on disk (`InstalledKind.ManagedElsewhere`), or the install ledger is readable and does not name a copy found in `progenitor-pending/` or `progenitor-reload/` that this session did not write. Such rows were copied by hand, deployed by a script such as `tools/deploy.ps1`, or installed by another mod manager. The Status column's text reads, in the muted tone and with no button, `Managed by <tool>` when the inventory row names the other manager and the caption is no wider than `Managed elsewhere`, otherwise `Managed elsewhere`, with the preloader's short reason on a second line when it gave one. No production inventory names a manager yet, so live rows read `Managed elsewhere`. A press opens the drawer, whose note is `InstallModsCopy.ManagedElsewhereNote` (delete the files from `BepInEx/plugins` or use the tool that installed it), followed by `Files: ...` when the scan found them. A plugin loaded from `BepInEx/plugins` has no file paths in the scan, so its note has no `Files:` line. The readback adds `listing.managed`, which holds the label or `""`, and `listing.actions` stays `[]`. With no ledger view (`LedgerProbe` null), or for a copy this session just wrote, the row keeps the plain word and no label. Ledgered rows and the loader row are unchanged.
  - **Uninstall for mods another tool installed (SETTINGS1 stage 3, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified).** Where the `Progenitor.Preloader` patcher can act with high confidence (the mod has a folder of its own under `BepInEx/plugins`, or it is a qualifying loose single DLL; see [mod-settings.md](mod-settings.md#ordinary-bepinex-plugins)), a MANAGED1 row offers **Uninstall** beside its green `Installed` word instead of the label. The press opens the framework confirmation `Uninstall <name>?` in the danger tone ("Progenitor moves it to BepInEx/progenitor-removed the next time the game starts. You can restore it from there by moving it back into BepInEx/plugins. It stays loaded until then."); **Yes, uninstall** writes the next-start request, and the row reads `Removed at next start` with **Cancel uninstall**. After the restart the row is reconciled: the mod is gone (the listing reads not installed), or the drawer says plainly why it could not be removed (`Could not be removed at the last start: …`) and Uninstall is offered again. Where the preloader cannot act the row keeps `Managed elsewhere`, and the drawer note starts with why in a few words (`Shares its folder with another mod`, `Another mod depends on it`, `Its folder contains a link`, …). The mod's own page offers the same **Uninstall** / **Cancel uninstall** in its status bar's action slot. Readback: `listing.plainUninstall` (true on such a row) and `listing.actions` `["uninstall"]` / `["cancel-removal"]`. **Restore** is by hand: move the mod's folder (or DLL) from `BepInEx/progenitor-removed/<yyyyMMdd-HHmmss>/` back into `BepInEx/plugins` while the game is closed; there is no in-game restore list yet.
  - **The body says `Client and server mod by ...`** for a both-sided listing (`Server mod` / `Client mod` unchanged). POLISH3 item D folded that wording into `InstallSummary.SideWord(PackageSide.Both)` itself, so both `SideWord` overloads and `InstallModsPane.PromptSideWord` (now a plain forward) answer the one word and no caller can still read `Both`.
- **Uninstall acts on every half the file is on (POLISH3 item A, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified).** Live at deploy #74 HotDemo was installed to Both — the client half hot loaded, the local server's written to `progenitor-pending/`. **Uninstall → Yes** unloaded the client copy and left the server's file, its ledger row and the Mods tab's `pendload` entry behind: the confirmation had one client-shaped route and no per-half accounting, so a half it failed to take looked exactly like a half that was not there. Now one **Yes** decides each half from `InstallModsPane.HalvesOf`: the client's half keeps its own route (a hot copy unloads now, an ordinary loaded one takes the `.uninstall` marker), and the local server's is its own action — a downloaded `progenitor-pending/` copy is **canceled** (deleted) and a promoted copy the server has loaded is **marked** for its next start, never deleted under a running server (`InstallModsPane.ConfirmUninstall` → `ActOnServerHalf`, scoped through `InstallCatalogService.RemovePackage(key, force, scope)` / `MarkUninstall(key, force, scope)`). The body names both halves before the press — `Unloads now on the client and cancels the pending install on the local server.` / `Unloads now on the client; removed from the local server at its next restart.` (`InstallModsCopy.UninstallHotBodyFor`; the restart variants of POLISH2's `UninstallRestartBodyFor` are unchanged) — and the readback gains `removed.halves {client, server}` with one of `none` / `unloaded` / `removed` / `cancelled` / `marked` / `failed` per half. A server half that was on disk and could not be taken is a **failure**, not silence: the Yes returns false and the drawer says `The local server's copy could not be removed`, which is the report the live defect never made. The removal and the mark also ask for every source spelling the ledgers on those roots actually carry for the listing (`LedgerSpellingsOf`, kept to the rows `InstalledState.PackageMatches` accepts), because the first-class restart route ledgered the server half under the *plugin id* while the hot and ordinary routes used the package key — POLISH4 below stopped it writing that spelling and taught every reader both. A taken server half also raises the inventory hook (`InventoryReasonUninstalled`), so the Mods tab stops listing the mod without reopening the workspace.
- **A server copy its reloader drops now says Now (REQRULE3, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified).** Live 2026-10-08: HotDemo installed on both sides from the client (the local route), the server's reloader hot-loaded its copy (`Reload: loaded Progenitor Hot Demo 0.1.0`), and the uninstall box said `Takes effect: Now on this game; at the next server restart on the server`, although the removal already deletes such a copy now (HOTDEMO1: the local server is running with its reload folder watched from here and the copy is a trusted hot-loadable image, `InstallCatalogService.ServerUnloadsNow`). The cross-process ledger row carries no `hot` flag by design (the flag means this process loaded it), so the box now asks the removal's own question (`InstallModsPane.LocalServerHalfUnloadsNow`): `Takes effect` reads `Now (unloads)` (or `Now on the server; at the next game restart on this game` when this game's copy is not hot), the body `Unloads now on the client and on the local server; nothing to restart.` / `Removed from the local server now; its reloader unloads it, nothing to restart.` / `Unloads now on the local server; removed from V Rising at its next restart, and it stays loaded on this game until then.`, and the restart note is left out. A copy the server can't drop now (not running, not watched, not a trusted hot-loadable image, including a start-promoted copy that fails those checks) keeps the marker and its next-restart wording. The Mods page's uninstall box already used the same answer (`ModUninstallPlan.ServerUnloadsNow`). Review L1: the box and the removal ask one function (`LocalServerHalfUnloadsNow`) on any route.
- **Yes, install on both acts at once (F39, game-free tested, not live-verified).** Live 2026-10-08: the client cell's box offering both opened a second box (`Install HotDemo 0.1.0 on this game and …?`) and only its Yes wrote both halves; in game a server notice covered that second box, the client half was never written and the player was disconnected. The first box already names both sides and the timing, so its `Yes, install on both` is the Yes: the both-sides flow starts already answered (`PromptYes`, honored when its plan is ready). The box stays only when that plan is refused, and then says why.
- **The ledger row names the listing, and the plugin id is its own field (POLISH4, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified).** Live at deploy #76 HotDemo was installed to Both again and the local server's half was *invisible*: its row read `{"path": "BepInEx/progenitor-pending/index/progenitor.hotdemo.dll", "source": "package:progenitor.hotdemo@0.1.0", "category": "pending"}` and the file was there, yet the Install row said `halves {client: loaded, server: none}`, POLISH3's per-half Uninstall reported `removed.halves {client: unloaded, server: none}` and did nothing, and the Mods tab went on listing `progenitor.hotdemo` as `pendload`. The cause was the spelling: the first-class `progenitor.json` restart route wrote the *plugin id* into `source`, and a later session's catalog entry — adapted from the index, with nothing inspected — carries an empty `Manifest.Id`, so none of the name spellings matched. Two changes. **Writing:** every ledger row Progenitor writes for a catalog install now records `source: package:<listing key>@<version>` (`package:Progenitor-HotDemo@0.1.0`) on both roots and for `pending`, `mod` and hot rows alike, and the plugin id, when the install knew it, goes in the row's new optional `pluginId` field (`LedgerSpelling.SourceFor`; the writers are `InstallStaging.AppendServerLedger` → `ServerInstall.TryAppendPending` / `TryAppendInstalledFile` and `HotLoadExecutor.Ledger`, and `ServerInstall.ReconcilePromoted` and `UninstallMarkers` carry the field through a promote and a mark). `framework` rows keep their `client-image:<sha>` source and are untouched. **Reading:** one comparison, `LedgerSpelling.Matches(entry, row, identityProbe)`, and no caller keeps its own — it accepts the listing key, the inspected manifest's id, the manifest package, `<owner>-<name>`, the row's own `pluginId`, and the plugin GUID the identity cache (`progenitor-cache/identity`, via `InstallCatalogService.IdentityProbe`) recorded for that listing, so a row an older build wrote under the plugin id resolves with no re-inspection and no download. `InstalledState.PackageMatches`, `LedgerRowsFor`, `LedgerSpellingsOf`, `ModsMenu.IsLedgeredOnAnyRoot`, `ModMenuInventory.LedgerRowsForPlugin` and the INSTORD1 dependency check (`OrdinaryDependencies.LedgerHasByIdentity`) all go through it. A cache miss is "not known", never a package name read as a GUID. **Caption:** a Progenitor-ledgered mod nothing has loaded registered no name, so the sidebar drew its GUID (live: `progenitor.hotdemo`); it now shows the listing's name from the ledger row's package key, else the key the identity cache resolves (`ModsMenu.SidebarNameOf`), and the GUID stays the row's identity in `entries[].id`.
- **`Install · restart` never appears on a row that has a pending half (POLISH3 item E — checked, already true).** A row whose only half is the local server's pending copy is `pending`, so `RowStatusText` gives it the status word `Installed · restart to load` and `RowButtonText` gives it **Cancel**; the `Install · restart` caption belongs to the state-`None` branch of `ActionFor`, which a row with a pending half never reaches, and POLISH1's `pending.sides` reads `server` for it so the bar says `restart the local server to apply`. The live `Install · restart` on HotDemo's row was a consequence of item A — the client half had been unloaded while the server's stayed on disk unreported — not a caption rule. Pinned by `InstallHotLoadTests.A_row_whose_only_pending_half_is_the_server_says_installed_restart_to_load`.
- **The Mods list shows a just-installed mod at once (INSTALLUX2 item 4, source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified).** Live symptom: HotDemo hot-installed to Both did not appear under **Mods** until the workspace was closed and reopened. Two triggers now, both without closing the workspace: the inventory-changed hook the Install tab already raises on a hot load or unload records that a Mods rebuild is owed while another tab is selected (it used to return early and forget), and selecting the Mods tab compares the install ledger's stamp (`progenitor-install.json` write time and length on the client and local-server roots - read, never consumed, because `InstallCatalogService.ReconcileChangedLedgers` clears its own stamps and the Install tab calls it on every build) with the one taken when the workspace opened. Either owes `ModMenuInventory.Invalidate` plus `RebuildSidebar(ReasonInventoryRefresh)` at the end of the tab select, so the next Mods draw lists the mod with `provenance progenitor` and its installed state.

- **Default install target** (superseded by INSTALLSIDES1: the row is no longer on Configuration and the key no longer routes Install rows; the toolbar [server picker](#install-rows-per-side-installsides1) does). Configuration → **Local dedicated server** → `Default install target`, bound to `[Install] DefaultTarget`. Captions (the stored values are unchanged): **Automatic (by mod type)** (`followSide`, the default: client mods to this game, server mods to the server, mods for both to both), **Client and server** (`both`), **My client only** (`client`), **Server only** (`server`). Older captions (`Follow the mod's side`, `Both`, `My client`, `Server`, `Local server`) and the old `localServer` value still load. Which server **Server** reaches is the route in [Server installs](#server-installs-one-server-target). The description column describes the option on show — its lead sentence, a live `Right now:` line from the actual route (for example `Right now: server mods go to your local server at <testserver>\server.`, `… the server you're connected to (you're on its admin list).`, or `Right now: no server is available, so server mods install to this game only. They still load in private games you host.`; **Server only** with no route says `Right now: not available — …`), one short line per choice, and what "server" means. It is recomputed on every hover and the moment the selection changes (`ConfigurationPane.DefaultInstallTargetDescription`, `DefaultInstallTargetHelp`; source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified). With no server route a server-naming value installs to the client, and the Install confirmation says why (`InstallModsPane.FallbackSentence`). Readback: `action.target` is the resolved target, `action.targetSetting` the stored value, `action.targetFallback` whether it fell back, and `action.chooser` is always `false`.
- **Finding the local dedicated server (suggest-only).** Configuration offers found folders, never applies one. Sources, in this order: running `VRisingServer.exe` processes (the folder of each full image path; labeled `running now`), the Steam root from the registry (`HKCU\Software\Valve\Steam` `SteamPath`, then `HKLM\SOFTWARE\WOW6432Node\Valve\Steam` and `HKLM\SOFTWARE\Valve\Steam` `InstallPath`; labeled `Steam (registry)`), the Program Files Steam roots (`Steam`), and the uninstall key `Steam App 1829350` `InstallLocation` (both views; `Steam (uninstall entry)`). Each Steam root's `libraryfolders.vdf` libraries are searched, and in each library `steamapps\appmanifest_1829350.acf`'s `installdir` names the folder (the usual `VRisingDedicatedServer` is tried too). Everything is read-only and guarded; an absent key is no suggestion. Candidates are deduplicated by canonical path and validated as Apply validates them. Each is one settings-grid row: the path as the row name (elided in the middle when long), its source, whether BepInEx is installed and whether Progenitor started it before (a launch record) in the hover description, and a key-cap **Use this folder**. The configured folder is never listed; what discovery knows about it is appended to its status line (`Validated: <path> · running now · BepInEx installed`). With a folder configured, the other candidates fold under an `Other servers found (N)` row after the path field and its status line (**Show** / **Hide**, collapsed by default; the open state is kept per configured path across the pane rebuilds, and resets when the path changes or the workspace closes); with none configured they are listed expanded. With no folder configured and exactly one candidate, it is shown prominently — `Found a dedicated server at <path> — Use this folder` — with the note that choosing it makes that folder the local server, so installs to the server and Stop server act on it. A live server on the same machine can be found this way; it is still only a suggestion. Source-checked and game-free tested (fake registry and process sources), not live-verified.
- **Running server line.** While `VRisingServer.exe` runs from the configured folder, the Local server section shows the short line `Server running — stop it before installing or removing`; the full sentence (with the process path) is the description when that row is hovered, and the `serverInstall` readback's `reason`.
- **Pending uninstall.** Removing an ordinary (non-hot) *loaded* mod no longer deletes anything: a `<file>.uninstall` marker goes beside the reload copy and the ledger row is flagged `uninstallPending` (`UninstallMarkers`), so the mod stays loaded and **Reactivate** can take the request back. Bootstrap honors the marker at the next start — see [hot-reload.md](hot-reload.md). A hot-loadable Progenitor mod keeps today's immediate hot unload (nothing survives to the next start to be marked), and a pending install that never loaded is canceled by deleting the written file.
- **Pending bar.** `N mods installed · M uninstalled — restart the game to apply. »`; pressing the text expands one row per pending mod with `Pending restart · to load` / `Pending restart · to uninstall` in its color. **Restart now** is still the RESTART-job stub. POLISH1 (live defect H): the sentence names the processes the pending changes are actually waiting on — `restart the local server to apply` when every pending half is on the local server and `restart the game and the local server to apply` when both are, read off where the ledgered files are (`InstallModsPane.PendingSides`, readback `pending.sides: client|server|both|none`).
- **Readback.** `listing.state` adds `penduninst`; `listing.uninstallPending` and `listing.newTag` are new; the opened row's `action.prompt {kind, heading, body, yes, no, chooser, target, ready, refusal, dependents}` is the box in the words it is showing (`chooser` is always `false`, `ready` says whether the download and inspection behind it have finished, `refusal` carries `Cannot install — …`); `action.plan.dependencies` carries DEPS1's closure rows with `action.plan.closureCount`; `action.result.members` names every package one confirmed install wrote; `action.removed.pending` says the removal was recorded as a request; the pending bar reads `pending {count, uninstall, sides, open, items[{key, state}]}`. POLISH1 (live defect F): every string the `mods_ui` readback puts on the bridge is escaped to `\uXXXX` ASCII (`ModsUiResult.JsonOptions` names `JavaScriptEncoder.Default` on purpose), so no reader's encoding choice can turn `»` into `Â»`; a readback that shows mojibake is being decoded wrongly after the bridge, not written wrongly before it.
- **Kit gaps (not implemented here; `UnityUiRuntime.cs` / `MenuPanelLayout.cs` are not this job's files).** There is no tag cell for the mock's small `new` chip and no row-level tone, so the tag is said in the caption and an orange row is drawn by toning its name cell and its button; `InstallModsPane.PendingBarRows` still reserves two rows of chrome, so an expanded pending list overlaps the list's row budget estimate.
