**Inventory refresh (HOT3).** `ModMenuInventory.Snapshot()` is taken fresh on every tab build, and an install or remove that changed what this process has loaded now marks a retake owed (`ModMenuInventory.Invalidate`, cleared by the next snapshot) through `ModsMenu.NoteInventoryChanged`, which rebuilds the Mods sidebar at once when that tab is the one on show — so a plugin hot loaded or hot unloaded from the Install tab appears in, or leaves, the Mods list within the same workspace open instead of waiting for the next one. A Remove the reloader answered with an unload is recorded for the session: the reloader drops the assembly about a second after the file goes and the snapshot the pane was built with still names the plugin, so without that record the rebuild straight after the Remove read the stale evidence back and the row stood at `installed` / **Installed**; the row now reads `none` / **Install** at once and one re-check is posted `InstallModsPane.HotUnloadRecheckSeconds` (2 s, one reloader scan plus slack) later through the menu's `MainThread.PostDelayed`, dropped if the pane was released or the selection moved. A re-install of the same listing takes the row back. **Note:** a mod whose plugin id falls under `progenitor` (the HotDemo's `progenitor.hotdemo`) used to be classed `required` by `ModMenuInventory.FrameworkIds` (then a namespace prefix; an exact allowlist since FWALLOW1) and listed in the collapsed **Required (N)** group; MODSPOLISH1 made the install ledger the stronger evidence, so an id under this namespace that the ledger lists as one of the player's mods is in the default list with Remove (`ModMenuInventory.IsLedgeredMod`), and only the framework's own unledgered images stay required.

Handshake levels become `Required by server` / `Optional on this server` / `Not required by server`. When no catalog envelope is present the overview says `Server mod list unavailable. Known requirements are shown where available.` The overview has no `Installed` or `Enabled` label; a mod page's status line reports `Enabled` for a loaded mod (SETTINGS1).

**Turning mods off** is the on/off switch on each mod's page (SETTINGS1 stage 3, [mod-settings.md](mod-settings.md#stage-3-the-enable-switch)): hot-loadable Progenitor mods switch live, restart-only ones at the next start, and ordinary BepInEx plugins whose files are known through a next-start request the preloader carries out. **This UI does not offer** a mod-manager installer or profiles (the [per-server mod pause](#per-server-mod-pause) opt-out is a file setting). The overview points at the Install mods tab for installing and updating and at a mod's own page for removing a Progenitor-installed mod.

## Per-server mod pause

**Status: implemented; source-checked and game-free tested (`ServerModPauseTests`), not live-verified.
Needs a client Bootstrap restart to deploy ([hot reload](hot-reload.md#per-server-mod-pause-unload-reason-suspend)).**

Some of your mods only work when the server runs them too: they declare `ServerMustHave`. When you
join a server that does not run one of them, Progenitor now pauses it for that server instead of
refusing the join, and shows a notice in the game's popup look (the dialog kit: the popup frame, the
title, the body, a vanilla **OK** button; it closes by itself after 20 s, or with **OK** or Escape).
It lists every paused mod on its own line with its readable name and why (at most 8, then `and N more`):

```
Mods paused for this server
Paused 2 mods for this server. They're off while you're on Night Garden and come back when you leave:
RotorCamera · Not allowed on this server
Hot Demo · This server doesn't run it
```

- The reasons: `This server doesn't run it` (a `ServerMustHave` mod the server lacks), `Another version than this server's (exact match required)` / `(same minor required)` (an optional mod under strict version matching, below), `This server doesn't run Progenitor` (a server without Progenitor; a note then names mods still running because they were loaded at game start), and CLIENTPOLICY2's `Not allowed on this server` (the server's client mod policy, Only approved, does not allow it; the same pause takes it before the handshake reply and the mod report, using the policy the server last signed, remembered in its profile below; [client-mod-policy.md](client-mod-policy.md#stage-2-as-built)). One notice covers both kinds when one reply pauses both.
- `1 mod` / `N mods` and `It's off … comes back` / `They're off … come back` follow the count; the server's name comes from its profile (its signed catalog), else `this server`.
- Requested 2026-10-05 (the notice was a flat box with a full-width bar); source-checked, built and game-free tested (`JoinNoticeKitTests`), not live-verified.

**What can be paused.** Only your own hot-loaded mods (from `BepInEx/progenitor-reload`) that declare
`ServerMustHave` for the failing row. A mod that declares it works without its server half
(`NoNeedForSync`) or is client-only (`ClientOnly`) is never paused, not even as a dependency of one
that is: a pause that would have to take one is not made. A mod's DLL is the unit (every plugin in
it goes together), and hot-loaded mods that `[BepInDependency]` on a paused mod are paused with it.
Mods loaded at game start (`BepInEx/plugins`) and mods that use `ctx.UnsafeHarmony` are never touched:
if one of those is what fails, the join is refused as before and the refusal panel adds
`this mod was loaded when the game started, so it cannot be paused for one server; to join, restart V Rising without it.`
On a server without Progenitor such a mod stays loaded and the notice names it.

**Optional mods under strict version matching** (mod compatibility C3, [decision 4](mod-compatibility.md#decisions-2026-10-09)).
When the server's handshake request announces **Exact version for all** or **Same minor for all** (the request's
`MatchExact` / `MatchSameMinor` bits; its rows already carry the strict rule), a both-sides optional mod
(`NoNeedForSync`) that the server runs at another version is paused for this server like a `ServerMustHave` one, with
the reason `Another version than this server's (exact match required)` or `… (same minor required)`. The other
rules still hold: only a pausable hot-loaded mod is paused (otherwise the join is refused by the server as before),
every other plugin the pause would take is still protected, and the pause is made only when the client predicts the
join is then clean (a server that declares the mod `ServerMustHave` refuses a client without it, so nothing is
paused). A later request from the same server does not resume it only to pause it again. A request carried across a
Core swap is answered without the strict bits, so the optional mod is not paused then. Under declared ranges nothing
changes. Source-checked and game-free tested (`StrictMatchPauseTests`), not live-verified.

**When it pauses.** On a Progenitor server the client predicts, before it answers the handshake,
whether the server would refuse it (the same `Compare.Evaluate` the server runs, on exactly the rows
the reply would carry). It pauses only if pausing makes that prediction clean, only when the server's
request was not full (16 rows may have been truncated), and never a mod the server asked about. The
reply is sent one frame later, after the pause, well inside the server's 20 s answer deadline. If
the connection ends before then, nothing is paused, no reply is sent and no profile is written; if
Core is swapped before then, the next Core answers the request after its plugins reload. On a
server that sends no handshake request at all, the join is treated as a server without Progenitor
once `[Networking] NoProgenitorGraceSeconds` (default 10) passes after the client reaches the game
world. A Progenitor server asks from its `OnUserConnected` handler; client logs show that request
answered in the same join as the prefab packet sent from the same place, which is inferred (not
timed) to precede the client reaching the world. This detection is fail-safe: it fires only when
Bootstrap says this client is **not** hosting a private game (a private game's server never sends
the hosting player a request, so silence proves nothing there) and the connect address has a
recognized remote form (`a.b.c.d:port`, `[v6]:port` or `name.domain:port`, not loopback). An empty or
unrecognised address (for example a P2P session id), an unreadable hosting flag, or a request already
seen on this connection (even across a Running/Loading flicker) means no pause. **Which address forms
real Steam, Lidgren and P2P joins produce needs live verification**; until then some legitimate
servers may simply not be detected. A mod the server's recorded request asked about is never paused
by this path either.

**When they come back.** On a real disconnect (kick, leaving to the menu, world end). Also, on every
handshake request: if the server asks about a paused mod (its own id or any row it provided), it is
loaded again *before* the reply (with the paused mods it depends on, dependencies first), so
switching to a server that runs your paused mods works even if the previous connection ended
abnormally; this also works after a Core update, because Bootstrap keeps the row ids each paused mod
provided. If a mod the server asks for cannot load yet (its previous plugin context has not been
collected), the reply waits for it (up to 8 s, while Bootstrap keeps retrying) so it carries the
mod's rows; only if it loads after that does the client re-answer the handshake. `ModsResumed` is
published when a mod has actually loaded. A mod released on a leave that is still loading when you
join a server that does not run it (it declared a `ServerMustHave` row that server did not ask
about, or the server has no Progenitor) is kept paused instead of loading mid-join. On the Mods tab
such a mod reads `Resuming…` until it loads. A slow Progenitor server whose request arrives after the
grace period is handled the same way. A Core update while connected keeps paused mods paused.

**Per-server settings.** `BepInEx/config/progenitor-server-profiles.json` remembers, per connect
address, the last paused ids, when, whether the server looked like it ran Progenitor, and its name
and operator fingerprint once a signed catalog arrived; CLIENTPOLICY2 adds `clientPolicy` (the client
mod policy the server last signed, for the automatic pause; at most 32 servers keep one). To stop automatic pausing for one server,
set `"autoPause": false` on its entry; that server then refuses the join as before. There is no menu
control for this yet. If the file exists but cannot be read or parsed, automatic pausing is off for
every server (logged once) and the file is never overwritten; writes go through a temp file and
`File.Replace`.

**On the Mods tab** a paused mod stays listed; its row reads `Paused for this server` (a hollow diamond).

**The switch and the pause (SETTINGS1 stage 3).** They are separate facts and the player's choice wins. A mod the player turned off on its page is `Disabled`: it is unloaded through the same Bootstrap suspend table, but it is *held* (`ModSwitch.IsHeld`, from `progenitor-mod-state.json`), so a leave or a server request never resumes it, `ServerModPause.Current` does not list it, and `ModsPaused` / `ModsResumed` are not published for it. The plugins a live switch-off took with it are held too. Turning a held mod on while the joined server's last pause took it keeps it paused until the player leaves.

**Counterparts (SETTINGS1 stage 3).** A client mod that declares it runs partly without its server part (`[Counterpart(CounterpartNeed.Limited)]` or `Optional`) declares its handshake row `NoNeedForSync`, so it is never paused and never refused for a server that lacks the server part: it runs, its `needsServerPart` rows are grayed with their fallback, and its page says `Server part not on this server · limited` ([mod-settings.md](mod-settings.md#counterparts)).

**For mod authors.** A paused plugin is unloaded with `PluginUnloadReason.Suspend` (keep state, like
`Swap`). `ServerModPause.Current` reports what is paused, and `ModsPaused` / `ModsResumed` are
published on the event bus ([API](api.md#networking)).

**Limits of this increment.** No in-game setting for the opt-out; the prediction cannot see server
rows the request did not carry (a server's `ClientOnly` copy of a mod), so it may pause a mod the
server would have accepted; a mod that needs more than 8 s to load may still cost that join (the
server may refuse before the re-answer). A deferred request carried across a Core swap is answered
only on the same live connection (same address and join, client loading or in the world);
otherwise it is discarded.

### Live acceptance procedure (not run)

Isolated test server only (`<testserver>`, ports 9976/9977); never the live server.
Requires a client whose Bootstrap and Core include this change (Bootstrap restart).

1. Put a hot-loaded client-side plugin that declares
   `[NetworkCompatibility(ServerMustHave, …)]` in the client's `BepInEx/progenitor-reload`, and make
   sure the test server does not run it. Join. Expected: client log
   `ServerModPause: paused <id> for <host>.`, `Reload: <id> unload reason=Suspend (bootstrap-slot)`,
   the notice, `Handshake: ok`, and the Mods tab row `Paused for this server`.
2. Leave to the menu. Expected: `ServerModPause: resumed <id> (disconnect).` and the plugin loaded.
3. Install the same plugin on the test server, rejoin. Expected: nothing paused, `Handshake: ok`.
4. Remove it from the server again, join (paused), then kill the connection without a normal leave
   and join the server after reinstalling the plugin there. Expected: `resumed <id> (server-runs-it)`
   before `Handshake: server asked about …; replied`.
5. Set `"autoPause": false` for the test server's host in `progenitor-server-profiles.json` and
   rejoin. Expected: the old refusal and deny panel.
6. Start the test server without Progenitor (plain BepInEx) and join. Expected after about
   `NoProgenitorGraceSeconds`: `no Progenitor handshake request within …`, the no-Progenitor notice,
   and the plugin paused; leaving resumes it.
7. With a `ServerMustHave` plugin in `BepInEx/plugins` (restart-only), repeat step 1. Expected: the
   refusal panel with the restart line; nothing paused.

8. For every join route used in the session (direct IP, server browser, reconnect after a
   disconnect, private game), log Bootstrap's `ProcessHost.ConnectionIdentity` after the join (for
   example through the Dev bridge) and confirm it is non-empty and changes on each new join. A route
   that leaves it empty cannot carry a deferred request across a Core swap.
9. Confirm the connect address (`ModManifest.ReadLiveConnectHost`: the server's `address:port`,
   see [mod-manifest.md](mod-manifest.md#trust-rules-eq3)) is non-empty when the first handshake request arrives:
   the `ServerModPause: paused … for <host>.` line must name the host, not `this server`.

Restore the test server's plugin set and leave the client at the title screen afterwards.

## Keybinds tab

**Status: source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified** (jobs KEYS1, KEYS2 and KEYS3, `tests/Progenitor.Tests/KeybindTabTests.cs`, `KeybindGuardTests.cs` and `KeybindStyleTests.cs`, full suite green). Nothing on this tab has been observed in the game. The KEYS3 look (the game's Bindings Menu) and its borrowed sprites are source-checked against the client metadata only; which donor each part resolves to at the title and in a world is not known until the live probes in the internal research notes run. The game-bindings reader in particular has never run; its calls are source-checked against the client metadata and analyze only.

The fourth workspace tab (decided 2026-09-29; since three homes, 2026-10-06, between Install Mods and Preferences). It is added through the same strip (`ModsTabStrip.Order`) and pane path as the other three tabs, so it appears on the title screen and in game. `mods_ui select_tab tab=keybinds` selects it; `status.tab` reads `keybinds`.

### What it shows

The tab is drawn after the game's own **Bindings Menu** (Esc > Options > Controls > Show Bindings). Two panes share the frame: the key list on the left and a description column on the right (`MenuPaneMode.ListWithAside` / `Aside`; the column is 22% of the frame, clamped to 240-400 px and never more than 40%). Neither pane draws a title band.

1. **Toolbar** (pinned at the top, one row inside the list pane, so it never reaches the description column). After the search, which grows into what the rest leave, come three visibly separate groups (requested 2026-09-30), set apart by extra space: the **filter**, a muted *Show* lead-in and **All** / **Conflicts N** / **Changed** as one three-way choice (the chosen one in the kit's selected accent, the other two muted); the **section jumps**, `↓ Mod keys` and `↓ Game keys` as small quiet captions (the current section's bright, the other muted); and the **mod picker**, `Jump to mod ▼`, a bar with a chevron drawn with the game's key-cap sprite, which opens the framework's pick-one list of the mods that have keys. On a narrow pane the section jumps drop first (Mod keys, then Game keys), then **Changed**, the *Show* lead-in and the filter's leading space; the search and the picker never drop. These are built from existing cell kinds (buttons with tones, muted text, the key-cap skin). A true joined segmented control, text links that brighten on hover and a dropdown bar need runtime cell skins, requested separately.
2. **Edit bar** (pinned, only while a key is being changed; it pushes the list down and never draws over it): one short line, `<mod> · <key>: press a key` while waiting, else the chord about to be saved (`… : Ctrl+F7`), with **Swap**, **Save**, **Unbind** and **Cancel**. Refusals and the preview sentence are in the description column (and `edit.status`).
3. **Mod keys**, first (requested 2026-09-30): a **Mod keys** group header, then one section per mod, in the Mods list's order. Mods without a list entry come after, by name. Each mod heading is the mod's name in the category size with a plain muted `»` about one space after it (when the mod has a Mods-list entry), and its key count at the right. The whole heading is the link: hovering it brightens the `»` and says `Open this mod's page.` in the description column, and a click opens the page. The glyph's place comes from the name's estimated width times 1.15, so a name much wider than its estimate could be cut (a runtime that measures the caption would place it exactly). Each mod heading has 12 px more space above it than a game category and the category underline under its name. Rows list the mod's Progenitor binds first, then any `KeyboardShortcut` / `KeyCode` settings from its own config file.
4. **Game keys**: a **Game keys** group header, then the game's keys under the Bindings Menu's own categories in the page's order: Movement, Skills & Attacks, General, Camera, Panels, Action Bar, Building. Movement starts with **Move Up**, **Move Left**, **Move Down** and **Move Right** (KEYS4; W, A, S, D by default), the halves of the game's analog `MoveX` / `MoveY` axes that the page lists as rows, read with the game's own slot selection (`GetBindingIndexInAction(action, primary, isAnalog: true, positive)`) and named with `TryGetAnalogInputActionLocalization`. Live 2026-09-30 they were missing, and a remap to W was offered as free. After the page's seven categories come two of ours (KEYS4, from the live sweep of the game's input asset on 2026-09-30, which found 35 keyboard-and-mouse actions the tab did not list): **Other game keys**, every other action the game registers with a keyboard-and-mouse binding, each with the screen it works in (Back, Enter and Select Progression `in menus`; Cycle Chat `in chat`; the map's pan, marker, center, legend and zoom `in the map`; the inventory and crafting screens' item, craft, recipe and routing keys; Ability 4; Building Exit and Pickup `while building`; the camera's pan halves `in camera modes`; and the mouse axes Zoom Camera and Rotate Camera X / Y), and **Debug keys** (Debug Menu F1, Disable Auto Snap Points / Grid), listed with their keys but never compared, because the game's debug menu is off for players (the description column says so). `ActionBar9` (key 9 in the asset) is not listed: it has no `ButtonInputAction` value, so the game never registers it. Actions with no keyboard-and-mouse binding (menu navigation, the camera buttons, admin keys, gamepad composites) are not listed. Mouse controls are shown with the game's words (`Mouse Button 4` is the forward button, `Mouse Button 5` the back button, `Scroll Wheel`, `Mouse X`). The middle, back and forward buttons are keys since KEYS4 (below) and clash like keys, so Auto Run holds the forward button; the left and right buttons and the mouse axes never clash. The actions the game has cleared (Walk, Vampire Power 9-12, Emote 9-12, the Vampire Powers and Emotes menus, Ability Details) show empty caps, as live. A category the catalog does not know comes after them. The titles are the game's localized words when a `RebindingMenu` was reached this Core generation (its `LK_Category_*` keys); otherwise they are the English labels from `GameKeybindCatalog.CategoryOrder`, which are the game's own words in `English.json`. The readback says which (`titleSource: game|catalog`). Its muted note line (on its own line under the group header, shown while any game category is) says `Click a game key to change it. This changes the game's own controls (also in Esc > Options > Controls).` where the game's keys can be written ([KEYS9](#changing-the-games-keys-keys9)), and `Change game keys in Esc > Options > Controls.` in a host without the game's writer. At the title screen, where the game's keys cannot be read, the categories come from the last in-world read kept in `progenitor-game-keys.txt` (every slot, with the game's key text, category and description), and the note starts `As last read in a world today.` or `As last read in a world on September 28, 2026.`, followed by `The game hasn't loaded its controls yet, so game keys can't be changed. Try again in a moment.` (user report 2026-10-12: the game's keys are read and written at the title too, because the game's input system has its actions loaded there; the last-read file is only the fallback while they cannot be read) There is no button that opens the game's own Controls page. The only programmatic route drives the Esc menu's own UI handlers, which is neither read-only nor verified.
5. **Reset all mod keys**, pinned at the bottom right like the game's "Reset to default key bindings".

**Groups and separators.** A group is a 30 px row of space, then its title row (28 px type, filling a 44 px row), then a full-width divider (the game's ornament when it was borrowed, else the kit divider), so the boundary between the mods and the game shows at a glance. The spacing runs group (30 px) > between mod sections (12 px) > between rows (the row gap). The **Mod keys** and **Game keys** jumps land on their group header.

**Live updates** (KEYS4, found live 2026-09-30). While the tab is shown, a bind that is registered, removed or changed (a mod's hot reload, a conflict dialog's answer, a config-file key written) shows at once: when the rows are the same, they are rewritten in place, so the scroll, the selection and a capture in progress stay; when a row came or went, the tab is rebuilt keeping its scroll, and while a row is being edited that rebuild waits until the edit ends so its capture is not lost.

**Default focus.** Opening the tab lands on the **Mod keys** group, and its jump is shown as the current one. A link from the Mods page that names a mod still lands on that mod. A rebuild after a save keeps the scroll position (user report 2026-10-10: it jumped to the top. The workspace's content-scroll lookup returned the first shown non-sidebar pane, which on this tab is the description column, so the key list's offset was never captured or restored; the lookup now skips the description column (`UnityPanel.IsAside`), and the restored offset is also re-applied through the layout's settle pass. Live 2026-10-12, client at the title: list scrolled to 0.40, a mod key changed and saved, still 0.40 after the rebuild); switching to another tab and back starts at Mod keys again, so a remembered scroll never fights the default. The default does not clear a search or filter left on the tab.

**Rows.** Every key is one line, like the game's: the name at the left, then two key caps at the right, then a gutter.

- **Key caps.** The primary cap and the secondary cap, each 150 px, drawn with the game's key-cap sprite when one was borrowed (below), else the kit button. An unbound slot is an empty cap. A chord is written modifiers first without spaces (`Ctrl+Shift+F12`), and a long one is drawn smaller (down to 11 px) before it is cut; the readback keeps the full text (`primary`) beside the caption (`primaryCaption`).
  - On a **game row** the primary cap is the action's first key and the secondary cap its second key, as on the game's page. At the title or in a world (2026-10-12; before, only in a world), clicking either one on an action of the game's Bindings Menu starts the same capture for that slot ([KEYS9](#changing-the-games-keys-keys9)). Otherwise the click selects the row and puts the reason in the description column, and no capture starts: `The game hasn't loaded its controls yet, …` before the game has loaded its controls, `The game's Controls page doesn't offer this key for rebinding, so it can't be changed here.` for the **Other game keys**, the **Debug keys** and the mouse axes, and `Change game keys in Esc > Options > Controls.` in a host without the game's writer.
  - On a **mod row** the primary cap is the bind's chord, and clicking it starts the KEYS2 capture (`press a key…`); every refusal and the Swap offer are unchanged. An ordinary mod's key setting has no secondary cap (decided 2026-09-30: the empty, unclickable cap confused players); its column is empty space, so every row's caps and gutter line up, and the readback reports no `secondaryCap` for it. PAD1: on a **Progenitor bind** the secondary cap is its **controller key** ([controller keybinds](modules.md#controller-keybinds)), empty when it has none and always live: a click starts the controller capture (`Press a controller button (B too), or hold one and press another; the key is taken when you let go. Escape, a click elsewhere or 5 seconds with no button cancels.`). The capture takes one button, or the second button pressed while the first is held, when the first button comes up (so a held button is never taken alone); two buttons going down on the same frame and three buttons are refused and it keeps waiting. While it waits the controller's menu navigation is off, Cancel (B) included, so B can be chosen. A button something the bind meets already has (the game's controller binding in the bind's scope, another bind's controller key) is refused with the holder named, and the capture keeps waiting; it is never taken. Save writes `[Keybinds] <id>.Gamepad`; the edit bar's Unbind removes the controller key; the gutter's Reset also puts the controller default back. A controller key that is not active is drawn in the warn tone and its sentence (`Controller: Not active: …`) joins the row's conflict text; the readback adds `gamepad`, `gamepadCaption`, `gamepadDefault`, `gamepadActive`, `gamepadChanged` and `gamepadConflict`. Second keyboard keys for mod binds are on the roadmap. A mod key this tab cannot write shows its reason in the column instead.
  - The cap whose key is not active has its caption in the warn tone, and a short warn marker left of the caps says `Not active` (or `Reserved` for a key on a reserved chord), so a conflict shows without hovering.
- **Gutter.** On a key this tab can write: reset, drawn as the game's own reset icon (`Icon_Reset`, the arrow on the game's Options and Bindings rows, borrowed and owned-copied like the other art; a small **Reset** button when it was not borrowed), and a small **Unbind** button. Hovering either says what it does in the description column (`Reset to default: F9.`, `Unbind this key (no key).`). They stay drawn and are dimmed when they do not apply, and hovering then says why (`Already the default.`, `Already unbound (no key).`, `This key has no default to reset to.`). A read-only row keeps an empty gutter of the same width, so every row's caps line up. The hover is the kit's pointer trigger on the button (a cell built with `CellSpec.Hover`), through Bootstrap's pointer slots; there is no tooltip near the button.
- **Highlight.** The row being changed, else the hovered row, else the selected one (the last row clicked), is the *current* row (user report 2026-10-10: hovering another row during a change hid the change's preview). A row the pointer leaves stays current for 150 ms (`KeybindsPane.HoverLeaveGraceMs`, cleared from the tab's frame tick), so crossing the gap between two rows no longer flashes the selected row's description; the bridge's `hover` with an empty target leaves at once. It is drawn as a solid band in the kit's soft selection tone (the MODLIST1 selected row, `ModsListLayout.RowFill(selected: true)`), with the game's `ListButton_TopGradient` as a faint top rule (at most 3 px, alpha capped at 0.35) when it was borrowed, and its name turns from the muted tone to the normal text tone. The game's own glow sprite (`ListButton_Glow2`) is not used: live it drew as a harsh white band, and its tint could not be found in a world. The band's brightness is capped (brightest channel times alpha at most 0.27), and `art.highlight` reads `source: kit, tint: … from kit-soft-selection`. A name whose key is not active or reserved stays in the warn tone.

**Description column.** It shows the current row, as the game's details panel does:

- the name as a heading, then the game's ornament under it (the kit divider when none was borrowed);
- the body: a game action's own localized description (the second key of `TryGetButtonInputActionLocalization`, the one the game's details panel shows), or a mod key's help;
- the warning line: the edit bar's status while this row is being changed (the capture prompt, a refusal, the preview), then the reserved-key sentence, then the conflict sentence. It is in the warn tone when this key is not active, is reserved or was just refused, and muted otherwise (a winner's `Also bound by <mod> (not active there).`);
- the notes, muted: a game cap's reason it cannot be changed, `The game's default is W.` for a changed game key, `Works only while building.` (and the other mode-only notes) for a game key, `From its config file.` and a not-editable reason for a config-file key, and `The default is <default>.`

With no current row it reads **Keybinds** and `Point at a key to see what it does. Click a mod's key to change it.` A rebuild after a save keeps the selected row.

Hover is the kit's existing pointer trigger (the `EventTrigger` buttons already carry, through Bootstrap's pointer slots), added only to rows built with `CellRowSpec.Hover`. Two side effects: moving onto a row's own caps does not count as leaving it, and dragging on a row no longer scrolls the list; the wheel and the scrollbar still do.

**Borrowed visuals.** `KeybindArtDonors` reads the game's menus and never modifies or clones them. For each part it takes the first rung that has it: a menu in a loaded scene (`live`); the sprite kept from an earlier read (`cached`); a menu prefab already in memory, or else a loaded sprite of the name found live (`resident`, at most 3 scans per world); else the kit (`kit`). A found sprite is held on an image of one hidden `DontDestroyOnLoad` object (`progenitor-ui-core-keybind-art`), and its pixels are copied once into a Progenitor-owned texture and sprite with the same size, pivot, pixels per unit and 9-slice border (`owned-copy`, drawn in preference to the original). Live, holding the reference did not stop the world load releasing the key cap's texture. The copy is made on the GPU (`Graphics.Blit` of the sprite's atlas region into a temporary `RenderTexture`, then `Texture2D.ReadPixels` and `Sprite.Create`), because the game's textures are not CPU-readable. A sprite whose transparent edges were trimmed by atlas packing is padded back to its full rect. The copy is refused, with the reason logged, for a sprite rotated in its atlas or larger than 1M pixels (4096 on a side); the borrowed original is then used while it stays loaded. The holder and the Core sprites are destroyed on Core unload. Each copy's pixels are also kept in Bootstrap's process-lifetime sprite table, so after a Core hot swap made in a world the new generation adopts the copies made earlier in the process (`kept`); the ornament and the category rule are also found by name among loaded sprites. See [Borrowed game art across Core swaps](ui.md#borrowed-game-art-across-core-swaps-buttonart1-source-checked-and-game-free-tested-not-live-verified). The rungs are `live`, `owned-copy`, `cached`, `kept`, `resident`, `kit`; each part's readback adds `copy` (`owned`, or why not). The scene is scanned once per world, and again only while a part is still missing. Every fall-through is logged once with why each rung failed (`Keybinds tab: art keyCap live=… cached=… resident=… -> kit`). The Bindings Menu's own entry (`RebindingMenu.ControlsInputEntryPrefab`) is preferred to the Options page's "Show Bindings" button (`OptionsPanel_Controls.ButtonPrefab`), which has the same look.

| Part | Donors, in order | Kit fallback |
|---|---|---|
| Key cap | `SettingsEntry_Binding.PrimaryButton` image; `SettingsEntry_Button.Button` image | today's kit button |
| Row highlight top rule | the entry's image drawing `ListButton_TopGradient` (`SettingsButton_Background/Gradient`) | none (the band is always the kit's soft selection) |
| Ornament | `RebindingMenu.DetailsDivider`; `OptionsMenu.DetailsDivider` | the kit divider |
| Category underline | an image under `RebindingMenu.HeaderPrefab` / `OptionsPanel_Controls.HeaderPrefab` whose object or sprite name says line, underline, divider, separator or rule (a guess; `art.headerImages` lists them) | none drawn |

The log line `Keybinds tab: art keyCap=<rung>(<donor>:<sprite>) highlight=… ornament=…` is written once per change. `mods_ui keys op=read` returns `art`.

Filtering and search hide rows in place without a rebuild. A section title that matches the search keeps all its rows. Core's `OpenSettingsPanel` bind is shown as **Open Mods menu**; its cfg key is unchanged. Layout decisions (grouping, the row's cells, the description) are in `KeybindsTabModel` / `KeybindsTabLayout` and the ladder in `KeybindArtLadder` (game-free); `KeybindsPane` and the runtime draw them.

**Secondary chords for mod keys** are not offered. Adding them would not be cheap: a `KeyChord` is one chord, and the `[Keybinds]` cfg entry, `Keybinds.Rebind`, the pump, the winner rule, the reserved and remap guards, the mod page's Keys rows and the problems line all assume one chord per bind.

### Keybind groups

**Status: source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified** (`tests/Progenitor.Tests/KeybindGroupTests.cs`). Nothing here has been observed in the game.

A group moves some of one mod's binds out of that mod's section into a titled section of their own, placed immediately after it. It changes only the display: the binds, their ids, their `[Keybinds]` cfg entries and the winner rule are unchanged. Core defines groups through the internal `KeybindGroups.Define(ownerPluginId, groupId, title, orderedBindIds, infoRows, unavailableReason)`, which returns an `IDisposable`.

- **Rows** follow `orderedBindIds`, whatever order the binds were registered in. A listed id that is not registered is skipped. Only Progenitor binds move; config-file keys stay with their mod. If a mod's section is left empty, it is dropped, and a `mod:<id>` jump lands on the mod's first group.
- **Read-only rows** (`KeybindInfoRow(Id, Name, ChordText, Description, Note)`) come after the key rows. Use them for controls the keybind API cannot bind. Each is drawn as a muted cap with its fixed input. It has no capture, reset or Unbind and never takes part in a conflict, Reset all, or the Changed and Conflicts counts. Clicking it shows its note. The description column shows its `Description` and `Note`, and search matches its name, input and note. The heading's key count leaves these rows out.
- **Unavailable.** While `unavailableReason` returns text, a muted note under the group's heading shows that text. The rows stay visible and can still be changed. The section readback reports it as `unavailable` (read when the readback is taken) and `unavailableShown` (the note as drawn, updated on the next filter pass or rebuild).
- **Section id:** `group:<groupId>` (`KeybindsTabLayout.GroupSectionId`). It is listed in the toolbar's `jumpTargets` and the **Jump to mod** picker, and `mods_ui keys op=jump target=group:<groupId>` lands on it. If you define the same group id again, the new definition replaces the old one. Disposing the handle removes the group, and its binds return to the owner's section.

**Photo mode** is the first group. Its section is **Photo mode**, placed right after **Progenitor**, with rows in this order: toggle (Ctrl+Shift+F9), exit (Escape), forward, back, left, right, up, down, fast, slow, zoom in, zoom out, lock on (middle mouse; hold to target, tap to release), hide HUD and pause (`PhotoMode`, `PhotoModeExit`, then `PhotoModeForward` … `PhotoModePause`), each with a short description such as `Pause the world (admins only).` Two read-only rows follow: **Look around** (`Hold right mouse`) and **Flying speed** (`Mouse wheel`), each with a `Fixed: …` note (photo mode reads the right button and the wheel itself as its look and speed controls; they are not keybinds). While the connected server disables photo mode (`[PhotoMode] AllowOnThisServer = false`), the section notes `Photo mode is disabled on this server`; the note redraws when the server's answer changes. Defined in `PhotoMode.Install`, removed on Core unload.

### Who gets a key: first installed

Decided 2026-09-29. `KeybindArbiter` decides; the tab, the pump, the mod page's Keys rows, the problems line and the Mods list all read the same verdict.

- **Exact chords only.** Two binds clash only when the main key and the modifiers are the same, which is the rule the pump fires by. A mod on `Ctrl + W` does not clash with the game's `W`.
- **The game always comes first**, for its always-active gameplay keys. A mod key on a chord that such an action is bound to is not active. Unbound slots, mouse and gamepad controls never clash. The **Other game keys** (KEYS4) work only in one screen, so they are mode-only keys (below): they warn and a remap onto them is refused. The **Debug keys** never clash. Actions with no keyboard-and-mouse binding (menu navigation, gamepad composites) are not listed.
- **Mode-only game keys only warn** until they are checked live: the building keys, the emote and shapeshift slots, their modifiers, and any binding whose key is itself a modifier. The mod key stays active and its row says `Also used by the game while building (Place); both fire there, and Progenitor does not stop either until that is checked in game.`
- **A lone modifier is shared with the game** (HOLDMOD1). A mod hold key on Ctrl, Shift or Alt alone meets the game's actions on that modifier (Left Alt: Advanced Tooltip, Toggle Emote Wheel; Left Ctrl: Toggle Action Wheel; Left Shift: Cliff Jump, Custom Split Item and others). An ordinary mod's own setting on it (`ModifierKey = LeftAlt`) is shared the same way. Both act on it in every scope: the mod key stays active, its row says `Alt is also held by the game (Advanced Tooltip and Toggle Emote Wheel); both act on it.` in the muted tone, it is not counted in **Conflicts N** or shown in the Conflicts view, a remap onto it is accepted, and Save never takes it from the game or the other mod or, for a game row, from the Progenitor mod. Two Progenitor mods on the same lone modifier follow the first-installed rule ([modules.md](modules.md#lone-modifier-hold-keys-holdmod1)).
- **`[Keybinds] EnforceGameWins`** in `progenitor.ui.cfg` (default `true`) switches the game-wins rule. Set it to `false` and every game clash is a warning only (`…; both fire, because [Keybinds] EnforceGameWins is off.`). It is re-read on every change.
- **Progenitor's own keys come right after the game.** Core (the Mods-menu opener, the input release key) and the dev bridge (`progenitor.dev`) outrank every mod, so no mod can take the Mods-menu opener.
- **Between mods, the one installed first wins.** Its install time comes from this client's install ledger (the earliest `writtenUtc` of the `progenitor-install.json` rows naming the plugin). Without a ledger row, it is the creation time of the plugin's DLL, or its last-write time when the creation time cannot be read or is implausible. The lookup is the shared `InstallTimes.Of`, so the Install tab's Installed column and this tab agree; `KeybindInstallTimes` only finds the plugin's file (hot-loaded ones through the reloader) and caches the answer.
- **A mod whose install time is unknown** comes after every mod whose time is known (`…, installed first (this mod's install time is unknown, so it comes after every mod whose time is known)`). Hot-loaded mods in `progenitor-reload/` are found through the reloader, the owner of their binds, or a scan of the reloader's assemblies, so their file time is known.
- **When the times are the same, or both unknown,** the mod first by plugin id wins (case-insensitive). The sentence says `which comes first by mod id (the install times are the same or unknown)`.
- The order is total and transitive: the game, then Progenitor, then known install times by time, then unknown times, with ties broken by plugin id.
- **Two binds of one mod on one key** are not a clash.
- **Scopes** (KEYS4). A key is a *gameplay* key (every mod key and every game key) or a *menu* key, which works only while the Mods workspace is open (`KeybindScope.Menu`, Core only). A menu key never meets a gameplay key. Menu keys clash with each other by the rules above, and with the game's menu keys (the **Other game keys** that work `in menus`: Back, Enter, Select Progression), which stop them. Remapping follows the same rule: a menu key may take a gameplay key's chord and a gameplay key may take a menu key's, but neither may take a key of its own scope that something has. `keys-row.scope` reads `menu`, `window` or `gameplay`; a menu row's description says `Works only while the Mods menu is open, so it never clashes with a gameplay key.` A *map* key (`KeybindScope.Map`, a plugin's `ctx.Keybinds.RegisterInMap` / `RegisterHoldInMap`, MAPTP1) works only while the game's world map is open (the full-screen menu on its Map tab); it meets other map keys, menu keys (the map is a tab of the game's full-screen menu), the game's keys that work `in the map` (Map Pan, Map Marker, Center Player, Toggle Legend, Map Zoom) or `in menus`, which stop it on an exact chord, and Push To Talk, which the game reads on every screen: that clash is shown as a warning and both fire. `keys-row.scope` reads `map`, and its description says `Works only while the map is open, so it never clashes with a gameplay key.` A *window* key (`KeybindScope.Window`, HUB2, Core only: Navigate back and forward and their Alt+arrow rows) works in menus and, outside menus, only while a window with tabs holds the input, when the game's actions are blocked. Against the game's keys it clashes as a menu key does. It is never in a clash with another mod's key: the mod's key always keeps the chord. `keys-row.scope` reads `window`. A *build* key (`KeybindScope.Build`, Core only: **Copy building**) works only while the build menu is shown, where gameplay goes on: it meets the game's gameplay actions, building keys and menu keys (the game wins), ordinary mods' key settings and mod menu and build keys (first installed wins), and Push To Talk as a warning (both act). A Progenitor gameplay key on the same chord is not a conflict: the build key acts while the build menu is open, the gameplay key everywhere else (see the note under the table). `keys-row.scope` reads `build`, `scopeText` reads `Build mode`, and the Keybinds tab lists it in a **Build mode** group.

**Where keys meet** (HUB2). Two keys on one exact chord are a conflict, with a row warning and, for the losing mod, a dialog, when both are meant to act in the same place. The rows are the key's scope; each cell says what happens against the other key.

| Key \ other | Game gameplay action (Auto Run, Interact) | Game key in menus (Back, Enter) | Game key in the map | Mod gameplay key | Mod menu key | Mod map key | Photo / chat |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Gameplay (mod) | conflict: the game wins | warning, both fire | warning, both fire | conflict: first installed | none (see the note) | none (see the note) | none |
| Menu | none | conflict: the game wins | none | none (see the note) | conflict | conflict | none |
| Map | none | conflict: the game wins | conflict: the game wins | none (see the note) | conflict | conflict | none |
| Build (Core) | conflict: the game wins (building keys too) | conflict: the game wins | none | Progenitor bind: none, shared by press (see the note); ordinary mod's key setting: conflict, first installed | conflict | none | none |
| Window (Core) | none: outside menus it acts only while the game's input is blocked | conflict: the game wins (no dialog) | none | none: the mod key acts, quiet row note | none: the mod key acts, quiet row note | none: the mod key acts, quiet row note | none |
| Photo | none | none | none | none | none | none | photo with photo only |
| Chat | game in-chat keys: both stay live | none | none | none | none | none | chat with chat only |

- **A window key always gives way to a mod's key** (any scope, a Progenitor bind or an ordinary mod's key setting): the mod key stays active and its mod never gets a dialog. At each press the window key stands aside when that mod key acts on the same press: an ordinary mod's key setting always counts (that mod reads the key itself); a Progenitor bind counts when it is active, its scope is open, and it may run while a panel holds the input if one does. So one press fires exactly one of them, never both and never neither. Core's window keys never raise a key-conflict dialog of their own.
- **A build key and a Progenitor gameplay key share the chord by press.** Neither is reported as losing and no dialog appears. While the build menu is open, the build key acts and the gameplay key stands aside on that press (`suppressed: Progenitor's CopyBuilding has this key while the build menu is open`); with the build menu closed, the gameplay key acts as usual. So rebinding **Copy building** onto another mod's key never turns that key off in play. The gameplay key's row has a quiet note naming the build key. An ordinary mod's key setting cannot be stopped by Progenitor, so against a build key it stays a conflict by the first-installed rule.
- **The game's full-screen menu.** The Esc / K / J / M / P menu is a menu for menu keys, map keys and window keys, but Progenitor does not hold the input there, so a mod's gameplay key also fires there. A menu or map key and a gameplay key on one chord can therefore both fire in that menu (and in the map, which is one of its tabs); the Keybinds tab does not report that pair. Menu keys are Core only and Core registers none today; map keys are a mod API (`RegisterInMap`). Window keys are covered by the rule above.
- A game key that works only in one mode (`while building`, `in the emote menu`) is a warning against a gameplay key, never a conflict. Push To Talk is read on every screen: against a map key or a build key it is a warning, and both fire.
- The game's inventory actions listed under General (Drop Item, Split Item, Custom Split Item, Repair Item, Smart Take Items, Auto Transfer Items, Sort Items) work only in the inventory, so they count as `in inventory and crafting screens` keys: a warning against a mod key, not a conflict.

What the losing side sees:

| Loser | Behavior | Row sentence |
|---|---|---|
| A Progenitor bind | **Enforced.** Its handler does not run while the conflict exists. The log says `Ui: keybind '<plugin>/<id>' was pressed but not fired: …` once per press. | `Not active: F8 is used by Hunts, installed first. Pick another key.` / `… used by the game (Interact), which always comes first. …` |
| An ordinary mod's config-file key | Reported only. Progenitor cannot stop another mod from reading its keys. | `F8 is also used by Hunts, installed first. Both fire; Progenitor can't stop this mod.` |

- **The winner's row** says `Also bound by <mod> (not active there).`, or `…, which still fires` when the loser is a config-file key.
- **A game row a mod wants** says `Also wanted by <mod>; the game keeps it.` (`…; both fire.` for a mode-only key or with `EnforceGameWins` off).
- **A conflict clears** the moment either side is remapped or unloaded.
- **When the picture is rebuilt.** The winner picture is rebuilt only on change events: a registration, rebind or removal; a config-file key written through this tab; a settings source added or removed (a plugin load or unload); the foreign-edit or game-wins switch; a world entered or left; and the Keybinds tab opening. The pump rebuilds it at the top of a frame when one of those changed. A key press only looks the bind up in the last build's table. The game's keys are read at world entry and when this tab opens, never on a timer. A change made in the game's own Controls page is therefore seen at the next world entry or tab open. A hand edit of another mod's cfg is seen at the next tab open. The built picture holds no `ConfigEntry`; a config-file key is found again when it is written.

**Problems.** Only the side that lost gets a `keybind-conflict` warning: the Mods list's diamond and second line (`Key F8 not active: used by Hunts`), the status bar's conflict chip and the problems line. The winner keeps its key and has no problem. A key on a reserved chord gets a `keybind-reserved` warning (not a conflict): `Key Alt+F4 reserved by Windows` on the Mods list. A Progenitor key's problem (its cfg value was replaced and written back, so it happens once) has **Dismiss** on its page line; an ordinary mod's clears only when its key is changed. The reserved table and its reasons are in [modules.md](modules.md#keybinds-fui5).

### Key conflict dialog

**Status: source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified** (KEYS4, `tests/Progenitor.Tests/KeybindConflictTests.cs`).

Requested 2026-09-30: when a mod loads wanting a key something else already has, ask, instead of only listing the conflict in this tab and on the Mods page. The winner rule above still decides at once, so the losing key is inactive from its first frame; the dialog asks what to do about it.

- **When.** A Progenitor mod registers its keys: at start, on a hot load or on a reload (`Keybinds.Register` notes the plugin). On the next frame, after every key of that registration is in, each Progenitor key that is **not active** and whose loss involves that mod becomes a clash: the mod lost a key to something, or the mod took a key from a mod installed later. There is **one dialog per losing mod**, listing each of its clashing keys. A key only a mode-only game action shares (a warning, not a loss) is not asked about. Configuration-file keys of ordinary mods never lose, so they are never asked about.
- **Never over something else.** The dialog waits while a key capture or a rebind waits, while any dialog is on screen or queued, while a Keybinds row is being edited, and while the player types in chat or a field. It also waits until a screen can take it: the Mods workspace open, the title screen with its Mods entry, or a world's HUD (a chat window can be read, which is not the case on a loading screen). Just before it is shown, every clash is checked again, and one that has gone (the player fixed it, the mod unloaded) is dropped. It is queued as a dialog, so a framework confirmation that needs the screen steps in front of it and it comes back afterwards, unanswered.
- **Waits for its art.** A key conflict is not urgent, so once it could show it also waits for the dialog's game-art copies (the idle preparation that logs `Settings kit: prepared ... (owned-copy=N)`), at most 3 s (`KeybindConflictPrompt.ArtWaitCapMs`); after that it shows with whatever art there is and logs that it did. Live finding 2026-10-02: right after a Core swap it drew the kit look a second before the art was ready.
- **Names.** Mods are named as the Keybinds tab's section titles name them: the Mods list's name, else the loaded plugin's BepInEx name, never the raw plugin id (live finding 2026-10-02: a mod without a menu registration showed as `progenitor.sample` after a Core swap). `KeybindConflictPolishTests`.
- **Framework defaults never start one.** A framework key's default must be free of every mod default in the repository (`FrameworkDefaultKeyTests` scans Core's `Default*` chords against every `Keybinds.Register` in `samples/`): photo mode's toggle is Ctrl+Shift+F9 because Ctrl+F9 is the Sample's log panel.
- **In game with no menu open** it is the framework's normal dialog over the HUD, with its backdrop (DIALOGS1 draws a dialog as its own panel anywhere a Progenitor panel can be shown). The small non-blocking notice was not needed. Like every dialog, it takes gameplay input while it is up.
- **Words** (KEYS10, after live feedback 2026-10-16: the old dialog did not say which mod caused it, who had the key first, whose action "Choose new keys" changes, or what "Leave unbound" leaves; then kept short). One pure function builds them, `KeybindConflictPrompt.ContentFor`. For the companion scenario (the Sample installed first; the version and dates are illustrative):

  ```
  Key conflict: Progenitor Sample Companion
  Progenitor Sample Companion 1.0.0 (installed Sep 28, 2026) wants a taken key.
  F8  Companion ping (gameplay) is off.
      Held by Progenitor Sample: Net ping (gameplay), installed first (Sep 20, 2026).
  Later: nothing is saved; asks again later.
  [Pick a new key for Companion ping]
  [Give F8 to Companion ping]      Unbinds Progenitor Sample's Net ping.
  [Leave Companion ping unbound]
                                                      [Later]
  ```

  | Part | Text |
  |---|---|
  | Body | `<mod> <version> (installed <date>) wants a taken key.` (`wants 2 taken keys.` for several; the version and date only when known). When the mod that just loaded holds the key: `<holder> <version> just loaded and had F8 first.` |
  | Facts, two per key | `<key>: <action> (<where>) is off.` then `Held by <holder>: <action> (<where>), <reason>.` Where: `gameplay`, `menus`, `menus and windows`, `map`, `photo mode`, `chat`, or a game key's own mode (`while building`). Reasons: `the game's key comes first`, `Progenitor's key comes first`, `installed first (<date>)`, `counts as first: <mod>'s install date is unknown`, `same install date; decided by mod id`. A game key whose words differ reads `Mouse Back (the game's Mouse Button 5)`. |
  | Options | **Pick a new key for \<action\>** (several: **Pick new keys for \<mod\>**); **Give \<key\> to \<action\>** (several keys: **Give F8 and G to \<mod\>**), the only option with a second line, `Unbinds <holder>'s <action>.`; **Leave \<action\> unbound** (several: **Leave \<mod\>'s N actions unbound**). With Give they are list rows; without it, as many buttons as fit. |
  | Note, and the dismissal | `Later: nothing is saved; asks again later.`; the button row's **Later** (also Escape). |
  | Footer | the framework's `Progenitor <version>` line (it is the framework's question) |

- **Choices.**
  - **Pick a new key / Pick new keys** opens the Mods workspace (in a world through the game's menu, as the Open Mods menu key does), selects this tab on the mod's section and starts the capture of its first clashing key. The capture sets the key of the dialog's mod (the one whose action is off), never the holder's. Nothing is written; the other clashing keys are not asked about again in this Core generation.
  - **Give \<key\> to …** is offered only when every holder of the chord is another mod's key this tab may write: a Progenitor key, or an ordinary mod's key setting while `EditOtherModsSettings` is on. Never the game and never Progenitor's own keys. Conflicts are never allowed, so each holder is **unbound** (its `[Keybinds]` cfg value becomes `None`), and the new mod's key becomes active. The unbound row says `Unbound: F8 was given to Progenitor Sample Companion (Companion ping).` in its help and the description column until it is bound again (this note lasts for the Core generation; the cfg value lasts). With several clashes, Give applies to the givable ones; the others are treated as Later.
  - **Leave … unbound** unbinds the dialog's mod's clashing keys (`None` in its cfg); the holders keep theirs. Its rows say `Left unbound: F8 is used by Progenitor Sample (Net ping).`
  - **Later**, and **Escape**, writes nothing. The keys stay inactive, as the winner rule left them. It asks again in the next Core generation: the next start, or a Progenitor update or reload. Escape is never a destructive choice.
- **Decisions are kept.** Give and Leave write through the same `[Keybinds]` cfg entry every remap writes (`Keybinds.TryRebind`, or `SettingsCatalog.WriteKey` for a key setting), so they last across restarts and, with the conflict gone, the dialog does not come back. Every key write saves its cfg file even when the file does not save on set (live 2026-09-30 a Give left the Sample's cfg unchanged: while connected, ConfigSync turns `SaveOnConfigSet` off on a plugin's file whose synced settings the server locks, and it parks those keys with the player's own values, so an explicit save changes only the key; `Keybinds.Persist`). **Later** (and Pick a new key) is remembered in memory by mod, action and chord for the Core generation: a hot reload of the mod does not ask again, the next start does while the conflict is unchanged. A Core swap starts a new generation and asks again.
- **Choose shape.** Enter does nothing (a Choose has no main action). Clicking a list option or `widget_click dialog-option-<i>/option-<i>` answers as the bridge op below does.

Progenitor's own keys are named **Progenitor** (and the dev bridge's **Progenitor Dev**) wherever a holder is named: the dialog, its lines, the row notes and help, the capture refusals and the log (live 2026-09-30 they read `progenitor`).

The code is `KeybindConflictPrompt` (game-free); it runs from the keybind pump and owns no Unity object besides the dialog's panel.

**The Conflicts view.** While any Progenitor key is not active, choosing **Conflicts N** shows one line above the sections: `N keys are inactive because of conflicts. Pick new keys to turn them back on.` (`1 key is inactive because of a conflict. Pick a new key to turn it back on.` for one). A config-file key that loses still fires, so it is not counted. A losing row's key cell opens the capture for that row in one click.

### Remapping

1. Click a mod key's primary cap. The next chord pressed is read with the key-capture rules (`Keybinds.BeginCapture`): chat or a text field with focus suspends it, Escape cancels the capture and nothing else (the Mods workspace, its panes and the search stay: the panel Escape sweep and the search clear stand down while any capture waits and for 400 ms after one ended on Escape, because the capture's pump and the panel hook see one press in either order; in a world, where the workspace is hosted in the game's FullscreenMenu, the menu-hotkey guard context at order 498, ahead of `FullscreenMenuMapper` at 502, also takes `Back` and `Menu_Close` while a capture or a dialog owns Escape and while that Escape is still held, so the game's menu does not close either; `mods_ui status.focus.closeHeld` reads the hold), holding modifiers never ends it, but one of Ctrl, Shift or Alt pressed and released with no other key or button in between records that modifier on its own (`Alt`, [HOLDMOD1](modules.md#lone-modifier-hold-keys-holdmod1)): a mod key that acts while held takes it, a press-only mod key refuses it with `Alt on its own only works for a key you hold down, and this key acts on a press. Press another key with it (for example X + Alt) or pick another key.` and keeps waiting, a game row takes it as the left key, and another mod's config-file key refuses it (`Alt on its own can't be a key for a mod's own setting: the mod may act on a press. Press another key with it or pick another key.`; its own default is still accepted); a modifier already down when the capture began counts only after it is released; two modifiers released together record nothing (a capture that delivers no key is refused with `Two modifiers together can't be a key; hold them with another key (for example F9 + Ctrl + Shift), or press and release one modifier on its own.` and keeps waiting; it is never an unbind, KEYS4), an AltGr keystroke is refused with its reason, and no bind fires while it waits. Nothing is stored yet.
   - **A key something else has is taken on Save** (requested 2026-10-10; source-checked and game-free tested in `KeybindTabTests`; live 2026-10-12 at the title for two Progenitor binds: Photo mode slow took R from Photo mode fast through the bridge, the preview, warn tone, Swap offer, Save, both rows' notes after the rebuild and the reset-arrow cancel all read as described. Live 2026-10-12 in a world on the isolated test server, through the bridge: Move Left on W named Move Up, the reset arrow mid-change wrote nothing, Save left Move Up unbound and Move Left on W with both notes; Drop Item back on Space only warned (Travel Skill and Respawn kept it); Interact on Space listed and took six holders, including three building-mode keys, and Deselect Current Placement kept Escape; every key was reset afterward. Hovering across rows with a real pointer is not live-tested). The capture stops and the key is pending, as for a free key, and the description column (warn tone) names what loses it before anything is written: `F8 is used by Progenitor Sample (Net ping). Save moves it to Open; Net ping will have no key.` A game action that keeps its other slot says so (`… Move Up will keep only Up Arrow.`). Every holder that meets the row's scope is taken: a game action on the game's Controls page (in a world, where game keys can be written), another Progenitor bind (the same mod's included) or a config-file key. **Save** unbinds each holder through its own path, then stores the key; if any step fails, the holders already unbound get their keys back and nothing changes (`Couldn't take F8 from … Nothing was changed.`). Afterwards the row's description says `Took F8 from Progenitor Sample (Net ping), which has no key now.` and each holder's row says `F8 moved to Open; this action has no key now.` (these notes survive the rebuild that follows a save). **Cancel** or Escape changes nothing. A remap therefore still never leaves a conflict. The log line is `Ui: keybinds took <chord> from <claim key>`.
   - **A key that cannot be used is refused, and the capture keeps waiting.** The description column shows the reason, then `Press another key, or Escape to cancel.`; the edit bar still reads `press a key`. This covers a chord Windows or Steam reserves (`Alt+F4 closes the window in Windows. Pick another key.`), a key pressed with a Windows key held (`Windows-key shortcuts belong to Windows. Pick another key.`), and a key held by something this tab can't change: a read-only mod key, or a game key at the title screen. Outside a world the game's keys are compared with the last in-world read, and the refusal says how old it is; with no earlier read, the preview adds `Game keys can't be checked outside a world; this key may clash with a game action.` A check that cannot run refuses the key (`Couldn't check this key; try again.`). Escape cancels whatever modifiers are held with it. A capture ends after 30 s in total however many keys were refused.
   - **Swap.** When the key belongs to another Progenitor bind, and that bind could take this row's key without a new clash, the edit bar also shows **Swap** beside **Save** (Save takes the key and leaves the other bind with none; Swap gives it this row's key). It asks once in the framework dialog (`Swap keys?`, `<mod> (<key>) gets F8 and <mod> (<key>) gets F9.`, **Yes, swap** / **No**). Yes stores both keys and writes both `[Keybinds]` entries; if a write throws, both are put back. Config-file keys and game keys are never offered a swap.
   - **Mouse buttons** (KEYS4): the middle, back and forward buttons can be captured as the key. They are shown **Middle Mouse**, **Mouse Back** and **Mouse Forward** on the caps, the edit bar and in every sentence (KEYS5; the cfg keeps `MouseMiddle` / `MouseBack` / `MouseForward`, and a cfg also reads the shown names and the game's words `Mouse Button 4`, `Mouse Button 5`, `Scroll Wheel`). The game names the same buttons differently, so a sentence about a game key the game writes differently adds the game's words: `Mouse Forward (the game's Mouse Button 4) is used by the game (Auto Run). Pick another key.` (`KeybindArbiter.ChordPhrase`; the refusals, the not-active line, the conflict dialog and its notes). The left and right buttons can since KEYS8, with Ctrl, Shift or Alt held (shown **Left Mouse** / **Right Mouse**, `MouseLeft` / `MouseRight` in the cfg): the click that opened the capture is never taken, a bare click is never taken and still reaches the panel (so **Cancel**, **Unbind** and the scroll bar work with the mouse; on Progenitor's UI it is silent, elsewhere it is refused with `A plain left or right click attacks, moves and clicks menus, …`), and the release of a taken click does not press the button under the pointer. A menu key refuses them. Rules: [modules.md](modules.md#keybinds-fui5). Source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified. While a capture waits, the back and forward buttons are the new key, never navigation.
   - Pressing the key the row already has is accepted as a no-op (`F9 is already this key.`), even when that key is in a conflict. The capture also runs at the title screen, where no chat window exists, whenever a Progenitor panel holds the input. A capture (or a Keys-row rebind) that gets no key within 10 s is canceled with `No key was pressed within 10 seconds, so the key was left as it was.`, and every bind works again.
2. The edit bar previews the result: `F7 is free. Save to use it.`
3. **Save** stores it. A Progenitor bind goes through `Keybinds.Rebind`, which writes the same `[Keybinds]` entry the mod page's Keys section writes, so the two always agree. `Rebind` applies the same refusals as the capture, so a key that became taken in the meantime is still refused. A refusal (a printable key on the Mods-menu opener, a reserved or used key) is shown on the edit bar. **Unbind** stores `None`. **Cancel** changes nothing.
4. **Reset** (the reset icon) is enabled on a changed row and restores its default. While the row is being changed it is enabled too (user report 2026-10-10: it stayed dimmed until Save): it drops the change first, then restores the default if the stored key differs from it; on a key already at its default it only drops the change, and hovering it says `Cancel this change and keep the default, A.` When that default is now taken or reserved, the reset is refused: `Its default, F9, is now used by Hunts (Open). Unbind it or pick another key.` **Unbind** unbinds it.
5. **Reset all mod keys** asks first in the framework dialog (`Reset all mod keys?`, **Yes, reset** / **No**; a destructive dialog, so Enter does not answer it and **Yes, reset** is in the danger tone) and resets every changed key that this tab can write. A key whose default is taken or reserved is skipped and logged. It never changes the game's keys.

Escape on any framework dialog (Reset all, Swap, Jump to mod) answers the dialog with No and closes only the dialog; the workspace behind it stays. The next Escape, with no dialog open, closes the workspace as before. This is the kit rule for every dialog (`Ui.OnBootstrapKey`), not only this tab's. In a world the same guard hold keeps the game's FullscreenMenu open.

The mod page's Keys rows and the settings panel's rebind use the same checks. Their capture keeps `press a key…` after a refusal and puts the reason on the row (`RebindResult.StillCapturing`). A typed chord and a reset are refused with the same sentences.

**Config-file keys** (`ConfigEntry<KeyboardShortcut>` / `ConfigEntry<KeyCode>` from the config files the settings catalog already walks) are written through `SettingsCatalog.WriteKey`, which applies the catalog's rules:

- a server-synced entry is never written;
- another mod's entry is written only while `[Settings] EditOtherModsSettings` is on in `progenitor.ui.cfg` (otherwise the row is read-only and says so);
- a `KeyCode` entry takes one key without modifiers;
- the value is read with the same separators as BepInEx's `KeyboardShortcut` (space, `+`, `,`, `;`, `|`), and a written value keeps the side of a modifier the entry already names (`RightControl` stays `RightControl`); new modifiers are written as `Left*`;
- the switch is asked again at write time, not taken from the row;
- a chord Windows or Steam reserves is never written;
- a chord something else already has is refused before anything is written, as for a Progenitor bind;
- the value must read back.

After a save the row says `Saved to its config file. It applies when the mod reads the setting again; many mods read it only at start, so restart the game if the new key does not work.` Progenitor cannot tell which case applies. `KeyCode` `Mouse0` and `Mouse1` read as the left and right buttons and are compared like any chord (KEYS8; no Progenitor bind could use them before, so no existing winner changes). `Mouse2` and up (the middle, back and forward buttons included) and joystick codes are shown as written and never compared, as before, so a Progenitor bind on Mouse Back keeps its install-order result.

### Links with the Mods tab

Both directions go through `ModsMenu.JumpWorkspace`. It selects the tab with the ordinary `SelectWorkspaceTab` path, so tab memory and Back behave as a strip click does, then scrolls on the next frame with `UiReveal`. The heading goes to the top of the viewport unless everything from it down already fits. A jump clears a search or filter that would hide its target.

- **Mods page → Keybinds:** the Keys section heading on a mod's page (the automatic Keys section, else the first builder section with a keybind row) has **Open in Keybinds**. It opens this tab at that mod's section.
- **Keybinds → Mods page:** a mod section's heading and its **Open mod page** button select the mod in the Mods tab and scroll its page to the Keys section.

### Readback and bridge

`mods_ui widget_read pane=content` on this tab:

| Key | Row | Value |
|---|---|---|
| `keys-toolbar` *(live)* | toolbar | `{query, filter, filters {all, conflicts, changed}, shown, total, resettable, gameAvailable, jumpTargets [{target, label}] (mods first), lastJump, currentJump: mods\|game, groups {filter, jumps, picker}}` |
| `keys-group` *(live)* | a group header | `{id: mods\|game, title, divider: ornament\|kit, visible}` |
| `keys-footer` *(live)* | the bottom row | `{resettable, caption}` |
| `keys-description` *(live, `pane=list`)* | the description column's heading and ornament | `{row, source: hover\|selected\|none, heading, body, warning, warningTone: warn\|muted\|empty, info, hovered, selected, ornament {source, donor, sprite}}` |
| `keys-edit` *(live)* | edit bar | `{editing, capturing, pending, status, lastStatus, swapWith {owner, action, chord, gets} \| null, visible}` |
| `keys-conflicts-header` *(live)* | the Conflicts view's line | `{text, inactive, visible}`; `text` is empty unless the Conflicts filter is on and a Progenitor key is not active |
| `keys-section` *(live)* | a section heading | `{id: game:<category>\|mod:<id>\|group:<id>, title, owner, source: game\|progenitor\|cfg\|mixed, count, countText, shown, hasModPage, available, category, titleSource: game\|catalog\|empty, group, infoRows, unavailable, unavailableShown}`; the id is `game` for the unavailable note; `group` is empty and `unavailable` null outside a [keybind group](#keybind-groups) |
| `keys-row` *(live)* | a key's name row | `{id, section, owner, action, name, chord, keyText, default, source: game\|progenitor\|cfg, active, changed, editable, notEditableReason, comparable, tone: warn\|readonly\|normal, help, reserved: windows\|steam\|empty, visible, editing, nameRow, helpRow, conflict {with [{owner, name, action, source}], winner, winnerAction, rule: game\|install\|load-order, reason, enforced}, primary, primaryCaption, primaryTone, secondaryCap, secondaryCaption, secondaryEnabled, marker, description, current, hovered, selected}`; a game row adds `category`, `categoryTitle`, `secondary`; a row in a [keybind group](#keybind-groups) adds `group`, and a group's read-only row also adds `info: true`, `note` and reports `source: info` |
| `keys-game-note` | a game category heading (or the unavailable note) | `{available, note, fromCache, shown, opener: words}`; `shown` is true on the heading that draws the note |
| `page-keys-jump` | the mod page's Keys heading | `{target, plugin, found, measured, landed, rowTop, viewportTop, viewportHeight}` after an Open mod page jump |

`mods_ui keys {op, target?, chord?}` drives the tab:

| `op` | What it does |
|---|---|
| `read` | Returns the whole tab, with `description` (the column), `gameFromCache`, `conflictPrompt` (as `op=conflict`), and `art` (`keyCap`, `highlight`, `highlightTop`, `ornament`, `rule` as `{source, donor, sprite}`, `entryImages`, `headerImages`, `categories: game\|catalog`, `categoriesFrom`). |
| `jump` | `target`: `game`, `mods`, `mod:<id>`, `group:<id>` or a plugin id. |
| `filter` | `all` / `conflicts` / `changed`. |
| `search` | Filters by the given text. |
| `edit` | Starts changing the row with the given row `id`. |
| `capture` | As if `chord` were pressed while that row waits. `target` is the row `id`; without one, the row being edited (`op=edit`) is used. With neither, or a row that is not editable, the op fails with an error naming `args.target` (live 2026-09-29: it used to return `captured: false` silently). `chord` is required and must name a key: a missing, empty or `None` chord, or modifiers alone (`Shift`, `Ctrl + Alt`; live 2026-09-30 `Shift` offered `Unbind this key`), is an error that points to `op=unbind` (KEYS4, live 2026-09-30: a capture without a chord set the pending key to None and the following save unbound the key). |
| `conflict` | The key-conflict dialog: `{open, queued, dialog {plugin, mod, title, body, lines, options [{index, choice, caption}], cancel, clashes [{row, action, name, chord, sentence, givable, holders [{owner, name, action, actionName, source}]}]} \| null, waiting [plugin ids], noted, later [owner/action/chord], editRequest {plugin, row} \| null, last {plugin, choice, results [{owner, action, unbound, reason}]}, notes {rowId: note}}`. Works with the workspace closed. |
| `conflict-choose` | Answers the open key-conflict dialog as its button does. `target`: `edit`, `give`, `leave` or `later` (`later` is the dismissal, as Escape). An error when no conflict dialog is open or the choice is not offered (Give when the game holds the key). Works with the workspace closed. |
| `save` / `unbind` / `cancel` | The edit bar's buttons. |
| `reset` / `clear` | Takes a row `id`. |
| `swap` | The edit bar's **Swap**: opens the dialog (only after a refused key another Progenitor bind has). |
| `hover` | As the pointer entering the row with the given row `id`, through the row's own pointer slot; an empty `target` leaves it. Returns `description`. |
| `select` | Selects the row with the given row `id` (a row click). Returns `description`. |
| `press-key` / `press-key2` | Clicks the row's primary / secondary cap: a game cap shows its read-only note, a mod's primary cap starts the capture, a Progenitor bind's secondary cap starts the controller capture (PAD1), an ordinary mod's key setting has none. |
| `reset-all` | Opens the dialog. |
| `open-page` / `open-keybinds` | Takes a plugin id: the two links. |

The bridge's `keybind_press` ([bridge-ops-client.md](bridge-ops-client.md#keybind_press)) presses a registered bind through the same winner rule.

`keys-row` also appears as the mod page's keybind rows' `active` and `conflict` ([mod-settings.md](mod-settings.md#readback-mods_ui-widget_read)).

### Changing the game's keys (KEYS9)

**Status: verified live on the Steam client (2026-10-10, Core `65f9d713…` gen 36 from branch `fix/game-key-rebind`, in a world on the isolated test server); game-free tested (`tests/Progenitor.Tests/GameKeyRemapTests.cs`).** Before KEYS9 the game rows were read-only, so every attempt to change a game key here did nothing beyond the note (user report 2026-10-10). What was observed is listed under [Live results](#keys9-live-results-2026-10-10).

- **Which keys.** Every action on the game's own Bindings Menu (Movement, Skills & Attacks, General, Camera, Panels, Action Bar, Building), both slots. The **Other game keys**, the **Debug keys** and the mouse axes stay read-only. Changes are made in a world only, because the game's input actions exist only there.
- **What a game slot takes.** One keyboard key, or the middle, back or forward mouse button. A chord with Ctrl, Shift or Alt is refused, and the capture keeps waiting (`Shift+X is a combination. The game's keys take one key or mouse button, without Ctrl, Shift or Alt.`), because the game's own rebinding takes one button and none of its keyboard-and-mouse bindings combine a modifier with a key. A bare left or right click is refused as for every key. A modifier key on its own (Jump from Cliffs ships on Left Shift) cannot be captured; **Reset** puts such a default back.
- **Clashes** (changed 2026-10-10 on request; source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified). A key another action already has is taken on Save, as for mod keys ([Remapping](#remapping)): `W is used by the game (Move Up). Save moves it to Move Left; Move Up will have no key.` This covers another game action on the game's Controls page (on either slot; one key on both directions of a movement axis can no longer happen, because the other direction loses it), a Progenitor bind and a mod's key setting. A key Windows or Steam reserves and a bare left or right click are still refused. Two game overlaps only warn and take nothing: a key held by a game action this tab can't change (`W is also used by the game (Move Camera Y +, in camera modes). The game allows this, as its own Controls page does. Save to use it for Move Up anyway.`), and a key going back to its own default where another unchanged game action shares that default (the game ships Space as Travel Skill, Drop Item and Respawn, so Drop Item back on Space leaves Travel Skill alone). Any other action put on Space takes it from all of them. Pressing the key the slot already has is a no-op. Swap is not offered for game keys.
