- **Save** writes the slot into the game's own controls through the game's route: the binding gets an override path, the action is disabled and enabled around the change, and the game saves its input settings to its own settings file, refreshes its binding version and clears its key-text cache. The game's key text (what its Controls page and key hints draw) reads the new key. The key is written to the game's own input settings file (observed live); that the game loads it again after a restart was not checked. If the save itself fails after the key is applied, the row says `Applied for this session, but the game could not save its controls (…)`, never that the key is unchanged. Move Up / Left / Down / Right are the halves of the game's `MoveY` / `MoveX` axes; the write targets that half's own binding, so the other direction keeps its key. Choosing the game's own default again removes the override instead of storing one equal to it, so the game's settings file goes back to no override. The tab then reads the game's keys again, so the row, the **Changed** filter and every mod key's verdict use the new key.
- **Unbind** in the edit bar clears the slot being changed; the gutter's **Unbind** clears the primary key, as the game's own Clear does. **Reset** (the gutter) puts both slots back to the game's default and reads each back against the game's default; an action with no second keyboard binding has only its first slot reset, and its second cap is dimmed and says `The game has no second keyboard key for this action.` A changed game row says `The game's default is W.` in the description column. **Reset all mod keys** does not touch the game's keys.
- **Readback** (`keys op=read`): a game row's `editable`, `default` / `secondaryDefault` (the game's default key text), `primaryChanged` / `secondaryChanged`, `secondaryExists`, and `notEditableReason` (empty when it can be changed now). The edit readback adds `slot` (0 primary, 1 secondary). `keys op=edit` on `game:game/<Action>#2` edits the second slot; `press-key` / `press-key2` press the caps.
- **Log.** Each write logs `Ui: game key <action or axis±>#<slot> -> <path|unbound|default> index=<binding> before=<path> after=<path> saved` (or `MISMATCH` when the read-back path differs), then the tab's `Ui: keybinds game <Action>#<slot> -> <key>: saved (Saved in the game's controls.)`.
- **Controllers.** The tab lists and changes only the **keyboard-and-mouse** slots, as the game's keyboard Bindings Menu does: the reader and the writer always ask the game for `ControllerType.KeyboardAndMouse` (asking for a gamepad type with no pad connected would make the game add a fake XInput device). The game's gamepad bindings are neither shown nor changeable here; change them in the game's own Controls page. A keyboard write overrides only its own binding index, and the action's gamepad bindings were read before and after every live write and did not change. The Mods workspace, the Keybinds tab and its capture have no gamepad navigation (Progenitor's UI is mouse and keyboard only; gamepad is out of scope, see [Verified and open](#verified-and-open)); with a pad, use the mouse to open a key and press the new key on the keyboard.

#### KEYS9 live results (2026-10-10)

Steam client, Core `65f9d713…` (generation 36, verified with `ping`), joined to the isolated test server on 9976, no other player. Bridge ops only: `mods_ui keys`, the Dev bridge `key` op with `background: true` (no focus, no OS input), and throwaway readback snippets on the client. Evidence is in the internal research notes.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Baseline | `keys op=read`: 118 game rows, 84 editable (the Bindings Menu's), the rest read-only with their reason; no gamepad device (`Gamepad.current` null, controller type KeyboardAndMouse); the game had no binding overrides. |
| Plain key on movement | Move Up W → I: `Ui: game key MoveY+#1 -> <Keyboard>/i index=2 before=<Keyboard>/w after=<Keyboard>/i saved`. Only binding 2 (the `positive` part of `MoveY`) changed; S, A, D and the gamepad `leftStick/y` kept theirs. The game's input settings file held the override. |
| The game uses it | With the workspace closed, holding I (background `key` op, 1.2 s) moved the character 2.6 units forward on the server; holding W moved it 0; holding S moved it 4.8 back. After moving Move Up back to W: W moved 4.8, I moved 0. |
| Action key, second slot | Interact F → G and second slot H: the game's own key text (`GetBindingDisplayInfo`, which the HUD hints use) read G and H. |
| Modifier chords | `X + Shift` and `F7 + Ctrl` refused with the combination sentence, capture kept waiting, nothing written. |
| Mouse buttons | Weapon Skill 1's second slot took Mouse Back (`<Mouse>/backButton`) after Interrupt's second slot was unbound; a bare left click was refused. |
| Clashes | A mod's key (F10) refused, naming it. Another game action's key warns and saves (after the fix above; the first run refused S, W, Mouse Forward and the middle button). |
| Title screen | Superseded 2026-10-12: game rows are read live at the title and a cap click starts the capture. Live 2026-10-12, Steam client at the title through the bridge: `gameFromCache false`, Move Left's cap click started the capture, J saved (`Saved in the game's controls.`, taking it from Spellbook Menu), and both reset back. Before, the rows showed the last in-world read and a cap click only said why it could not change them. |
| Gamepad bindings | Read for Move X/Y, Interact, Weapon Skill 1, Interrupt and Auto Run before, during and after: unchanged. |
| Restore | **Reset** on every changed row, then the override for Move Up removed: all 118 rows equal the baseline, the game reports no overrides, and every action read was enabled. The workspace was closed and the client left at the title screen. |

Not observed live: the HUD's on-screen key hint (only its text source was read), the game's own Controls page showing the new key, persistence across a game restart (the settings file was written; the game was not restarted), and any gamepad (none was connected; no virtual device was added).

### Hot reload

- The pane, its capture callback and the Reset-all dialog belong to the workspace (Core). They are released when the workspace closes or the tab is rebuilt.
- `KeybindArbiter`'s providers (the game reader and the install-time lookup) and `GameKeyRemap.Writer` (KEYS9) are set by `UiKeybindHost.Install` and cleared by its `Uninstall`. A game key written before a Core swap is the game's own setting and stays.
- Nothing is handed to Bootstrap or IL2CPP.
- The game reader has no timer: its snapshot is kept until the next world entry or tab open.

### Acceptance procedure (not run)

Client only, and the isolated test server only (`<testserver>`). Requires:

- a Core with this change (a Core swap; Bootstrap is unchanged);
- the Sample and `Progenitor.SampleCompanion` built from the same tree;
- for step 6, a plain BepInEx plugin with a `KeyboardShortcut` entry.

1. At the title screen, open the Mods workspace. `mods_ui status`: the strip reads Mods / Install Mods / Keybinds / Preferences / Server. `mods_ui select_tab tab=keybinds`, then `mods_ui keys op=read`. Expected: no `game:*` section and `gameNote` equal to the in-world note; one section per mod with keys.
2. Join the test server, open Mods, select Keybinds. Expected: one `game:<category>` section per Bindings Menu category in the page's order, listing Interact, the spells, ActionBar1–8 and the rest, each with the primary and secondary keys and the descriptions shown in the game's Bindings Menu (compare a capture of Esc > Options > Controls > Keyboard & Mouse Bindings). The log has no `game key binding(s) could not be read` line.
3. Try to rebind the Sample's F9 bind to a key the game uses (for example `keys op=edit` on its row, then `op=capture chord="Q"`). Expected (in a world, 2026-10-10 rule): `edit.pending: "Q"`, `edit.takeFrom` naming the game action and `edit.status` `Q is used by the game (…). Save moves it to …`; `op=cancel`, and the bind is still F9. At the title screen: `edit.pending: null`, `edit.capturing: true` and `edit.status` `Q is used by the game (…), as last read in a world …. Pick another key. …`. Then `op=capture chord="F4 + Alt"`. Expected: `Alt+F4 closes the window in Windows. …`, still capturing. `op=cancel`. To see an enforced loser, give the Sample a game key in its cfg by hand while the game is closed (`[Keybinds]` value `Q`), then join. Expected: `keys-row.active: false`, `conflict.winner: game`, the Mods list line `Key Q not active: used by the game (…)`, the Conflicts view's line `1 key is inactive because of a conflict. …`, and `keybind_press plugin=progenitor.sample bind=<id>` returns `fired: false` with `not-active: …`. Press the reset (`«`). Expected: the conflict clears everywhere.
4. With the Sample and the companion both loaded on F8: the one installed later reads `active: false`, and the rule is `install` (or `load-order` when neither has a ledger row, with the sentence saying so). Pressing F8 in game (a foreground test, only with the user's approval) runs only the winner's handler. Rebind either side: both rows read `active: true` without a rebuild of the Mods page.
5. On the Sample's page, press **Open in Keybinds**. Expected: `status.tab: keybinds` and `keys-toolbar.lastJump.section: mod:progenitor.sample` with `landed: true`. Press the section's **Open mod page**. Expected: the Mods tab with the Sample selected and `page-keys-jump.landed: true`. Capture both.
6. With the plain plugin loaded and `EditOtherModsSettings` off, its section is labeled `From its config file.` and its row is read-only with the reason. Turn the opt-in on, reopen, remap it. Expected: its `.cfg` holds the new `KeyboardShortcut` value and the row says it applies when the mod reads it again. Try to put it on a Progenitor bind's chord. Expected: `… is used by <mod> (<key>). Save moves it to …`; Save writes the cfg key and leaves that bind unbound, and both rows say where the key went. Put a reserved chord into its cfg by hand (`F4 + LeftAlt`) and reopen. Expected: its row says `Reserved by Windows; this key may not reach the mod.` and its Mods list line reads `Key Alt+F4 reserved by Windows`.
7. **Reset all mod keys** → **No**: nothing changes. **Yes, reset**: every changed mod key reads `changed: false`.
8. **Key conflict dialog** (KEYS4): follow [the companion's README](../samples/Progenitor.SampleCompanion/README.md#key-conflict-dialog). Expected: one dialog titled `Key conflict: <mod that lost F8>`, `mods_ui status.dialog.kind: key-conflict`, and each choice doing what the [section above](#key-conflict-dialog) says; the dialog never appears while a capture waits.
9. **Back and forward** (KEYS4): open the workspace, open a mod, **Open in Keybinds**, **Open mod page**, select Configuration. `mods_ui nav_state`: five steps. `mods_ui nav_back` three times: the Mods page's Keys section, the Keybinds tab on that mod, the mod page (`status.tab` and `nav_state.index` each time). Then with the user's approval for a foreground test, press the mouse's back and forward buttons: the same moves, and Auto Run does not start. `nav_back` at index 0: nothing happens and the workspace stays open. Close the workspace: `nav_state` still lists the steps; reopen and press Back: the view before the close comes back (KEYS6). Join or leave a world: `nav_state.count` is 0 with `clearedReason: world-change`.
10. **Game keys** (KEYS9), in a world on the test server. Note each slot's starting key from `keys op=read` first, and put every one back at the end. Run on 2026-10-10; results in [KEYS9 live results](#keys9-live-results-2026-10-10).
    1. A plain key on a movement direction: `keys op=press-key target=game:game/MoveUp`, then `op=capture chord="I"` and `op=save`. Expected: the `Ui: game key MoveY+#1 -> <Keyboard>/i … saved` log line; `keys op=read` shows Move Up `I`, `primaryChanged: true`; Move Down still `S`. With the workspace closed, holding I through the bridge `key` op (`background: true`) moves the character forward on the server and W no longer does.
    2. An action key: Interact (`F`) to `G` the same way. Expected as above, and the game's key text for Interact reads G.
    3. A modifier chord: `op=capture chord="X + Shift"` on Weapon Skill 1. Expected: `edit.pending: null`, `edit.capturing: true`, `edit.status` starting `X + Shift is a combination.`; no `Ui: game key` line.
    4. Mouse buttons: unbind Interrupt's second slot (`op=edit target=game:game/Interrupt#2`, `op=unbind`), then `op=edit target=game:game/WeaponSkill1#2`, `op=capture chord="MouseBack"`, `op=save`. Expected: the slot reads Mouse Button 5. `chord="MouseLeft"` is refused.
    5. Clashes (2026-10-10 rule): Move Left to `W`: the preview says Move Up will have no key; Save leaves Move Up empty and both rows' descriptions say where W went. Reset both. Move Up to `W` after a change: a warning naming Move Camera Y + (in camera modes), nothing taken. Move Up to `F10`: Save takes it from Progenitor's Open Mods menu (put it back afterwards).
    6. The second slot: `op=edit target=game:game/Interact#2`, `op=capture chord="H"`, `op=save`. Expected: the secondary cap reads H, the primary still G.
    7. Restore: **Reset** (`keys op=reset target=<row>`) on every row changed above. Expected: each reads `changed: false` and its starting key, and the game reports no binding overrides.
11. Capture the tab at 1600×1024 and 3840×1080 (a focus-free capture). Check that:
   - the toolbar fits on one row;
   - key cells line up in one column;
   - the warn tone is visible on conflict rows;
   - long help wraps under the name only.

Leave the client at the title screen afterwards.

**Known limits.** Everything above is unverified live, including:

- the reader's interop calls and the shape of the control paths it builds;
- the row sizes (two-line rows at 53 px, the Install list's);
- whether the pinned toolbar, selector and edit bar fit at 1600×1024.

The winner rule compares only exact chords, and only the actions on the game's Controls page. A cfg key's new value applies only when its mod reads it again. The mouse and gamepad are out of scope.

## Back and forward

**Status: source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified** (KEYS4, `tests/Progenitor.Tests/KeybindNavigationTests.cs`).

Requested 2026-09-30: back and forward in the menus like a web browser, "just have it applicable when the menus are open". A later decision the same day (KEYS6) keeps the history across a close.

- **Keys.** Two Progenitor keys, **Navigate back** (default the mouse's back button, shown **Mouse Back**, `MouseBack` in the cfg, the game's "Mouse Button 5") and **Navigate forward** (the forward button, shown **Mouse Forward**, the game's "Mouse Button 4"), in `progenitor.ui.cfg` `[Keybinds]` as `NavigateBack` / `NavigateForward`. They are ordinary `ctx.Keybinds`-path binds (no direct polling), listed under Progenitor in the Keybinds tab and remappable there. They are **window keys** ([scopes](#who-gets-a-key-first-installed), HUB2): in menus they walk this history, on the title screen and in game alike; outside menus they only step a window's tabs while that window holds the input. They never clash with gameplay keys, so the forward button's Auto Run and the back button's Interrupt Ability Cast are not conflicts.
- **Auto Run does not fire while the workspace is open.** Source-checked: the workspace's panes use `UiCaptureMode.WhileShown`, which applies the full input mask (`UiMaskScope.Full`, chat's `BlockAllKeys` recipe through `GameInputBlocker`), so the game's button actions, Auto Run on the forward button and Interrupt on the back button included, are blocked while it is shown. No extra block was added.
- **History.** Every place the player arrives at is one entry: a tab (Mods, Install Mods, Keybinds, Preferences, Server), and a group jumped to through a left list ([three homes](#drawers-and-the-left-list)); on Mods the overview, a mod, its settings group, all-settings or custom page; on Install Mods the listing whose details are open; on Configuration the section (Mods menu / Sources / Local server; settings organization stage 3 removed the raw **Progenitor settings** page that used to open inside it); on Keybinds the section a jump landed on (a link from a mod page, the ↓ Mod keys / ↓ Game keys jumps, **Jump to mod**, and the default Mod keys); and the mod page's Keys section after a link from the Keybinds tab; and Progenitor's page at its **Server settings** heading after the Configuration tab's link (settings organization; `nav_state` `place: progenitor-page@server-settings`). A rebuild after a save is not a new place. The same place twice in a row is one entry; at most 50 are kept.
- **One observer** (KEYS5; live 2026-09-30 Configuration's **Progenitor settings** page was not a step, so Back skipped to the tab before). A step is taken in one place: once a frame, from the keybind pump, the settled view is read as a whole (tab, plugin, page, page id, section) and recorded when it differs from the current entry. Every entry point (a strip click, `select_tab`, a list row, Overview / refresh, a page button, Back to mod, the Keybinds links and jumps, the Configuration nav and buttons, an Install listing, a key-conflict dialog's **Pick a new key**, a bridge op) therefore records exactly one step without code of its own, and a half-built view is never recorded. After a back or forward the entry becomes the view actually reached. An Install list that selects its first listing by itself is the same place.
- **Like a browser.** Back and forward move through the entries and bring the place back through the ordinary paths (the tab's remembered search and scroll come back with the tab; a Keys-section, Server-settings or Keybinds-section step scrolls to that section again). A new place after going back drops the forward entries. Back at the first entry does nothing; it never closes the workspace. Forward at the last does nothing.
- **Ignored** while a dialog is open and while a key capture waits (the capture takes the button as the new key instead).
- **Kept across a close** (KEYS6, decided 2026-09-30, replacing KEYS4's clear on close). Closing the workspace keeps the history. Reopening records the opened view as a new place by the usual rules (it drops the forward entries, and adds nothing when it is the place the history is at), so Back right after reopening returns to where the workspace was when it closed. The keys walk the history only while a menu is open.
- **Cleared** when the world changes: a join, a leave to the title, or a server change (the client world's identity, `ClientWorldEpoch.ObservedSequence`, moved; checked every frame, open or closed), and on a world or server lifecycle close (`CloseForLifecycle`). A new Core generation (a Core reload, and so every game start) starts empty: the history lives in Core's memory and is never written to disk.
- **Places that no longer exist are skipped.** Back and forward pass over, and drop from the history, a mod page whose mod is no longer listed or was unloaded or turned off, a custom page or settings group the mod no longer registers, a settings page whose plugin is gone, a Keybinds mod section with no keys left, and a Configuration section that is not one of the tab's sections; also an entry that has become the same as the place it lands next to. When no valid place remains in that direction nothing happens. The log says `history dropped N place(s) that no longer exist`. At most 50 places are kept.

Bridge: `mods_ui nav_back` / `nav_forward` press the navigation keybind through `Keybinds.SimulatePress`, the gates and winner rule a real button press meets, and return `{dispatch [{plugin, bind, chord, fired, reason}], nav}` (with no menu open and no window holding the input the dispatch reads `suppressed: works only in menus, or while a window with tabs holds the input`). `mods_ui nav_state` returns `{index, count, canBack, canForward, steps [{tab, plugin, page, pageId, section}], last, open, current, clearedReason: core-reload|world-change, clearedCount, world}`, readable with the workspace closed (it shows the kept history); `last` says what the last back or forward did (`back: nothing before this place`, `back: ignored (a dialog is open)`). Neither needs an open workspace. The code is `ModsMenu.Navigation.cs` and the game-free `WorkspaceNavHistory`.


### The game's menu in the same history (KEYS7)

**Status: source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified** (`tests/Progenitor.Tests/MenuHistoryTests.cs`).

Requested 2026-09-30: "switch between Vampire Powers tabs, go to Mods, press Back, go to Social", with back and forward walking all of it like a browser. The game's full-screen menu (the Esc / K / J / M / P menu: Emotes, Vampire Powers, Spellbook, Map, V Blood, Social, System) and the Mods workspace share **one** history.

- **A place** is the workspace's view while the workspace is open (in a world that is the MODS top tab, with its tab, mod, page and section), else the game menu's top tab (`FullscreenMenuState` name) and, when inner tabs are on (below), its inner tab. With neither open there is no place (plain gameplay records nothing).
- **Observed, not hooked.** The same once-a-frame observer reads the game menu from what `NativeModsEntry`'s tick already read that frame: the HUD's current `FullscreenMenu` and its `CurrentView.State`. No game method is patched or hooked. Opening the menu with the game's own hotkeys lands on a tab, which is a new place like any other.
- **Restoring** a game tab clicks it the way a player does: the tab entry's `FullscreenMenu_OuterTabEntry.OnPointerClick` and its `EventTrigger`, the path `mods_ui native_tab` drives, so the highlight and sound are the game's. Leaving the in-game Mods workspace that way lets the native route close it, as a real click does. A game step while the game's menu is not up (the workspace in its own window) asks the game to open the menu (`HUDMenuManager.GoToMenu(FullscreenMenu)`, the Mods shortcut's call) and selects the tab when it is up. A workspace step while a game tab is shown opens the workspace the way the Open Mods menu key does (in a world through the MODS tab) and restores the view once it is up. **A back or forward arrives over several frames** (live 2026-09-30: leaving the in-game Mods view for Spellbook appended Spellbook, because the workspace closing and the tab changing on later frames looked like the player's own navigation). It is held as one restore in progress: it keeps its target and the place it left, acts as soon as it can (the click, or opening the menu or the workspace once), clicks a game tab once more if it has not landed after 10 frames, and settles only when the observer sees the target; the history's entry then becomes the place reached and nothing is added. While it is in progress the observer records nothing and a further back or forward is ignored. A place that is neither its start nor its target, seen after 30 frames, is the player going elsewhere: the restore is dropped and that place is recorded, dropping the forward entries. After 300 frames it is dropped and the history stays where it was. A workspace close does not cancel it. A Core generation in a world reads the world as unknown until the epoch watch notes it; game tabs recorded then are kept when it does (live: the first tabs after a Core swap were lost).
- **Rules** are the workspace's: 50 places, kept across a close, cleared on a world change and a new Core generation, no duplicate in a row, a new place after going back drops the forward entries, and places that no longer exist are skipped and dropped. A game tab the menu no longer shows (its tab entry is not active) is such a place.
- **Keys.** Navigate back and Navigate forward walk this history while the Mods workspace or the game's full-screen menu is open on any tab (`ModsMenu.MenuContextOpen`). Over the workspace they first step the tabs of a window with a tab history under the pointer (it takes the press only when it can move). They never clash with gameplay keys.
- **Keyboard keys and window tabs** (HUB1, merged into one action per direction by HUB2; source-checked and game-free tested, not live-verified). **Navigate back (keys)** (Alt+Left) and **Navigate forward (keys)** (Alt+Right), `NavigateBackKeys` / `NavigateForwardKeys`, do what Navigate back and forward do. A bind holds one chord, so they are separate rows. All four step a window's tab history ([ui.md](ui.md#hub-controls-hub1)): in a menu, the window drawn on top under the pointer, else, with the pointer over no panel, the topmost window holding the input, else the workspace's history. Outside menus they act only while a window with tabs holds the input: the native input mask applied is full (a `WhileShown` window, or the pointer over a hovered one), so the game's actions are blocked (`GameInputBlocker` applies `BlockAllKeys`). They are **window keys** (`KeybindScope.Window`), which clash as menu keys do. The game's Auto Run and Interrupt Ability Cast on the same buttons are therefore not conflicts: no row warning and no key-conflict dialog. A mod's key on the same chord, in any scope, is not a conflict either: the mod's key keeps it, its mod gets no dialog, and at each press the window key stands aside whenever that key acts on the press ([where keys meet](#who-gets-a-key-first-installed)). The window key's row in the Keybinds tab says so in a quiet note (`<mod>'s <action> also uses <key>; whenever it can act, it does and this key doesn't.`). One press fires one of them, and steps either a window or the workspace, never both.
- **The separate window mouse keys are gone** (HUB2, requested 2026-10-16: "there are already keybinds for forward / backwards in the menus"). PR #42 added **Window tab back (mouse)** and **Window tab forward (mouse)** (`WindowTabBackMouse` / `WindowTabForwardMouse`) on the same buttons as Navigate back and forward, in a scope that met the game's gameplay actions, which raised a key-conflict dialog at every start. They were removed; Navigate back and forward now do their job, so one row and one remap apply everywhere. At registration Core drops both ids from `progenitor.ui.cfg` (`ModsMenu.DropRetiredNavigationKeys`: the live row and BepInEx's orphaned row, then one save, logged as `Ui: dropped N retired navigation key row(s)`). A player who chose **Leave unbound** or **Later** for them in the old dialog therefore has nothing left unbound: the action is Navigate back and forward, which that dialog never touched. A key a player had remapped those two rows to is dropped with them; Navigate back and forward keep their own values (pick a key there to move them).
- **Does the game stop Auto Run while its menu is open?** Source-checked (native `FullscreenMenuMapper.GetConsumedInputs`): the menu's input context blocks `ButtonInputActionCollection.GameplayAbilities` (39 actions) and `GameplayMenuActions` (13), the camera actions on keyboard and mouse, the camera zoom axis and five single actions (SpellBookMenu 24, VampirePowersMenu 26, EmotesMenu 25, VBloodMenu 18 and Respawn 8). Which actions the two collections hold is in the game's static array data (`<PrivateImplementationDetails>` blobs), not decoded, so the source alone did not say whether Auto Run (`AutoMove`, the mouse forward button) and Interrupt (the back button) are among them. **Live 2026-09-30 (user, Steam client, in a world):** with a game menu tab open (not the Mods page), pressing the mouse forward button navigated and the character did **not** start Auto Run, so the game's menu context already blocks it. No game-input block was added, and none is needed. Interrupt on the back button was not separately observed.
- **Bridge.** `mods_ui nav_state` lists game steps as `{tab: game, game, inner}` and adds `menuOpen` (a covered menu is open) and `pending`; `mods_ui menu_tab_state` returns `{probe, menuOpen, tab, innerTabsEnabled, inner {current, count} | null, available {<tab>: shown}, step, workspaceOpen, menuContextOpen, place}` without a screenshot.

**Inner tabs** (KEYS7 layer 2, **on by default** since the live check 2026-09-30, when Spellbook schools were recorded and restored with the mouse; `[Ui] MenuHistoryInnerTabs` in `progenitor.ui.cfg`). With the switch on, a game step also keeps the page's inner tab where the page has an adapter, and a back or forward selects it after the top tab. Live 2026-09-30 the first adapter, the full-screen menu mapper's shared inner-bar fields (`FullscreenMenuMapper._SelectedInnerTab` / `_LastInnerTabCount`), read 0 while the player clicked Spellbook schools: those fields follow only the gamepad's inner-tab navigation, so that adapter was dropped. Pages:

| Page | Adapter | State and select |
|---|---|---|
| Spellbook | **SpellbookSchools** | `SpellbookMenuMapperSystem._SelectedTabIndex` (int, 0x70), set by the page's handler `SpellbookMenuMapperSystem.OnTabClicked(int)`, which `Initialize` stores as the view's `FullscreenMenuView.OnTabClicked` (`Action<int>`, 0x30), the handler a school tab click runs; a select invokes that delegate on the current view, and `RefreshTabs` redraws the highlight from the index. The count is not read (a list over a game struct). **Not live-checked.** |
| V Blood | none | `VBloodTrackingMenuMapperSystem._SelectedTabIndex` (0xFC) exists, but no tab handler is wired in `Initialize` (its five lambdas are the page's buttons); not traced further. |
| Vampire Powers, Emotes | none | `PowerSelectionMenu` pages: no tab index or tab handler. |
| Map, Social (clan), System | none | `MapMenu`, `ClanMenu`, `EscapeMenuView`: no tab index field. |
| Options (General / Controls / Graphics / Sound) | none | a `ViewManager` view outside the full-screen menu; its tab select needs a generic `GoToView<OptionsMenu.InitData>`. |

An inner tab that is not known (-1) is the same place as any inner tab of that top tab, so turning the switch on mid-session adds no duplicate. `mods_ui menu_tab_state.pages` gives each top tab's adapter name, or `no adapter`, with its current index and count while that page is shown.

The code is `GameMenuHistory` (the game-free model and the `IGameMenuProbe` / `IMenuInnerTabs` seams), `FullscreenMenuProbe` and `SpellbookSchoolTabs` (client) and `ModsMenu.Navigation.cs`.

## Hot reload

Owned widgets and page-builder callbacks are released on every unload reason. If the selected mod is the one unloading, the workspace closes; `SettingsPanel.CloseForMod` also closes a pane that still holds that plugin’s rows. Reopen is explicit. A later generation must register again from `OnLoad`.

Live swap counts and limits: [Verified and open](#verified-and-open).

A `Both` install can land differently on its two halves (job HOT4). Each half is admitted for its own process, so a review that covers this client while the local server has none installs the client half now and writes the server's copy for its next start. That row is `installed` with `Remove` and `restart false` — the client half is live — and the drawer adds a second line, `Local server: installed, loads at its next start`, so the half that still waits is not hidden behind the client's "No restart needed". Remove takes both halves back in one click: the reload copy here (the reloader unloads it within a second) and the server's pending file with its ledger row. The readback names each half: `listing.halves {client, server}` is `loaded` | `pending` | `none` per process, `listing.ledger {client, server}` says which roots' install ledgers name the listing, `listing.files [{path, kind, root}]` the files they name that are on disk, and `plan.halves [{target, decision, executorAvailable, reason}]` the decision each process got.

## Author rules

- Use `ctx.Menu`. Never copy `NativeModsEntry`, never attach a Mods tab or main-menu button yourself, never inject plugin `MonoBehaviour`s into native UI.
- Never wire collectible native callbacks (`UnityAction`, `EventTrigger`, Harmony on a plugin method). Widget clicks go through the supplied panel; Bootstrap owns the trampolines.
- Do not parent under `UICanvasBase` or retain native `OuterTabEntry` marker components.
- Failed builders must not leak widgets: the host already disposes the partial page; do not catch-and-keep handles.

## Verified and open

**Source-checked** against current `ModsMenu`, `NativeModsEntry`, `NativeTabSelection`, `PluginMenu`, `ModMenuInventory`, `SettingsPanel`, `SettingsCatalog` and `ServerSettings`. **Compile-verified:** `dotnet build samples/Progenitor.Sample/Progenitor.Sample.csproj -c Debug` from the repository root (the snippet above is that file). Consent close-on-empty (removed 2026-10-05 with the consent prompt) was **offline-checked** at one checkpoint (choice-click Close plus `CloseWhenNothingUndecided`); internal review R86 certified the choice-click path; the catalog-wipe path was R86 finding 3 and is not independently certified here. Overview / refresh resetting list scroll to the top (`position` 1) is **offline-checked** (button click after a mid-list 0.4; selection still preserves 0.778 through the layout settle pass, not only at restore; a fresh open still starts at 1). **Nothing from this batch is in a candidate or live-accepted.** Pointer-dependent scrolling, intended tab selection and physical input remain unrun. One unexplained close/reopen (`open caller=Install`) remains open.

**Sidebar rebuild cost.** A per-row full-scene `FindObjectsOfType(TextMeshProUGUI, includeInactive: true)` is a main-thread stall risk. `FindFont` caches the TMP font asset until the world epoch changes, the asset is destroyed, or Core unloads — never a scene `TextMeshProUGUI`. Plugin or fixture `Dispose` queues one directory rebuild rather than rebuilding per row. **Source-checked, offline-tested** (200-row rebuild stays at one scene sweep; 200 `Dispose` calls queue one rebuild). **Not live-retested.** This is the named site of three automatic 2026-09-10 dumps plus the on-demand 200-fixture reproduction. It does **not** address the `ntdll!ZwWaitForMultipleObjects` dump, and it is **not** established as the cause of the historical 29.9-second MU21 stall.

**Candidate-1 live defects (2026-09-10), later offline passes — not live-retested.** Six workspace defects measured on candidate 1. After the first offline pass, a Core swap re-measured: `visibleRows` and layout pinning worked; typing and selected-row color were still broken; selecting a row jumped the list to the **bottom** (was top). A later live pass with a **real mouse** showed the wheel was never dead: `listScroll.position` moved (0.484 → 0.579 → 0.389), then a follow-up measured `deltaY=120` on 13/13 samples with `applied=none` on 12/13 and one `applied=ugui` 2-row step. uGUI's `OnScroll` is starved; `Mouse.current.scroll` is not. The shown-input pump is now the mover (`path=pump`): one 2-row step per new notch, `ScrollRect.scrollSensitivity = 0` so uGUI cannot also write. Next live grep: `Ui: shown-input `. Pass: `notch=1 applied=pump path=pump` with `pos=a->b` a 2-row step. Fail: `notch=1 applied=none` (dropped) or `notch=1 applied=both` (double). `deltaY=120 notch=0 applied=none` is a held last reading, not a drop. OS-injected `SendInput` wheel must not be used to judge the wheel. (2) pin + `RectMask2D` landed; `mods_ui rects` now marks rows outside the list viewport `visible: false`. (3) typing uses the shown-input pump. (4) restore writes content `anchoredPosition` from the captured 0–1 value against this pass's content height, not `verticalNormalizedPosition` against empty bounds, and the pending preserve stays armed through `settle=armed` / a later `settle=final` re-layout so the settle pass can re-apply after Unity zeros the offset. Next live grep: `Mods workspace: scroll-preserve `. Pass: a line with `settle=settled` (or `settle=final` if the canvas was already sized) whose `restore=` matches `capture=` (mid-list, not `0`). Fail: `capture=0.778 restore=0.778` followed by `listScroll.position` 0, or a `settle=settled` line with `restore=0`. (5) gold-brown fill is the selected marker; the `›` glyph is gone. Overview / refresh and Close Mods sit in a footer band separated from the list by `FooterGap`, with inset rimmed buttons, not catalog-row chrome. (6) `visibleRows` already the rendered subset. Caret, typed characters, per-notch live row count and the preserved scroll value cannot be proven offline.

Live methods: **handler-invocation** via Dev `mods_ui` (owned `Button.onClick`, `OuterTabEntry.OnPointerClick` plus the grafted `EventTrigger`, `ModsMenu.Toggle`); **os-injected** via `observe.ps1` SendInput (`click` / `key` / `type`). Physical human input is untested. Gamepad is out of scope. PrintWindow captures were viewed by the reviewing agent. Evidence (internal research notes): the 2026-09-09 mods UI usability sets (matrix-set6, matrix-set7, os-injected-input-3, modal-and-tabs-2, final-readback), the 2026-09-09 near-stall review and the 2026-09-10 perf-and-ui defect list.

## Build

From the repository root:

```powershell
dotnet build samples/Progenitor.Sample/Progenitor.Sample.csproj -c Debug
```

Offline lifecycle tests live under `ModsMenuTests`, `ModMenuInventoryTests`, `MenuPanelLayoutTests`, `ServerSettingsTests` and `UiSettingsTests`. A passing offline run is not a live result. Current live qualification and its limits: [Verified and open](#verified-and-open).

Row backgrounds apply their state tint without a fade to avoid white bands on page creation. This follow-up is build-checked; live entry-flash acceptance remains pending.


## Mod-page actions and listing links

A mod's Details includes **View in Install Mods** when the listing these files came from is known (`page-install-listing`). It follows the same tie rule as the page's listing facts: the ledger row's package, or a package manifest beside the DLL. A listing found only by the plugin GUID or by a name, which may be another package, is not offered. The button opens the Install Mods tab, expands that listing and puts its header at the top of the list once layout is complete, including rows created later; ordinary drawer clicks keep their existing scroll anchor (`InstallModsPane.RevealListing`). A listing the current filters hide is not revealed, and the request is dropped so a later build does not jump the list to it.

**Configure in Mods** from a listing returns to the installed plugin's page even when the package name differs from the plugin id. For a package that installs several plugins, it opens the package's own plugin (the one the listing's manifest id or plugin identity names) when that plugin is installed; otherwise it opens the first plugin tied to the package in Mods-list order (`ModsMenu.InstalledPluginOfPackage`).

While client changes wait for a restart, the mod page shows **Pending changes** with the same restart button as the pending-restart bar (`page-restart`). The pending changes include ledger removals and an ordinary plugin marked for removal at the next start (the plain uninstall of a loose DLL or a folder another tool installed).

A client-only uninstall that takes effect at the next start also offers **Yes, uninstall and restart** (dialog button `yes-restart`), on the mod page's prompt and on the plain uninstall dialog; **Yes** and the cancel choice are unchanged. The combined action marks the removal, then asks for the restart through the shared restart button:

- The restart confirmation opens. When the game cannot relaunch itself here, it uses the Manual wording with **Quit to desktop**. A second press while a restart is starting does nothing.
- It asks only when the pending offer covers this mod. Otherwise nothing restarts and the page's note says to restart the game.
- While a join is in progress (Join now's reconnect, or the client connecting before the server has answered), neither this action nor the bar's button starts a restart. The page's note says to restart once the join has finished.

Status: built and game-free tested (`ModPageTests`, `ModStage3Tests`, `InstallSidesPaneTests`). On the 2026-10-15 build before the port to current main, the dialog, pending removal and its cancellation, the restart confirmation and the listing round trip (HuntsAndRifts, 1920x1080) were checked live; **Yes, uninstall and restart** was not run end to end. The port and the 2026-10-18 review fixes (removals in the pending offer, the shared confirmation, the mid-join guard, the listing tie rule) have not been run live.
